# Changelog

## Unreleased

### Follow-up: the SERVICE chip at phone width — the OBSERVA bar fits a phone in every mode
- **The OBSERVA bar is two rows at phone width** (**a declared default change** to the bar's layout at 720 px and below; deferred in 6b-ii, #153, whose `app.css` comment said the page still scrolled sideways in a pack's views). In a pack's views — Discover, Diagnose, Remediate and every Advanced view, the modes that show the SERVICE chip — the bar was 472–507 px wide at 320, 360 and 390 px (an operator, a viewer, an owner acting in an org), the action cluster past the page's edge; and in every mode with tabs the three tabs were squeezed to 28 px each, drawn on top of one another. At 720 px and below (`studio/ux.css`, its phone block) the bar wraps in the markup's order, so the Tab key reads it as the eye does (WCAG 1.3.2, 2.4.3; `order` places only the break, never one control before another): the brand, the ORG chip and the SERVICE chip on the first row; a zero-height break; then the tabs, which share the second, and the action cluster at its end (the home has no tabs — it names no journey — and the action cluster ends its one row). The chrome's side padding is 16 px there and its gaps 8 px. The SERVICE chip's name takes the ORG chip's 8ch phone cap (`studio/app.css`): the button's `aria-label` and `title` name the service in full, a label (a service no record covers) now carries `title="service: <name>"` (`studio/app.mjs` `updateObservaServiceChip`), and the context bar's SERVICE select shows it. Each tab is a size container: under 100 px of content it shows its number alone, centred — its `title` and `aria-label` name the step — and otherwise its number and its question, 13 px, in up to two lines. At 320, 360, 390 and 720 px, in both themes and every mode, the bar scrolls nothing sideways and every control in it — the brand, both chips, each tab, Advanced, the account menu — sits inside the viewport, none on top of another, each tab 40 px wide or more, each right of the one before it in the markup on its row or on a row below; in headless Chromium the chrome is 101–108 px tall in a journey (it was 109) and 59 px on the home. Above 720 px nothing moves. A pack's view draws its own content, which the bar no longer hides: at a phone width some views' tables and fact lists (Diagnose, OTLP Coverage, References, Schema, Traceability, Neuron; Discover and Conformance at 320 px) still scroll the page sideways by themselves.
- **Tests**: `server/test-settings-studio.mjs`'s long one-org label subtest sweeps every mode at 320, 360, 390 and 720 px in both themes — the home, a service page, Discover (the SERVICE chip capped, the button naming the service in full), the Diagnose and Remediate tabs, the seven Advanced views, Discover with the chip a label (the services table unread, the home's derived tile: a `<span>` whose `title` is `service: payment-service`, its name capped), Settings, Build as oscar, and Discover as olive (an owner in three orgs: the ORG switcher beside the SERVICE chip): the bar's own scroll width is the viewport's, every control inside it and none on top of another, each tab 40 px wide or more, the controls in the markup's order (the Tab key's: none takes a positive `tabindex`) each right of the one before on its row or on a row below, and no horizontal page scroll on the home, a service page, Settings and Build. Against the previous stylesheets it fails at the first screen with tabs (a service page at 320 px: the tabs 28 px wide, on top of each other and of the action cluster); in a pack's views the previous bar measured 472–507 px. The subtest is extended, not added: the suite total stays at 1392 tests. No vendored module changed.

### Follow-up `auth-off-way-in-studio`: without sign-in, the studio names the server's own way in
- **The MCP panel's Server settings button and the empty MCP picker's lines say the server's sentence** (**a declared default change** to both texts; deferred by name in 6b-ii, #153). In the token posture the button said `Configuring the MCP server needs a signed-in admin — this server has no sign-in: add the first user with npm run users -- add <login>, or configure OIDC.` and the picker hint `registering one needs a signed-in admin (npm run users -- add <login>)` — a way in `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off` defeats (adding a user arms nothing there, and it beats OIDC). Both now take `GET /api/mcp-endpoints`' `policy.register.why`, the server's sentence for this reader, whose way in is `server/authz.mjs` `noSignInWay` (under `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off`: restart without it, once a user exists or with OIDC configured), so the studio keeps no copy of the rule. `studio/mcp-settings-model.mjs` `settingsGateModel` reads the token posture as it reads the open one — `Configuring the MCP server is endpoint configuration: <the server's sentence>.`, `Checking whether you may configure the MCP server…` while unread, `Could not check whether you may configure the MCP server — close and reopen the panel to try again.` after a failed read, neither naming a way in — and its `NO_SIGN_IN` constant is gone. `studio/settings-model.mjs` `mcpTargetModel` (new input `registerWhy`) and `mcpTargetMissingText` (new inputs `posture`, `registerWhy`) say `No MCP endpoint is registered in <org> yet — <the server's sentence>.` wherever there is no sign-in: the token posture, and the open postures too, where `an admin registers them in Settings → MCP endpoints` was untrue bound off the loopback (closed while exposed) and to a request not sent straight to it (only from this machine); when the server named none, no way in is named (`registering one needs a signed-in admin`, `registering one is not open to you here`). Signed in, both read as before. In the token posture the panel's opening now reads `GET /api/mcp-endpoints` (`studio/app.mjs` `readMcpGatePolicy`; again at the next opening after a failed read) for the button alone: the pickers there still draw no list and keep the typed URL, and the home reads nothing; the identity and open postures read as before, and the static bundle reads nothing new. One server sentence changed (**a declared default change**), where it was itself short of true: open-exposed under `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off`, `server/mcp-target-policy.mjs` (`typedMcpUrlDecision`, so a typed MCP URL's 403 and `policy.typed.why`; `policy.register.why`) and `server/service-admin.mjs` (a server-run journey's typed Pack B URL) named `add the first user with npm run users -- add <login> (it arms sign-in; the first user is an owner)` beside `or bind the server to loopback` — adding a user arms nothing there, and with OIDC configured the first local user is no owner. They now name `restart it without OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off, once a user exists (npm run users -- add <login>) or with OIDC configured, and sign in as an admin; or bind the server to loopback` (`exposedWay`, the restart worded as `server/authz.mjs`' closed-posture refusal and `noSignInWay` word it), and the studio says it unchanged; without `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off` the sentence is as before.
- **Tests**: `server/test-mcp-target-policy.mjs` (one more: the three texts over the policy the server answers in each posture, through `mcpCallerOf` over each posture's env as the route reads it — the token posture with and without `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off` and with OIDC configured under it, open on the loopback asked straight and through a proxy, open-exposed without `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off` and with it, OIDC configured or not (the restart, never `add the first user`), identity by role, the static bundle; and the typed URL's, the list's and a journey's open-exposed sentence under `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off`), `server/test-authz.mjs` (the same, and `GET /api/mcp-endpoints`' policy on the open-exposed server under `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off`), `tools/test-mcp-settings-model.mjs` (one more: the token gate as served, unread, failed and silent, and no way in kept in the module), `tools/test-settings-model.mjs` (the hint and the line per posture), `server/test-settings-studio.mjs` (the token posture subtest opens the MCP panel on three servers — the token, `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off`, and that with OIDC configured, a new child: the button's reason is the server's sentence as served, read once, a failed read naming none and the next opening reading again; the open-exposed subtest, under `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off`, pins the served sentence to the restart and the hint, the button and the nothing-to-send line to it), `tools/test-studio-bundle.mjs` (the bundle's button names no sign-in way). Suite total 1390 → 1392 tests. No vendored module changed.

### Follow-up `malformed-json-app-wide`: a body the parsers refuse is answered as JSON, quoting none of it
- **A malformed JSON body is answered `400 { ok: false, error: 'the request body is not valid JSON' }` on every route** (**a declared default change**: it was Express's HTML error page, which quoted a fragment of the body — a `POST /api/mcp/ping` `mcpAuth`, a password — and printed the error's stack, fragment included, on stderr). `server/index.mjs`'s `payloadTooLarge` error handler becomes `bodyParserError` (`server/route-table.mjs` `MIDDLEWARE` follows): every error the app-wide parsers raise is answered on every path as JSON with a fixed text and the parser's own status — 400 not JSON, 413 too large (`/api/*` keeps its crawler text; elsewhere `the request body is too large`, which was the HTML page), 415 an unsupported charset or content encoding (was the HTML page) — with `Cache-Control: no-store`, no fragment of the body, and nothing logged. The parsers still run after the auth gate and before every route's `authorize()`: an anonymous caller, or a session without the CSRF header, is refused as before; a caller `authorize()` would refuse gets the 400 first, as it got the page. `POST /api/mcp-settings/describe` and `/submit` keep their own parser and answers (batch 4, M6 (b)); the static bundle reads no body and is unchanged. `server/test-malformed-json.mjs` (new, 2): a marker in a malformed body to every mutating route, in the token and identity postures — JSON, never the marker, the refusal unchanged — plus oversize and charset, and the marker in neither child's log; `server/test-mcp-settings.mjs`'s check of another route's answer flips from the page to the JSON 400. Suite total 1357 → 1359 tests.

### Rebadge batch 4, D1–D4: in-page MCP server settings
- **`tools/lib/artefact-classify.mjs`** gains `compileBoundedPattern(pattern, { flags, noun, timed })` → `{ re }` or `{ reason }`: the taxonomy's pattern rule (a non-empty string of at most `PATTERN_MAX_LENGTH` characters, anchored with `^`, flags `""` or `"i"`, no quantified group, the four adversarial ids within the 50 ms budget) as one export, so another file carrying a user-written regex applies the same rule. `noun` names the pattern in each reason (default `pattern`); `timed: false` skips only the wall-clock run, every static check still runs. The taxonomy compiles through it and every one of its texts is unchanged. `tools/test-artefact-classify.mjs` +3. Suite total 1250 → 1253 tests at this entry's commit.
- **`tools/lib/mcp-url-safety.mjs`** gains `isLoopbackOrigin(origin)` — moved unchanged from `server/mcp-target-policy.mjs`, which re-exports the same function, so the server's allowlist and loopback rules do not move — and `mayBeThisMachine(hostname)`: every host `isLoopbackOrigin` accepts plus `*.localhost`, the unspecified addresses `0.0.0.0`, `[::]` and `[::ffff:0:0]`, and one trailing dot. The wider reading is for a check that refuses more when a host may be the reader's own machine (the browser's same-machine rule for server settings), never for an exemption: the server keeps the strict `isLoopbackOrigin`. `server/test-mcp-url.mjs` +2 cases.
- **`tools/lib/mcp-server-settings.mjs`** (new; listed — `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json` 56 → 57 modules; pure, browser-safe, imports `artefact-classify.mjs` and `mcp-url-safety.mjs` only): the MCP server-settings contract, one module for the studio, the studio server and an MCP server author who vendors it to check their own description. **The descriptor, version 1** (`SETTINGS_DESCRIPTOR_VERSION`), served at `<MCP server root>/admin/schema` (`SETTINGS_DESCRIPTOR_PATH`): `{ version, endpoint, auth?: { field, scheme: 'bearer' }, fields: [{ name, label, type, required?, help?, placeholder? }] (1–24), actions?: [{ name, label, endpoint?, fields?, confirm? }] (0–4) }` within `SETTINGS_LIMITS` (16 KiB, label 80, help 240, placeholder 120, confirm 160, …); `FIELD_TYPES` `text`, `url`, `secret`, `boolean`, any other type read as text; unknown keys ignored and a `value` key never read. `parseSettingsDescriptor(text, { mcpUrl, contentType })` → `{ descriptor }` (normalised, frozen), `{ notDescriptor }` (a JSON-RPC answer, an object without `version` and `fields`, a type that is not JSON — the generic form's cue) or `{ reason }` naming the broken rule (`this server describes its settings in version 2; this studio reads version 1 — update the studio, or use the generic form`, `fields[2].name "__proto__" is reserved`, `duplicate field name "user"`, a control character, `auth.field` not a secret, an action naming an unknown field). **The root and the path rule**: `settingsRoot(mcpUrl)` drops the last path segment (one trailing slash ignored), the query, the fragment and any userinfo (`…:9000/mcp` and `…:9000/mcp/` → `…:9000/`, `https://gw/team-a/mcp` → `https://gw/team-a/`); `resolveSettingsPath(path, mcpUrl, { noun, fix })` → `{ url }` or `{ reason }` takes only `^/?[A-Za-z0-9._~-]+(/[A-Za-z0-9._~-]+)*$` within 128 characters with no segment `.` or starting `..` — no `%` escape, `;`, `:`, `@`, `\`, `?`, `#`, white space or empty segment —, a leading `/` relative to the root, then re-checks the result (same origin, under the root, no userinfo, search or hash, `mcpUrlPolicy` clean). `normaliseUrlValue(value)` → `{ href }` (http(s), scheme and host lower-cased) or `{ reason }`, refusing userinfo (`the backend URL carries a user or password before "@" — put them in their own fields, so they are treated as secrets`). **The generic form**: `genericDescriptor({ names, path, auth })` — `GENERIC_NAMES` `url`, `user`, `secret`, `apiKey` (any subset renamed), `/configure`, every field in the body unless `auth: 'bearer'`. **The request**: `settingsRequest(descriptor, values, { action })` → `{ path, body, payload, headers, sent, carries, secretValues, secretNames, user }` or `{ reason, missing? }` — a null-prototype body, a url normalised, a secret exactly as typed, an empty optional omitted, a missing required field, a bad url or a body over 64 KiB refused; the `auth` field as `Authorization: Bearer`; an action sends `{ action }` plus its declared `fields`, else the `auth` field, else every non-empty secret, and lists the candidates in `carries`. **The outcome**: `outcomeOf({ status, contentType, text, type, truncated }, { secretValues, secretNames, user })` → `{ tone, headline, success, ok, message, checks, shape, raw, json, redacted, capped }` — `The server reports the settings verified (HTTP <s>).` only on `ok: true`, `accepted … It reported no verification.`, `answered HTTP <s> but reports a failure.`, `refused the settings: HTTP <s>.`, the redirect line; check statuses as words (`pass`, `fail`, `skip`, else `unknown`); message ≤ 500, 24 checks, the body verbatim after redaction, 8 KiB shown of 64 KiB read. **Redaction**: `redactEchoes(text, secretValues, { user })` hides every submitted secret of 4+ characters raw, URI-encoded, JSON-escaped (with `\uXXXX` and `\/` variants), base64 and as base64 `<user>:<secret>`, longest first; `outcomeOf` runs it on the raw text, then on every parsed string and key, then `redactSecretKeys(json, secretNames)` — a submitted secret field's name or `/^(pass(word)?|secret|token|api[-_]?key|credential|authorization)$/i` becomes `"<redacted>"` whatever its length. **The settings policy**: `compileSettingsPolicy(json, { timed })` → `{ policy, errors }`, strict (an unknown key anywhere, a version other than 1, 0 or more than 32 rules, a rule on a secret); a pattern passes `compileBoundedPattern` and then the settings bound — at most one unbounded quantifier (`^https://.*.*.*\.internal$` is refused); these are checks of shape, and no load-time probe times a pattern against URL-shaped values (a probe cannot prove a pattern fast on every value — see the review fixes), so a caller that must not block runs `policyFindings` under a deadline; `timed: false` skips only `compileBoundedPattern`'s id clocks; the optional `generic` block (`path`, `names`, `auth`) is validated as the generic form. `policyFindings(policy, descriptor, values)` → `[{ rule, field, warn, ack, unevaluated, note }]`: a non-empty value only, a url as its normalised href, a value over 512 characters itself a finding, a rule whose field the form lacks (or holds as a secret) unevaluated with its ack still required. Nothing calls it yet: inert. `tools/test-mcp-server-settings.mjs` (46 tests at this entry's commit, 48 at the head of the branch — the review fixes' two timing-run cases, counted in their own entry; new, in `npm test`: the descriptor, the root table, the path table against a loopback and a path-prefixed https root, the request, the outcome and every redaction form, the policy and its findings) and `tools/test-vendor-manifest.mjs` (`EXPECTED_MODULES` +1). Suite total 1253 → 1299 tests at this entry's commit.
- **`OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_SETTINGS_POLICY=<path.json>`** (new; `TOMOGRAPH_MCP_SETTINGS_POLICY` honoured, the modern name in every message) — `server/mcp-settings-policy.mjs` (new, the taxonomy loader's twin) reads the settings-policy file once in `start()`, right after the taxonomy and before the store: one leading byte-order mark is stripped (Windows PowerShell 5 writes one), the JSON is parsed and compiled by `compileSettingsPolicy` (strict, every pattern's shape checked), and an unreadable or invalid file **refuses the start** with `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_SETTINGS_POLICY: <path>: <the ENOENT text | invalid JSON: … | the first schema error (+N more)>`, nothing written. A loaded file is logged once, `[studio] MCP settings policy: <path> (<n> rules)`, never on a silent boot. **`GET /api/mcp-settings`** (new, `viewer`, `no-store`, deployment-global; `server/routes/mcp-settings.mjs`) answers `{ ok, proxy, policy, configured }` — the document as the file holds it or `null`, never its path, and `proxy: true` only while `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_ADMIN_PROXY=1` (read per request; the pass-through itself lands later in this batch). Nothing calls it at boot; the static bundle's shim answers `{ ok: true, proxy: false, policy: null, configured: false }`. Unset, nothing is read and no answer changes. `server/fixtures/serve-child.mjs` STRIP gains `MCP_SETTINGS_POLICY` and `MCP_ADMIN_PROXY`; `.env.example` and the README API Surface gain their rows; `server/route-table.mjs`, `server/test-authz.mjs` (`EXPECTED_CLASS`, `PROBES`, a case probe) and `server/test-tenancy.mjs` (deployment-global) the route; `tools/test-studio-bundle.mjs` (the shim's answer equals an unconfigured server's). `server/test-mcp-settings.mjs` (new, in `npm test`, 8 tests: the reader against plain env objects — unset, both spellings, the modern name winning —, each refusal's exact text, a BOM-led file, the unconfigured and configured answers, the one log line, the refused starts before the store, the switch's values, and the STRIP proof); `tools/fixtures/mcp-settings/policy.json` is the SPEC's example. Suite total 1299 → 1307 tests at this entry's commit.
- **`tools/lib/mcp-url-safety.mjs`** gains `parseOriginList(value)` → `{ any, origins: Set, rejected: [entry] }` — moved unchanged from `server/mcp-target-policy.mjs`, which re-exports the same function, so the server's `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_ORIGINS` rule does not move; the static-bundle builder reads `--mcp-origins` with it (below). `server/test-mcp-url.mjs` pins the re-export.
- **`tools/build-studio-bundle.mjs`** — `--mcp-settings-policy <file.json>` and `--mcp-origins <origin>,…` (each at most once; twice or without a value exits 2 with the usage). The policy file is read with one leading BOM stripped, parsed and compiled with `compileSettingsPolicy` as `server/mcp-settings-policy.mjs` does, the refusals the server's texts behind the origin (`--mcp-settings-policy: <abs>: ENOENT …`, `…: invalid JSON: …`, `…: <first error> (+N more)`), and the document is appended to the config as `mcpSettingsPolicy`. The origin list is read by the server's rule (`parseOriginList`; `*` any origin), normalised and sorted, and appended as `mcpOrigins: { listed: true, origins: [origin] | null }` (null for `*`) — the shape `GET /api/mcp-endpoints` gives a server's studio; an entry that is not an http(s) origin, or a list with none, **fails the build** naming it (a server ignores such an entry; a bundle is distributed, so a silently shortened list would show only as a refusal in a reader's browser). Both keys come after `taxonomy`, only when baked — an unbaked config is byte-identical. When a flag is absent `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_SETTINGS_POLICY` and `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_ORIGINS` are honoured (legacy `TOMOGRAPH_` too; the per-org lists have no meaning in a bundle). The summary line gains ` · MCP settings policy: N rules` / ` · MCP origins: <origins | any (*)>` only when baked; `--json` gains `mcpSettingsPolicy: { source, file, rules } | null` and `mcpOrigins: { source, origins } | null`, always present — never the policy's contents. New exports `resolveSettingsPolicySource`, `loadSettingsPolicyFile`, `resolveMcpOrigins`, `checkBakedOrigins`; `buildStudioBundle({ …, mcpSettingsPolicy, mcpOrigins })` guards and returns them. **The shim** (`studio/static-backend.mjs`) serves the baked policy at `GET /api/mcp-settings` (`{ ok, proxy: false, policy, configured }`, the server's answer for the same file) and adds `mcpOrigins` only when baked. README ("Serve The Studio Without The Server") and DOWNSTREAM §9 (W6 row) and §10 document both flags. `tools/test-studio-bundle.mjs`: T3 (the `--json` keys, twice, no value), T3b (the four seams), T4b (the unbaked identity with the new keys null, the shim's unconfigured answer, a parent env with `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_SETTINGS_POLICY` and `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_ORIGINS` set bakes nothing, `main`'s env bakes both) and T8d (new: the shim equals a server started with the file; the list rule, `*`, the env fallback and the flag winning; the refusals; the config key order; the summary and `--json`; no operator path or rule text in any output). Suite total 1307 → 1308 tests at this entry's commit.
- **The MCP panel's Server settings** (**a declared default change**: the panel shows a **Server settings…** button, `#mcp-settings-btn`, which asks nothing until it is clicked) — `studio/mcp-settings-model.mjs` (new, pure), `studio/mcp-settings-api.mjs` (new, the loaders) and `studio/mcp-settings-view.mjs` (new, the renderer; every node by `createElement`, every string by `textContent`), the `.mss-*` zone in `app.css` under its own `==== MCP server settings` marker. The button opens a modal in the page (a scrim, `role="dialog"` `aria-modal="true"`, Esc, the scrim and `esc` close it and only it, the Tab trap, the focus returned) for a reader who may change an MCP endpoint — `GET /api/mcp-endpoints` `policy.register.allowed`: a session admin or owner, or the open posture's caller on a direct loopback request; anyone else sees it `aria-disabled` with the reason, as do the token posture, a policy that could not be read, a panel with no target and a static bundle served off this machine with no baked MCP origin list. Opening it reads `GET /api/mcp-settings`, checks the panel's target in the browser — `mcpUrlPolicy`, never the studio's own origin, a loopback MCP only from a loopback page, https unless loopback, a loopback or listed origin (`policy.typed.origins`; in the bundle the baked `--mcp-origins`) — and reads the server's settings description from `<MCP server root>/admin/schema` **from the browser**, with no header and no credential, no redirect followed, 10 s, 16 KiB. A description is rendered as its form; a 404/405/501/401/403 or an answer that is not a description gives the generic form, named, with its path, field names and API-key placement editable under "What the server expects" (prefilled from the policy's `generic` block, never kept); a description that breaks the contract is refused by name, with Try again and Use the generic form; a page origin the server's CORS does not name reads "unreachable", naming that origin. **Send to the server** and each declared action POST from the browser straight to the MCP server (`credentials: 'omit'`, `redirect: 'manual'`, no `authHeaders()`: no cookie, no CSRF header, no `X-Observogram-Org` go to another origin), the descriptor's `auth` field as `Authorization: Bearer`; every secret input is emptied as soon as the request is sent and every input on close; inputs carry `data-field`, never `name`, in no `<form>`, secrets `autocomplete="new-password"` with the password managers' ignore attributes. The answer is shown as the server returned it — the verdict only from `ok: true`, the checks as words, the body verbatim after the echoed secrets are hidden (`n value(s) the server echoed back were hidden`); a request sent whose answer cannot be read is "unknown", never "nothing happened", with **Test the connection**. After a verified configure the panel's connection test runs (`pingFromPanel` now returns what it drew; `openMcpTarget` returns its read) and the modal says what it showed from the read's outcome (`connected, and the read <tool> answered: …` / `connected, but the read failed: …`), then offers **Open the live panel** on the same target. A settings policy that cannot be read (or compiled in the browser) holds the send: `Could not read the settings policy, so its checks cannot run — close and reopen to try again.` The proxy is not used by this modal yet, and no sentence names it. Nothing modal-related enters `state` or storage.
- **`server/test-authz.mjs`** — the studio fetch guard's exemption becomes **file-scoped** (**a declared default change** to the guard): `fetch('/auth/me')` anywhere, and `fetch(direct.href, direct.init)` in `studio/mcp-settings-api.mjs` only, pinned by a new guard test (exactly that one call, built by `directRequest` with `credentials: 'omit'`, `redirect: 'manual'` and `mode: 'cors'`, no mention of `authHeaders`); a second new guard pins that `studio/mcp-settings-view.mjs` assigns no `innerHTML`/`outerHTML` but the host's emptying and imports `host.mjs` only. `tools/test-mcp-settings-model.mjs` (new, 15) covers the gate, the target rule, the descriptor read, every status line, the inputs, the action notes, the settings policy's view, the primary's reasons and the line after the connection test; the AA scan in `tools/test-build-model.mjs` reads the `.mss-` zone as its sixth, and `tools/test-studio-layout.mjs` lists the modal and its scrim among the fixed layers stacked over the chrome and the context bar. Suite total 1308 → 1324 tests at this entry's commit.
- **`server/test-mcp-settings-studio.mjs`** (new, 10; skipped without Playwright, `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_SETTINGS_SMOKE=require` fails instead — stripped from every child in `server/fixtures/serve-child.mjs`): the Server settings journeys in headless Chromium against a child identity server and fake MCP servers with their settings surface, as ada (admin) and oscar (operator) — discovery → configure → the outcome → the connection test (nothing asked at boot or when the panel opens, every input empty at open, the secrets emptied once sent, the descriptor GET without a credential, the configure from the studio's origin with no cookie and no `X-Observogram-*`, the API key as the descriptor's Bearer); the generic form for a 404 and a JSON-RPC answer, its names and path edited, the key in the body; a 401 verbatim and an echoed secret hidden in each form, nothing left in the DOM, storage or the server's log; the disable action of the SPEC's descriptor as written and a confirm; the settings policy (below); the target refusals with no request; the gate for oscar and for no target; no CORS, a redirected description, an unreadable answer and a held one, a 307 configure reaching no one; Esc, the scrim, esc, Try again and Use the generic form leaving the panel open, Tab kept inside, the focus returned; markup in every server string shown as text. Suite total 1324 → 1334 tests at this entry's commit.

### Rebadge batch 3, C0: a caller-supplied MCP URL is a privilege
- **`tools/lib/mcp-client.mjs`** (behaviour, no export changed — **a declared default change**, D10): the client never follows a redirect. Every request is sent with `redirect: 'manual'` (a transport hook's `fetchImpl` receives it as advice, like `signal`), and an MCP answering 3xx — or a browser's `opaqueredirect` — is refused with `MCP HTTP <status> on <method>: the MCP answered with a redirect to <origin> — Observogram does not follow redirects; register (or type) the URL it points at`, naming only the Location's origin, never its path or query. This holds for every caller — the server's routes, `npm run fetch-live`, the scheduled refresh workflow and journeys: an MCP behind a redirect (http → https, a trailing slash) is configured with the final URL. `tools/test-mcp-transport.mjs` +3.
- **`tools/lib/mcp-client.mjs`** (behaviour, no export changed — **a declared default change**): every MCP answer text is redacted by value, native `fetch` included — a non-OK body, a JSON-RPC or SSE error message, a tool's `isError` text and a fetcher's rejection have the bearer, the URL's userinfo and every credential-named query parameter value replaced by `<redacted>`, as a hook's `fetchImpl` answers already were; an upstream that repeats the Authorization header (a 401 page) no longer carries the token into a 502 body, a log line or a `mcp.probeErrors.*` annotation, and the CLI's error lines are redacted too. A body that is not JSON is reported as `MCP <method>: the answer is not valid JSON` (the parser's message quoted a cut of it). The old pin (`upstream says Bearer tok`) is flipped to `upstream says Bearer <redacted>`. A successful answer is redacted the same way — every string of the result `rpc` and `callTool` return (keys included, over JSON and SSE), so an MCP that repeats the request in a result (a version string, a description) cannot carry a server-held read token into a ping's `read.detail`, a snapshot, draft or refresh pack, or a recorded fixture; a result holding none comes back as it was. From a successful result the credential itself — the bearer (a server-held read token or the caller's `mcpAuth`) and the URL's userinfo — is redacted at any length, so a 7-character read token the MCP repeats never reaches an operator's ping; a value merely taken from a credential-named query parameter is redacted from it only from 12 characters, and a tool's JSON text is parsed before it is redacted: a short credential-named value (`sortkey=title`, `partitionkey=1`) is ordinary data, and replacing it renamed keys, cut ids and left a tool's JSON unparsed in packs, fixtures and the deploy's rollback snapshot. `tools/test-mcp-transport.mjs` +4 (an echoing MCP over native fetch: a 401 body, a JSON-RPC error and an SSE error frame, a tool's `isError` text, a body that is not JSON) and +6 (a tool's JSON and plain-text answers and a `tools/list` description, over JSON and SSE), `server/test-mcp-ping.mjs` +1 (an MCP repeating the endpoint's read token in its version: `read.detail` says `version Bearer <redacted>`): 1246 → 1247 tests.
- **The MCP routes redact the resolved credential** (`server/mcp-target-policy.mjs`, new: `redactTarget(text, target)`): draft-from-mcp and refresh-live, deploy, deploy-bulk, rollback and a journey run's live Pack B run every 502 body, deploy-record item error and stderr line through the target `resolveMcpTarget` answered — the token it resolved (a server-held variable's value or the caller's `mcpAuth`), the URL's userinfo and credential parameter values, then `redactCredentials` — a backstop behind the client's own redaction. `server/test-mcp-target-policy.mjs` (new, 5), `server/test-services-api.mjs` +1 (an MCP echoing the Authorization header: the draft, refresh and deploy 502 bodies, stderr and the rows hold `<redacted>`), `server/test-transport-hook.mjs` (a hook fault naming its own credentialed upstream is masked in the refresh-live, draft-from-mcp, deploy, deploy-bulk, rollback and journey-run 502s).
- **The MCP origin allowlist** (`server/mcp-target-policy.mjs` gains `mcpOriginList`, `mcpOriginDecision`, `parseOriginList`, `isLoopbackOrigin`, `originOf`, `credentialThatRides`; **a declared default change**, D2 (c)): every MCP target — a typed `mcpUrl`, a registered endpoint at each use (draft-from-mcp, refresh-live, deploy, deploy-bulk, rollback, through `resolveMcpTarget`) and an endpoint at registration (`POST /api/mcp-endpoints`, a `PATCH` of `url` or `readTokenEnv`, judged on the record after the change) — meets an origin rule whatever the caller's role. `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_ORIGINS` (every org) ∪ `OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<KEY>_MCP_ORIGINS` (one org, read only by the org owning the name): comma-separated origins, normalised by the URL parser; an entry with a path, query, fragment, credentials or `*` in its host is named once on stderr and ignored; a list without an accepted entry allows nothing; a lone `*` allows every origin. Loopback always passes. With a list set, every other origin must be in it. **With none set, no credential leaves for an unlisted origin but loopback** — an endpoint's server-held token, the caller's `mcpAuth`, a credential in a typed URL, or a loaded transport hook (it may attach its own); a typed URL without one reaches only an origin of the org's own endpoints; a token-less endpoint registers and fetches anywhere. Refusals: 403 `{ denied: 'origin' }` at a fetch, 400 at registration (the record and its row are not written), naming the origin only. **Migration:** an install that sends a credential to a remote MCP (deploys, authenticated reads, a `readTokenEnv` endpoint, a transport hook) sets `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_ORIGINS` before upgrading. `server/fixtures/serve-child.mjs` STRIP gains `MCP_ORIGINS`. `server/test-mcp-target-policy.mjs` +5, `server/test-service-admin.mjs` +2, `server/test-services-api.mjs` +1, `server/test-tenancy.mjs` (each org's list is its own), `server/test-smoke.mjs` (mcpAuth by id to an unlisted origin → 403 before any wire call; an endpoint registered under `*` refused once the list changes).
- **A typed MCP URL needs the admin role** (`server/mcp-target-policy.mjs` gains `TYPED_MCP_URL_ROLE = 'admin'` — the one constant —, `typedMcpUrlDecision`, `mcpCallerOf`, `mcpTargetView`; **a declared default change**, R4): `POST /api/draft-from-mcp` and `POST /api/refresh-live` refuse a typed `mcpUrl` from an operator session, the bearer token (judged by rank, so flipping the constant to `operator` admits it) and the open postures' anonymous `local` caller (by kind, even on loopback): 403 `role` or `posture`, each sentence naming the way that works for its reader in its posture — a registered endpoint (`mcpEndpointId`), an admin registering one in Settings → MCP endpoints, and in the token posture arming sign-in (`npm run users -- add <login>`). A typed URL with `user:password` is a 400 (`send the token as mcpAuth`). `resolveMcpTarget(db, body, { forWrite, caller, typedRule })` takes the caller (none throws); the deploy, deploy-bulk and rollback routes passed `typedRule: 'any'` here, until the next entry. `GET /api/mcp-endpoints` gains `policy`: `{ typed, register }`, each `{ allowed, why, listed, origins }` — the reader's own org's origin list only. **Migration:** a script below admin that sends `mcpUrl` to the draft or the refresh registers the endpoint (an admin) and sends `mcpEndpointId`. - **The deploy routes take a typed MCP URL from an admin only** (**a declared default change**, D3): `POST /api/packs/:id/deploy/:target`, `POST /api/packs/:id/deploy-bulk` and `POST /api/deploys/:deployId/rollback` — which send the caller's write token to the target — follow the same rule as the draft and the refresh: a typed `mcpUrl` from an operator session, the bearer or a caller without sign-in is 403 `role` / `posture`, before anything is sent or recorded; `resolveMcpTarget`'s `typedRule` option is gone (every call site applies the rule). The 502 body is redacted with the resolved target. `loadJourneyDef` accepts `packB.mcp.endpointId` (a positive integer); the CLI is unchanged for `url` / `authEnv` and refuses a def whose Pack B names only an `endpointId` (`… is resolved by the server; run this journey through the server, or give packB.mcp.url`). **Migration:** a journey captured with a raw URL fails for non-admins until an admin re-captures it (or registers the endpoint and it is re-captured). `tools/test-journey.mjs` (the resolver, the CLI refusal, the def rule), `server/test-services-api.mjs` +1, `server/test-smoke.mjs` (capture by endpoint, a file for an unregistered URL, local's raw-URL run refused; the vantage-lost run through an endpoint).
- **The studio's MCP pickers are list-only for a reader the server refuses a typed URL** (**a declared default change**; 6b-i's D-B "typed URL kept" narrowed for operators, through the server's answer only): the refresh panel, the draft panel, the deploy modal and the home's source card read `GET /api/mcp-endpoints` with its `policy` (`studio/settings-api.mjs` `loadMcpEndpoints({ withPolicy })` → `{ endpoints, policy }`; `state.mcpTargetPolicy`, closed when the read failed). Below admin and without sign-in: no "Type a URL…" option, no URL row, a hidden field's leftover never sent (`mcpTargetOf`, `deployTargetBody`, the deploy review's target), and an empty list names the way in for that reader — `No MCP endpoint is registered in <org> yet — an admin registers them in Settings → MCP endpoints.` (the token posture: `registering one needs a signed-in admin`), the Settings button only when the server says the reader may register (`policy.register.allowed` — the open posture's button now comes from the server, not a probe). Every "type a URL" sentence follows (`studio/settings-model.mjs`: `mcpTargetModel` takes `typed`, `posture`, `unreadable`; `profileEndpointNote` / `endpointDrift` take `typedAllowed`; new `mcpTargetMissingText` for the four status lines and the rollback toast; a profile with a typed URL opened by a reader who may not type: `Profile "<p>" sends a typed MCP URL, which only an admin may send — choose one of <org>'s MCP endpoints.`). Without sign-in and with no endpoint, the home's Connect is **Register and connect** (D4): the typed URL registered as the org's endpoint, named by its host, then tested by its id in the live panel (D8) — offered only for an origin the server would accept (new `mcpRegisterCheck`: loopback, or listed; any other refused beside the button, nothing sent). The bundle and the token posture never draw the list and keep the URL row (the server decides; the token posture reads it for the Server settings button alone — `auth-off-way-in-studio`, above). `tools/test-settings-model.mjs` +5, `server/test-settings-studio.mjs` (oscar's pickers list-only; Register and connect on an open loopback server: the demo origin refused, a loopback MCP registered and tested by id, no draft posted).
- **Tests**: 1102 → 1134 for C0 as a whole — `server/test-mcp-target-policy.mjs` (new), `server/test-service-admin.mjs`, `server/test-services-api.mjs`, `server/test-authz.mjs`, `server/test-hermetic-suites.mjs`, `tools/test-mcp-transport.mjs`, `tools/test-settings-model.mjs`, `server/test-settings-studio.mjs`, and assertions within `server/test-smoke.mjs`, `tools/test-journey.mjs`, `server/test-tenancy.mjs`, `server/test-auth-oidc.mjs`, `server/test-audit-api.mjs`, `server/test-transport-hook.mjs` and `tools/test-backend-live.mjs`; the shared fake MCP and endpoint registration in `server/fixtures/fake-mcp.mjs` (new). `deploy/k8s/deployment-studio.yaml` documents `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_ORIGINS`, commented.

### Rebadge batch 3, C2: test the connection, then fetch
- **`tools/lib/contracts/mcp-capabilities.mjs`** gains `capabilityInventory(toolNames)` → `{ capabilities: { <capabilityId>: [advertised tool names] }, unmatched: <count> }`: an MCP's advertised tools mapped to the capabilities Observogram reads, in candidate order, a name advertised twice counted once, and every tool no capability calls only counted, never listed. The tool surface is unchanged. `tools/test-contract-guard.mjs` +2.
- **`pingMcp`** (`tools/fetch-live-pack.mjs`, with `PING_READS`, `PING_TIMEOUT_MS` = 5 s per request, `PING_DEADLINE_MS` = 10 s for the whole ping and `TOOLS_LIST_MAX_PAGES` = 10): `initialize` and `notifications/initialized`, the whole `tools/list` (its `nextCursor` followed up to ten pages; a cursor left over reads `more`) and **one** cheap read — the dashboards search asked for one item, else the Grafana health read (marked `credentialFree`: it answers without the MCP's backend credentials), else `system_health` — and nothing else. The verdict is read from where a failure surfaced: HTTP 401/403 on `initialize`, `tools/list` or `tools/call` → `auth-refused`; no answer or a 5xx → `unreachable`; a request's timeout or the deadline → `timeout` (the deadline aborts the request still in flight, through the client's `signal`); any other status (404), a body that is not a JSON-RPC result, a JSON-RPC error or a redirect → `not-mcp`; otherwise `connected`, with a read whose tool answered an `isError` text naming 401/403/unauthorized/forbidden kept as `backendAuthRefused` (the MCP's own credentials to its backend). A transport hook fault is thrown, never a verdict. `tools/test-mcp-ping.mjs` (17 tests at this entry's commit, 18 at the head of the branch — one more for the deadline's abort: 1248 → 1249 tests), new.
- **`POST /api/mcp/ping`** (`server/routes/live.mjs`, new: `liveRoutes`, `pingAnswer`) — the live MCP API's first route: `operator` by class, yet `direct` (without sign-in answered only to a request sent straight to a loopback address), closed when the server is exposed without sign-in, and `csrf: 'always'` (the bearer exempt) — R2. Its target is `resolveMcpTarget`'s, as a draft's: `{ mcpEndpointId }` with the endpoint's read token (a request's `mcpAuth` overrides it), or a typed `mcpUrl` from an admin. The answer is R2's and nothing else: `{ ok, verdict, origin, mcpEndpoint, reachable: { initialized }, auth: { outcome: sent \| not-sent \| refused \| null (a token was to go but nothing answered before `initialize` — unreachable, or silent — so the ping does not say it was sent), sent: endpoint-variable \| request \| none }, tools: { count, capabilities, unmatched, complete }, read: { capability, tool, outcome, detail, backendAuthRefused, credentialFree }, timings, sentence, checked, notChecked }` — tool names only through `capabilityInventory`, an HTTP failure named by its status and step (never its body), a credential-free read saying what it did not check. 200 whenever the ping ran, 400 / 403 for the target, 502 a transport hook fault. It writes no live file and no pack; an admin's typed ping writes one `live.ping` row (`{ verdict, typed: true }` on the origin), a ping by id none; stderr `[mcp-ping] <verdict> <origin> <ms>`. The route table's `direct` invariant admits the live MCP API beside admin and owner rows (`closedAs: 'the live MCP API'`; its `direct` refusal names `node tools/fetch-live-pack.mjs` as the way without the server, its CSRF refusal says `requests to the live MCP API`); the README API Surface intro states every `/api/mcp/…` route is `operator`. `server/test-mcp-ping.mjs` (9 tests at this entry's commit, 12 at the head of the branch — one more for the repeated read token, one for the token's `null` outcome when nothing answered: 1247 → 1248 tests, one for a 7-character read token repeated in a successful answer: 1249 → 1250 tests), new, `server/test-authz.mjs` (the decision cells, the texts, `EXPECTED_LIVE_MCP`, the probe and case rows), `server/test-tenancy.mjs` (another org's endpoint id → 400), `server/test-smoke.mjs` (local: a typed URL refused, a ping by id answered), `tools/test-contract-guard.mjs` (the route spells no tool name).
- **`POST /api/draft-from-mcp` and `POST /api/refresh-live` join the live MCP API's posture** (**a declared default change**, D7): like the ping, without sign-in they answer only a request sent straight to a loopback address (a foreign `Host`, a proxy header or a foreign `Origin` → 403 `posture`, naming the studio at `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` and `node tools/fetch-live-pack.mjs` as the way without the server), they are closed on a server exposed without sign-in (`OBSERVOGRAM_INSECURE_NO_AUTH=1` → 403 `posture`), and every request takes `X-Observogram-CSRF: 1` in every posture (`requests to the live MCP API need it …`; the bearer is exempt) — one posture for every server-side MCP request. **Migration:** a script that drafts or refreshes against an open server sends the header (the studio always has); an `OBSERVOGRAM_INSECURE_NO_AUTH` LAN demo drafts from the CLI or arms sign-in. `server/test-authz.mjs` (`EXPECTED_LIVE_MCP` holds the three; the open-exposed and non-direct refusals pinned per route).
- **The MCP panel's refresh button tests the connection; rebuilding production-live is its own action** (**a declared default change**): the panel is "Live MCP connection". `#mcp-refresh-btn` keeps its id and becomes **test connection** — `POST /api/mcp/ping` with the picker's target — so a habit or a script that clicks it as a connectivity check no longer rewrites production-live or writes an audit row; its status line (`#mcp-ping-status`, `role="status"`, `aria-live="polite"`) says the verdict as a word, and `#mcp-ping-result` draws the server's sentence, the timings, the read's outcome, what the MCP offers that a fetch reads, the families it does not offer (`Not offered by this MCP: …`; while tool pages remained, never called absent) and a "What this did not check" disclosure. Any change to the picker or the auth field clears the result. **rebuild production-live** (`#mcp-rebuild-btn`, new; its status `#mcp-rebuild-status`) is the explicit, separate action — `POST /api/refresh-live` as before, the note under it saying what it reads, how long it usually takes and that it writes an audit row; the dead branch that reloaded a selected `production-live` is gone. The Advanced item reads "test it, rebuild production-live". New studio modules: `studio/live-api.mjs` (`pingMcp`), `studio/live-model.mjs` (`pingResultModel`, `durationText`, `rebuildNoteText`, `capabilityLabel`), `studio/live-view.mjs` (`renderPingResult`, every MCP-sourced string by `textContent`); CSS zone `.mcpc-*` under its own AA-scanned block in `app.css`. The static bundle names the ping (`FEATURES` `['/api/mcp/ping', 'Testing an MCP connection']`): the panel's status line is the 501 sentence. `server/test-live-studio.mjs` (new, browser; skips without Playwright, `OBSERVOGRAM_LIVE_SMOKE=require` fails instead; `LIVE_SMOKE` in `server/fixtures/serve-child.mjs` STRIP), `tools/test-live-model.mjs` (new, 8), `tools/test-studio-bundle.mjs` (T3c, T6, T7), `tools/test-build-model.mjs` (the `.mcpc-` AA pass), `server/test-settings-studio.mjs` (the ping and the rebuild by id).
- **Tests**: 1134 → 1169 for C2 as a whole — `tools/test-mcp-ping.mjs` (new, 17), `server/test-mcp-ping.mjs` (new, 9), `tools/test-live-model.mjs` (new, 8), `server/test-live-studio.mjs` (new, 1), and assertions within `tools/test-contract-guard.mjs`, `server/test-authz.mjs`, `server/test-tenancy.mjs`, `server/test-smoke.mjs`, `server/test-store-guards.mjs`, `server/test-settings-studio.mjs`, `tools/test-studio-bundle.mjs` and `tools/test-build-model.mjs`.

### Rebadge batch 3, C1: a true-snapshot live pack
- **Stored diff goldens** (`tools/test-golden-diff.mjs`, new; `npm run test:golden:diff[:update]`): 27 cases written from `diffPacks` before this batch touches `tools/lib/diff.mjs` — the catalogue's self-diffs, payment-service and krystaline-repo-carlos against production-curated in every `scopeMode` and with a service override, the remediation-flow pairs and a live side that could not observe some families — each pinned by the sha256 of the whole answer beside a readable projection. Nothing in this batch regenerates them.
- **`tools/lib/live-fetch.mjs`** (new; listed — `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json` 54 → 55 modules; pure, no import): the live fetch's contract — `LIVE_STAGES` (the stage ids, a downstream contract: `connect`, `services`, `backends`, `metric_names`, `recording_rules`, `alert_rules`, `dashboards`, `scrape_targets`, `alerting_routes`, `signals` (the draft only), `build`, `register`, each naming the capability ids it reads), `LIVE_KINDS`, `STAGE_STATES`, `stagesFor(kind)`, `stageNoun`, `fetchPlan(capabilities, { kind, complete })` (`run` / `gap` / `unknown` — an incomplete or absent `tools/list` is never a claimed gap), `SCOPE_LIMITS`, `SNAPSHOT_LIMITS`, `normalizeScope(input)` (`{ scope: { metricPrefixes, folderUids, datasourceUid }, errors }`, each refusal naming the field and the rule), `scopeIsEmpty`, `SCOPE_FAMILIES`, `scopeAnnotations(scope, learned)` (`observogram.live.scope` always, `observogram.scope.<kind>` only where the scope was applied), `scopeOf(annotations)`, `folderUidOf`, `inScope(kind, artefact, entry)` (`true` / `false` / `null` when the artefact does not say) and `scopeReason(kind, verdict, entry, { holder })`. `tools/test-contract-guard.mjs` guards it (a literal may equal a capability id, never another tool name).
- **`tools/lib/crawler.mjs`** gains `dashboardSpecId(dash)` — the crawler's dashboard id rule (the uid, else the title: lower case, runs outside `[a-z0-9-]` → `-`, cut at 60, `d-` before a non-letter; `null` when neither yields an id) as one export, which the crawler's own dashboard walk now calls: crawl output byte-identical (`npm run test:golden`). It is the one rule a crawled pack and a live snapshot will share, so the same Grafana uid pairs by `definedId` whichever side read it.
- **`tools/lib/mcp-client.mjs`** (behaviour, no export changed): `createMcpClient` takes an optional `signal` — a caller's AbortSignal, combined per request with the request's own timeout (`AbortSignal.any`), so aborting it ends every request in flight (an SSE stream being read included) and refuses every later one before the hook or the wire sees it, with the signal's `AbortError`. Without it each request carries its timeout alone, as before. A live job's cancel and watchdog will use it.
- **`tools/lib/mcp-client.mjs`** gains `MAX_MCP_ANSWER_BYTES` (32 MiB) and `createMcpClient`'s `maxAnswerBytes` option (**a declared default change**): one MCP answer is read up to the cap, counted while it is read — the SSE stream, the JSON body and a non-OK body alike, through `body.getReader()` (a Response-like without one is refused on its `content-length`, else counted after `text()`) — and past it the client stops reading, releases the stream and throws `MCP <method>: the answer exceeded 32 MiB — Observogram stopped reading it` (a non-OK answer's error carries the same words in place of its body). An answer under the cap reads exactly as before.
- **`tools/lib/diff.mjs`** (now imports `live-fetch.mjs`; behaviour, no export changed — inert without the annotation): `diffPacks` honours a pack's `observogram.scope.<kind>` annotations, read per side beside `observogram.unobserved.<kind>`. An unmatched artefact on the other side, in a scoped family, goes to `notObserved` with a reason that states only what is known — `outside the snapshot's metric scope (prefixes payments_, checkout_)`, `outside the snapshot's dashboard folders (Payments)`, or, when the artefact does not say what the scope needs (a crawled dashboard names no folder uid), `the snapshot read only the folders Payments; Pack A does not say which folder this dashboard is in, so it was not checked`. One artefact of a key inside the scope keeps the group compared as usual; an unobserved family keeps its own reason. Without the annotation the answer is byte-identical: the stored diff goldens are unchanged.
- **`tools/fetch-live-pack.mjs`** — `fetchMcp({ mode, scope, onStage, signal })` and `buildSnapshotPack(fetched, { refreshedAt, origin, endpoint, packName, scope, gaps })`. `mode: 'draft'` (the default) is the fetch as it was: its canonical is byte-identical with or without the stage reporter and the signal (`npm run test:fetch` replays it; `tools/test-live-snapshot.mjs` pins it with `onStage` set). `mode: 'snapshot'` inventories what is deployed: every stage boundary reports `{ stage, state, counts, message, gap }` with the stage ids of `tools/lib/live-fetch.mjs`; `tools/list` is read whole (up to 10 pages) and one that does not answer fails `connect`; **no core abort** — without the service-health tools `services` is skipped with its gap and the fetch goes on; the stack self-metrics, the Alertmanager and Grafana observers and the ALERTS query are not read, while the recording-rule grep runs over the **unscoped** metric names (it feeds the SLI/SLO inference); the scope applies metric prefixes (then a 20 000-name cap, named), dashboard folder uids **before** the serial detail reads (dashboards without a folder dropped and counted) and rule folders only when every rule names one (otherwise the stage says the scope was not applied); a datasource uid no advertised tool takes is named in the gate log; every advertised alert-rule engine is read and the rules unioned by name (the first engine wins a clash; each rule's engine is the one its own tool attests); a dashboard's id is the obs-pack-id tag, else the crawler's `dashboardSpecId`, so the same Grafana uid pairs with a crawled repository; the caller's signal aborts requests in flight and is checked at every stage boundary and in the dashboard loop. `buildSnapshotPack` wraps `buildCanonicalPack` (unchanged for the draft): `mcp.url` is the **origin** only, and it writes `observogram.live.mode: snapshot`, `observogram.live.source`, `observogram.live.scope`, `observogram.live.gaps`, `observogram.scope.<kind>` for each family the scope narrowed, and `observogram.unobserved.<kind>` for every family of every gapped stage with the stage's reason — `alert_rule` included, which the draft does not stamp; `metadata.name` defaults to `live-snapshot`. The mock-MCP golden (`npm run test:golden:snapshot[:update]`, `tools/fixtures/golden/snapshot/`, synthetic dashboards and a crawled repository in `tools/fixtures/snapshot/`) proves the snapshot pairs with a repository pack: every dashboard (5 of 5), both alert rules, the scrape job, the declared metrics under the prefixes, a recording rule, an SLI and an SLO — `inBoth` 14 — and a repository metric outside the prefixes reads *not checked*, not *declared, not live*.
- **A snapshot reads Grafana-managed alert rules (D9).** `tools/fixtures/mcp/grafana_alert_rules.json` is a new **recording**: Grafana 12.4.4's own `GET /api/v1/provisioning/alert-rules` answer on the local Docker stack (`docker/stack.compose.yaml`'s `grafana` service; the stack's provisioned rule plus a seeded alerting rule and a Grafana-managed recording rule in a second folder; provenance and reproduction in the fixtures README — recorded from Grafana directly, because no otel-mcp-server 1.8.0 was available to the build, so the MCP envelope is not evidenced). `tools/fetch-live-pack.mjs` gains `adaptProvisionedAlertRules(response)` and the alert-rule probe's `adaptSnapshot`: in snapshot mode an answer in the provisioning shape (an array of rules carrying `title` and `data[]`, bare or under `rules` / `data`) is read `title` → name, the first query node's `model.expr` → expr (the crawler's reading), `for`, labels, annotations and `folderUID` → the folder the rule scope reads; a Grafana-managed recording rule is left out. Unioned with vmalert's rules each keeps its engine (`grafana`, `victoriametrics`), and with Grafana's engine alone a folder scope applies to alert rules and is recorded as `observogram.scope.alert_rule`. The draft's reader is unchanged (it reads nothing from that shape, pinned). **`tools/lib/contracts/response-shapes.mjs`** (behaviour, no export changed): the `rule-groups` shape also locates a bare array and accepts `title` as a rule's identity and `data` as its body; the adapted golden is `tools/fixtures/mcp/adapted/alert_rules.grafana.json`.
- **The catalogue labels a live pack scaffold or snapshot.** **`tools/lib/service-keys.mjs`** gains `livePackKind(canonical)` → `'snapshot'` (`observogram.live.mode: snapshot`), else `'scaffold'` (`mcp.refreshedAt`: every pack the fetcher drafts), else `null`; `catalogEntryOf` adds `live` (after `environments`) only for a live pack, and so do the server's file-backed catalogue entries and the static bundle's (`studio/static-backend.mjs`) — every other entry is byte-identical (**a declared default change**: `GET /api/packs` entries of drafted packs gain `live: 'scaffold'`). `isLiveAggregatePack` also holds for `live: 'snapshot'`, so a snapshot under a custom label (`Payments prod`) is never one service's pack; a draft keeps the label rule (documented, unchanged — its links do not move). **`tools/lib/pack-conformance.mjs`**: a snapshot's report gains `liveKind: 'snapshot'` beside `writers` (`writers.fetcher` is true for a draft and a snapshot alike; a fetcher-written report without `liveKind` is a scaffold); every other report is byte-identical. A provenance claim like any annotation — a hand-uploaded pack can make it, and the catalogue shows what the pack says.
- **Live MCP jobs** (`server/live-jobs.mjs`, new, in memory; four routes in `server/routes/live.mjs`, the live MCP API's posture): a snapshot or a draft runs as a job (RULINGS R1). `POST /api/mcp/jobs` `{ kind: snapshot | draft, mcpEndpointId | mcpUrl, mcpAuth?, scope?, packName?, label? }` answers **202** `{ ok, job, poll }` with a `Location` at once — the target is `resolveMcpTarget`'s, as for every fetch (a typed URL is an admin's); `GET /api/mcp/jobs/:jobId?since=<seq>` is the job's gate log by cursor (`{ ok, job, stages, next, result?, error? }`; each record `{ seq, stage, label, state, counts, startedAt, finishedAt, message, gap }` with the stage ids of `tools/lib/live-fetch.mjs`, every message redacted with the target and cut at 300 characters); `POST /api/mcp/jobs/:jobId/cancel` stops it; `GET /api/mcp/jobs` answers the configured snapshot scope, your running job in the org and how long the org's last snapshot and draft took. A job is its starter's (the org and the principal: another member's, another org's, an expired or a lost one is the same 404 `{ gone: true }`); an admin of the org may cancel any (`{ id, state }`, never the log). Bounded: one running job per org, four in the server (409s that say who started it and the way on), a finished job kept 15 minutes (8 per org), a 10-minute watchdog, 500 records; the server's `close` aborts every running job; a restart loses them (a pack registered before stays). The job runs in its org (`runWithOrg`) and, before it registers, checks its starter's authority again (`your access to <org> changed during the job — nothing was registered`); it registers under the actor captured at start. A snapshot is `fetchMcp({ mode: 'snapshot' })` → `buildSnapshotPack` (the origin and the endpoint passed), validated against the schema and bounded at 16 MB before it registers (`<label> (live MCP snapshot)` by default); a draft is `draftFromMcp` — extracted from `POST /api/draft-from-mcp`, which keeps answering API callers — so a draft job's canonical is the route's byte for byte. A typed label the other live kind holds is a 409 (the registry deduplicates by label and carries verdicts). Every terminal state writes one `live.fetch` row (`{ kind, outcome, typed, mcpEndpoint, jobId, packId, stages, gaps }` on the origin) through `auditAfterAs` (`server/audit-after.mjs`, new: `auditAfter`'s form for a captured actor). The configured scope (inert when unset): `OBSERVOGRAM_SNAPSHOT_METRIC_PREFIXES`, `_FOLDER_UIDS`, `_DATASOURCE_UID` for the deployment and `OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<KEY>_SNAPSHOT_*` for one org (its owner's only), the org's overriding the deployment's field by field; a request's `scope` replaces it whole, a variable that does not parse is a 400 naming it (and `GET /api/mcp/jobs` `scope.errors`); the draft and the refresh never read them; `server/fixtures/serve-child.mjs` STRIP gains the three. A journey whose Pack B is a snapshot saves it as a file — an inventory read at one time, never re-fetched as a draft. `server/test-mcp-jobs.mjs` (new, 18), `server/test-authz.mjs` (the four rows in `EXPECTED_LIVE_MCP`, the probes, a case row; the live MCP API's reads take no CSRF header, as every GET), `server/test-tenancy.mjs` (a snapshot job per org; the other org's job is a 404 to read and to cancel; acme's configured scope never in bravo's answer), `server/test-store-guards.mjs` and `tools/test-contract-guard.mjs` (`server/live-jobs.mjs` holds no SQL and spells no tool name).
- **The live panel — test, choose Draft (scaffold) or Snapshot, scope, the gate log with resume; the pickers say scaffold or snapshot** (**a declared default change**): "new from live" opens **New pack from a live MCP server** (`#draft-mcp-panel`, still a dialog) in three steps. **1 · Test the connection** — the draft picker, the auth field, **test connection** (`#live-test-btn`, `POST /api/mcp/ping`; its status `#live-ping-status` announced, its result the MCP panel's block). **2 · Choose what to build** is drawn only after a connected test for the target on screen — any change to the picker or the auth field hides it again: **Draft — scaffold from MCP discovery** or **Snapshot — inventory what is actually deployed**, Draft preselected unless the org (or the server) configured a snapshot scope; under Snapshot the plan computed in the browser from the ping's mapped inventory (`fetchPlan`, `/lib/live-fetch.mjs`: `Not offered by this MCP: … — the snapshot will name them`, and while tool pages remained, "not known", never a gap) and the scope fields **Metric prefixes** and **Dashboard / rule folder uids** prefilled from `GET /api/mcp/jobs` (no datasource field: no advertised tool takes one); Pack name (its placeholder what the server uses: `live-snapshot` / `production-live`) and Label; the duration from this org's last finished job of that kind, else the measured range, the same for both kinds. **3 · Progress and result** — both kinds run as live jobs (`#draft-mcp-go-btn` "start draft" / "start snapshot" → `POST /api/mcp/jobs`; the panel never posts a draft itself), polled every second while the panel is open, every five while closed, ten after a lost connection (`connection lost — retrying`); the gate log (`#live-gatelog`) draws every stage of the kind with an icon and a state word (waiting, reading…, done, failed, skipped — never colour alone), its counts and its message or gap, every MCP-sourced string by `textContent`; an elapsed time and **cancel**; at the end `Registered <label> — <n> artefacts; gaps: …` with **open it** and **compare with <Pack A>**, a draft's review block as before (its YAML read by id), or the failure, the cancel or the 404's sentence. **Resume**: the job is remembered per login and org (`studio/api.mjs` `rememberLiveJob` / `recallLiveJob` / `forgetLiveJob` under `liveJob.v1:<login|local>:<org>` — an id, a kind, an origin and a label, never a credential; `forgetMcpUrls` clears it at sign-out), so a reload polls it once at boot (identity and open postures only — never the bundle, never the token posture); opening the panel also follows a job started in another tab (`GET /api/mcp/jobs` `running`); while it runs "new from live" reads `new from live · live job running`; a job that ends with the panel closed is toasted. The key clears once the result was shown, or on the 404. The home's quick start (Krystaline) now fills the URL and tests the connection; the person then chooses. The home card's **Connect** does the same (D8): it opens the live panel with the endpoint chosen on the card (or the typed URL for a reader the server lets type one) and its auth, tests the connection there and drafts nothing — `connected — choose Draft or Snapshot in the live panel`, or `the connection test failed — the live panel says why`; its one-click draft, the capability report and **Render the manifest** under the card are gone. **The pickers say scaffold or snapshot**: `packSelectLabel` (both pack selects) and Compare's `packOptionLabel` append ` · scaffold` / ` · snapshot` from the catalogue's `live`; Compare's pack header gains a chip (`Scaffold — drafted from MCP discovery; sections marked scaffold are not compared` / `Snapshot — inventory read from <origin> at <time>`), and its source pill reads the entry's `live` before the id guess. New in `studio/live-model.mjs`: `liveTargetKey`, `stepTwoVisible`, `preselectedKind`, `scopeFormModel`, `scopeFromForm`, `planModel`, `gateLogModel`, `STAGE_WORDS`, `STAGE_ICONS`, `elapsedText`, `liveResultModel`, `LIVE_JOB_GONE_TEXT`, `liveKindSuffix`, `liveChipText`; `studio/live-api.mjs`: `readLiveJobs`, `startLiveJob`, `pollLiveJob`, `cancelLiveJob`; `studio/live-view.mjs`: `renderGateLog`, `renderLiveResult`; the `.mcpc-*` zone gains the steps, the choice and the gate log (the AA scan reads them). The static bundle names the jobs (`FEATURES` `['/api/mcp/jobs', 'Building a pack from a live MCP server']`; the README 501 list and DOWNSTREAM §10 say it). `server/fixtures/fake-mcp.mjs`: a handler's `{ __isError: text }` answers a tool error. `tools/test-live-model.mjs` (+8), `server/test-live-studio.mjs` (+1, browser: test → choose → start → the gate log → done for a snapshot and a draft, Draft preselected, an auth change hides step 2, the plan, an MCP error carrying markup creates no element, both pickers' suffixes, a held job resumed after a reload), `server/test-settings-studio.mjs` (the live panel and the home's Connect test with the id; no draft posted), `tools/test-studio-bundle.mjs` (T6).
- **Tests**: 1169 → 1222 for C1 as a whole — `tools/test-golden-diff.mjs` (new), `tools/test-live-snapshot.mjs` (new, 24), `server/test-mcp-jobs.mjs` (new, 18), `tools/test-live-model.mjs` (+8), `server/test-live-studio.mjs` (+1), and cases within `tools/test-crawl.mjs`, `tools/test-mcp-transport.mjs`, `tools/test-diff.mjs`, `tools/test-service-keys.mjs`, `tools/test-contract-guard.mjs` and `server/test-tenancy.mjs` (assertions).

### Rebadge batch 3, C3: comparison identity modes
- **`tools/lib/identity-modes.mjs`** (new; listed — `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json` 55 → 56 modules; pure, browser-safe, imports `artefact-model.mjs` and `artefact-classify.mjs` only; RULINGS R3): what pairs an artefact of pack A with one of pack B. `IDENTITY_MODES` — `behaviour` (`keyOf` is `identityKeyOf` itself, the default `DEFAULT_IDENTITY_MODE`), `name` (`nameKeyOf`) and `id` (`idKeyOf`), each `{ id, label, matchedBy, keyOf, hint }`; `identityMode(mode)` (an unknown id is a TypeError naming the three); `nameOf(artefact)` and `idOf(artefact)` → a string or `null`, from one material row per family, total over `FAMILIES` (a dashboard's `params.title` / `params.uid`, else its spec id; an SLI, SLO or derived view's spec id; a backend's `id`; a metric, recording rule, alert rule or scrape job's name — the name is the id; never a positional `XXX-NN` id, never an adapter's prose `title`); names are trimmed, white space collapsed and lower-cased, uids keep their case. A key keeps the `<kind>::` prefix, and an artefact without a name (or a stable id) is keyed with the side and its own artefact id (`nameKeyOf(a, { side })`), so it never pairs. `pairingOf(artefact, mode)` says why in words (`dashboard uid "ord-1"`, `no name — never paired by name`); `identityFamilies()` lists the families it has a row for. Nothing calls it yet: inert.
- **`tools/lib/diff.mjs`** (now imports `identity-modes.mjs`; behaviour, no export changed — the default byte-identical): `diffPacks(a, b, { scopeMode, service, identity })` takes the pairing key — omitted, a mode id (`'behaviour'`, `'name'`, `'id'`) or a function `(artefact, { side }) → '<kind>::…'`. The resolved key groups both sides (with the side, so an artefact without a name never pairs) and keys the parked placeholders; behaviour still decides aligned vs drifted, so a pair formed by name or id can drift. Omitted, `'behaviour'` or `identityKeyOf` itself is today's code path and adds no key — the stored diff goldens (`tools/fixtures/golden/diff/`, unchanged) now also run with `identity: 'behaviour'` and `identity: identityKeyOf`; any other mode adds `identity: { mode: 'name' | 'id' | 'custom' }` after `scope`. A function whose key drops the `<kind>::` prefix is a TypeError (`diffPacks: an identity function must return "<kind>::…" for each artefact (got "<x>" for a <kind>)`); an unknown mode names the three. `keyOf` stays `identityKeyOf`; verdict keys, the traceability graph, the remediation flow and chain history never see a mode, and `GET /api/diff` takes no parameter.
- **The fixture with three count sets** (`tools/fixtures/compare-modes/a.pack.yaml`, `b.pack.yaml`): two packs sharing 13 artefacts verbatim, plus three dashboards renamed under the same uid, two retitled-alike under other uids, one backend under another id and one alert rule with another expression — behaviour pairs 15 (5 only in A, 5 only in B), name 16 (4, 4), id 17 (3, 3); 13 aligned in every mode.
- **Compare pairs by behaviour, name or id — re-keyed in the browser over the two packs on screen, no refetch; the stat bar restates the mode** (**a declared default change**: the stat bar gains a **paired by** cell and a **Pair by** switch follows it): `studio/compare-identity.mjs` (new) loads the vendorable `/lib/identity-modes.mjs` and `/lib/diff.mjs` on first use (literal specifiers, so the static bundle's import map carries both — its bytes change; Compare stays denied there) and draws three native radios (`name="compare-identity"`, each hint its `aria-describedby`; arrows, Tab and Space by default). Behaviour is `GET /api/diff`'s answer itself; Name or Id runs `diffPacks(pack, packB, { scopeMode, service, identity })` over `state.pack` / `state.packB` — exactly `adapt()` (the parity case in `tools/test-identity-modes.mjs`) — after checking they are the packs the server compared; `state.compareIdentity` is session-only and outside `diffSelection()`, so a switch never refetches. Compare's stat bar, the cards and their per-column counts, the layer order, the pack headers, the slice hint and the Summary tile read that view diff; in a name or id view an in-both pill's title and the drawer (`openDrawer`'s new `opts.pairing`) say `Paired by id: dashboard uid "ord-1"`, an only-in pill says `has no name` / `has no stable id`, and a note says `Paired by name in this browser over the two packs on screen; behaviour still decides aligned vs drifted. Chains, Diagnose and every action pair by behaviour.` A change is announced (`Pairing by name: 16 in both, 4 only in A, 4 only in B.`). When the modules do not load, when the taxonomy override did not bind as the server's (`state.taxonomyBindError`, set by the boot's binding) or when the packs on screen are not the compared ones, Name and Id are off with `Name and id pairing could not load in this browser (<reason>) — behaviour pairing is shown.` Diagnose, Atlas, Compile, trace preferences, verdicts and every action keep the server's behaviour diff. CSS: `.compare-identity-*` and `.c-mode` in app.css under their own `==== Compare pairing` marker, measured on the card and the page by the AA scan (`tools/test-build-model.mjs`).
- **Tests**: 1222 → 1246 for C3 as a whole — `tools/test-identity-modes.mjs` (new, 23: the modes, the diff's identity, the three count sets of `tools/fixtures/compare-modes/`, the parity against a child server, the studio's models), `server/test-live-studio.mjs` (+1: Compare in headless Chromium — Name then Id by an arrow key at the three count sets with no second `/api/diff` request, the cards' per-column counts equal to the stat bar's in every mode, the pill and the drawer naming `dashboard uid "ord-1"`, Diagnose on behaviour), and assertions within `tools/test-golden-diff.mjs` (the default path per case), `tools/test-build-model.mjs` (the Compare pairing zone) and `tools/test-vendor-manifest.mjs` (`EXPECTED_MODULES` +1).

### STORE_PLAN slice 6b — Settings
Settings is the org's administration as a studio mode: Advanced → Settings or the account menu opens it, its sections and every control drawn from the rank the server reports for the active org. 6b-i (branch `codex/settings`) builds what operators and admins use; 6b-ii (branch `codex/settings-owner`, stacked on it) the owner's sections — users, organisations, the join role. The design's §19 decisions were taken as ratified (D-A two PRs, D-B pickers list first with the typed URL kept, D-C one environment editor with two doors, D-F the server texts, D-J the home reads the org's MCP endpoints for operators and up).
- **The mode** (`studio/settings-view.mjs` renderers, the pure models in `studio/settings-model.mjs`, the loaders in `studio/settings-api.mjs` through `requestJson`; `host.settings` built by `app.mjs`): a section nav (Environments, MCP endpoints, Members, Audit) where a section the reader's rank cannot open is listed `aria-disabled` with its reason (`needs the admin role in <org> — yours is operator; ask an admin of <org>`); Back returns to where Settings was opened from, a journey tab leaves it, a reload lands on the same section, the ORG chip reloads into Settings in the other org. One banner names the way in where the server names one, in its words: the token posture (the probe's 403), a server without sign-in (its posture sentence, or "you act as local, an owner" on loopback), the static bundle (`501: Settings needs the <product> server; …`). The SERVICE chip and the context bar are hidden in Settings. A signed-in user in no org is told the boot's refusal instead of an empty page.
- **Environments** (operators; every member reads): one editor — a pop-up over one environment — opened from Settings and from the service page (Add environment, Edit environment, the no-environment line's Add environment): name, tier, bindings, links and the MCP endpoint; it waits for the org's endpoint list before it draws; a save sends only what changed, so a tier save neither resends nor clears the binding; a list that cannot be read keeps the binding and says why. The service page's sentences now point at it (`Edit environment binds one of <org>'s MCP endpoints.`; a missing environment on the Build hand-off names the page's Add environment) instead of naming routes.
- **MCP endpoints** (admins write; operators read the URL and the variable's name; viewers the name and origin only): register, edit and delete the org's records — the token as the NAME of a variable under the org's prefix (`OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<ORGKEY>_<NAME>`), never its value; the delete names the environments it unbinds. A credential in the URL, another org's variable — the server's sentence as served.
- **The MCP pickers send `mcpEndpointId`**: the refresh panel, the home's source card (D-J), the draft panel and the deploy modal list the org's endpoints first and keep a typed URL; a chosen endpoint is sent as `mcpEndpointId`, never with `mcpUrl`; rollback and verify follow it and read a refusal as a sentence (no raw JSON in the verify line); a write re-reads the endpoint before it sends, so one moved or deleted since the list was drawn sends nothing and says so; a deploy profile remembers its endpoint per org (`mcpEndpoint.v1`), and in another org names it in a note instead of guessing. The picker's empty line offers Settings → MCP endpoints only to a reader known to be an admin.
- **Members and the org's name** (admins): add by login or verified email (an existing member gets the role), change a role, remove; the last enabled admin's Remove and lower roles are drawn `aria-disabled` with the sentence naming the way out before the server says it; a change to oneself asks first; removing oneself reloads this browser into the next org; Rename follows into the head and the ORG chip. A member row carries no email. The no-org screen's hint now reads `An admin of an organisation adds you in Settings → Members; reload this page once they have.`
- **The audit** (admins: the org's rows; owners: the deployment's too): filters by actor, kind, action, target and whole UTC days (`From`/`Through` → `since` and the day after as `until`), older rows paged by `next`, the actor as the server writes it; a filter the server refuses is its sentence.
- **Server text**: the token posture's way in under `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off` (`server/authz.mjs`) says `restart it without OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off, once a user exists (npm run users -- add <login>) or with OIDC configured` — adding a user alone arms nothing there, and neither does configuring OIDC (`OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off` beats it; 6b-ii dropped the `, or configure OIDC` that named it alone, here and in the second-org 409 under `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off`, `server/identity-admin.mjs`, which now reads `restart it without OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off, once a user exists (npm run users -- add <login>) or with OIDC configured, and sign in as an owner`).
- **Static bundle** (`studio/static-backend.mjs`): the Settings reads answer `501 Settings needs the <product> server…`; the page shows the sentence as its banner, no section, nothing logged (T7).
- **CSS and the AA scan**: the `.set-*` rules live under a `==== Settings` marker in `studio/app.css`; the scan in `tools/test-build-model.mjs` reads the zone as its own slice, every colour on its own background, none restyled by `ux.css` / `reskin.css`.
- **Tests**: 1055 → 1102 tests — `tools/test-settings-model.mjs` (new: the access model, the section and editor models, the MCP target body, the audit query, the renderers headless), `server/test-settings-studio.mjs` (new — the journey in headless Chromium through `OBSERVOGRAM_PLAYWRIGHT`, skipped without it, `OBSERVOGRAM_SETTINGS_SMOKE=require` fails instead; the knob joins `server/fixtures/serve-child.mjs` STRIP; its own fixture, where ada is acme's only enabled admin), `server/test-authz.mjs` (the AUTH=off sentence, the `mcpEndpoint.v1` keys), `tools/test-services-model.mjs` (the service page's sentences, `persistence.seed`), `tools/test-build-model.mjs` (the Settings zone in the scan), `tools/test-studio-bundle.mjs` (T3c, T6, T7's Settings step), `tools/test-studio-layout.mjs` (the editor exemptions). No vendored module changed.
- **Docs**: README (Roles, the token posture, the identity API's and the services section's "In the studio"), `docs/USER_JOURNEY.md` (invariant #10, "Settings"), `docs/STORE_PLAN.md` (slice 6b-i built; slice 6 (6b, Settings) is next), `docs/HANDOVER.md`, `docs/UI_CONVENTIONS.md` (`host.settings`, the `.set-*` zone), `docs/DOWNSTREAM.md` §10, `docs/UPDATE_JOURNEY.md`, `.github/copilot-instructions.md`.
- **6b-ii — the nav's deployment group** (D-H): an owner's nav gains Users, Organisations and Join role under "The deployment"; a signed-in reader who is not an owner reads `Users, organisations and the join role are an owner's — ask one. (A deployment with no owner gets one from the server's shell: npm run users -- owner <login>.)` instead of a list of sections they cannot open; the token and closed postures draw the items unavailable with their reasons.
