- **Studio**: `studio/brand.mjs` (new; UI_CONVENTIONS §2–3 loader, zero static imports) — `readBrandConfig(doc)` reads the injected `#brand-config`, `loadBrand({ importFn, doc })` loads `/lib/brand.mjs` the house way and returns the normalized brand with its `chrome`; `state.brand` holds it. Every chrome string now reads `state.brand.chrome` — the header's aria-label, logo (`logoHtml`, the one innerHTML the raw `logo.svg` reaches), wordmark and tagline (`studio/app.mjs` `installObservaChrome(chrome)`), the Advanced → About item, the About card (aria-label, wordmark, tagline; the changelog link only when the brand has one), the API-unreachable screen, the Reset confirm, the two product-name sentences in the home lede and the crawl evidence note, the Discover scanner title and hero (`studio/layers-view.mjs`; no `hero.src` ⇒ the CSS fallback), the origin chip's tip (`studio/ux-kit.mjs` — `STATUS_PROPERTIES.origin.values.library.tip` is now a function of the chrome, `statusRecord(property, value, { chrome })` resolves it; headless without a brand it reads "Instantiated from the pack library."), the atlas compass mark (`studio/atlases.mjs` draws `opts.compassMark`; `studio/atlas-view.mjs` passes the brand's) and the version tooltip (`studio/build-label.mjs` `renderVersionChrome(container, model, chrome)` — the third argument is new; without it no tooltip is painted). Default mode paints the same DOM text: `DEFAULT_BRAND` is those strings.
- **Default-mode boot order changes (stated, not hidden)**: `boot()` used to mount the header synchronously first; it now awaits the brand first — `const brandReady = loadBrand()` is kicked off at `studio/app.mjs` module top level (the fetch of `/lib/brand.mjs` overlaps the module graph) and the shell gains `<link rel="modulepreload" href="/lib/brand.mjs">`, so in practice the header paints after one extra, already-preloaded module evaluation; an unconfigured deployment now requests `/lib/brand.mjs` on every load. The shell file is a source edit (the preload line); the server still serves it byte for byte. `tools/build-studio-bundle.mjs` strips the preload line (the module is a `data:` URL in the import map) and inlines `brand.mjs` like every other `/lib` module; the bundle, built from the unbranded shell, stays unbranded (a `--brand` is the follow-up; `studio/static-backend.mjs` is exempt from the source guard for that reason — delivered in Rebadge batch 2, B1, above, which also lifts the exemption).
- **`tools/gen-design-tokens.mjs`**: `designTokensDocument(css, brand = null)` / `designTokensJson(css, brand = null)` — gated on `brand.configured` (an unconfigured brand yields exactly the one-argument document, so `studio/design-tokens.json` is unchanged); a configured brand's `tokens.light` override both themes and `tokens.dark` refine dark — the cascade the injected `<style id="brand-tokens">` has in the browser (`:root{}` later than the CSS's `[data-theme="dark"]{}` at equal specificity) — and a `brand` key names it; a token the CSS does not define throws `brand: tokens.<theme>.<name> is not a token studio/design-tokens.css defines`. CLI: **`--brand <file.json>`** (explicit — the generator never reads `OBSERVOGRAM_BRAND_FILE`, so `--write` in a branded shell still regenerates the vendorable default) and **`--out <path>`** for the branded JSON; `--brand` with `--write` refuses: `design-tokens.json is the vendorable default; write branded JSON with --out <path>`. `runDesignTokensCli(argv, io)` is exported for the test.
- **Hermetic suites**: `server/fixtures/serve-child.mjs` STRIP carries the nine `BRAND_*` names (both spellings), and every server suite that imports server code in-process now deletes that one list — `const { STRIP } = await import('./fixtures/serve-child.mjs')` and the two-spelling loop — before it sets its own posture and before any server module loads, every such import dynamic (a static one is hoisted above the loop). The per-suite hand lists had drifted (`TAXONOMY` and `TRANSPORT_HOOK` missing from six, `TRUST_PROXY_AUTH` from `test-store-import`; `test-smoke`'s static `import { start } from './index.mjs'` ran `initAuth()` before its strip), so a shell with `OBSERVOGRAM_TAXONOMY`, `OBSERVOGRAM_TRANSPORT_HOOK` or `OBSERVOGRAM_TRUST_PROXY_AUTH` exported failed `test-auth-local`, `test-auth-oidc`, `test-tenancy`, `test-store-ops`, `test-smoke`, `test-store-import` and `test-store`. **`server/test-hermetic-suites.mjs`** (new, in `npm test`) is the source guard: over the tokenizer's blanked code (comments and string bodies are not imports), every `server/test-*.mjs` importing a server module must bind `STRIP` from serve-child, run the two-spelling loop, import no server module statically, import every one dynamically after the loop and write no `OBSERVOGRAM_*`/`TOMOGRAPH_*` before it; negative cases prove each rule bites. 776 → 780 tests.
- Docs: README "Rebadge The Studio (brand config)" (the file shape, the scalars, what derives from `name`, what the server injects, the refusals, the trust boundary, what is never rebranded, the tokens and the bundle), the API Surface intro and rows, the Repository Map rows; `docs/DOWNSTREAM.md` §9 row and §8; `docs/VENDORING.md` rows; `docs/UI_CONVENTIONS.md` §3; `studio/assets/README.md`; `.env.example`; `docs/UPDATE_JOURNEY.md` W4 paragraph. `docs/ADAPTER.md` untouched (no adapter interface change).
- **Tests**: `server/test-brand-shell.mjs` (new, in `npm test`) — BROWSER (headless Chromium through `OBSERVOGRAM_PLAYWRIGHT` or the bare `playwright`, skipped when neither imports, `OBSERVOGRAM_BRAND_SMOKE=require` fails instead): default and acme children at 1366 and 390 px — title, header wordmark/tagline/aria/tooltip, footer, About card (aria, tagline, changelog href) and the rendered text's `Observogram` presence/absence, no page error, nothing off the loopback; default child: every shell path (`/`, `/index.html`, `/index`, a SPA path, a query path) byte-equal to `studio/index.html` with the static file headers, one ETag, 304 on a conditional GET, `/lib/brand.mjs` served; acme child: every shell path and the sign-in, failed sign-in, forced-change and reverse-proxy explainer pages name the brand and carry no `Observogram`, the config JSON and tokens injected, `#build-label` kept, `/healthz` brand-free; `OBSERVOGRAM_BRAND_NAME` alone (and `TOMOGRAPH_BRAND_NAME`) rebrands the shell; a missing or invalid brand file is REFUSED naming the path. `tools/test-brand.mjs` (new, in `npm test`) — the inert proofs (defaults deep-equal, shell identity, every anchor once, `loadBrand({ env: {} })`), the branded proofs (the acme fixture `tools/fixtures/brand/acme.json` and `{ name }` alone leave no Observogram in any chrome string, the shell or the injected JSON; `#build-label` survives with its version), escaping, token refusals, the file/env contract (path named, contents never), the golden proof (compile, crawl, adapter and gen-site import no brand), the studio loader with an injected import, the token generator (one-argument equality, the per-theme merge, the `brand` key, the unknown-token refusal, the CLI's `--brand`/`--out`/`--write` rules), and the **source guard**: a tokenizer strips comments (strings, nested templates and regex literals kept) from every `studio/*.mjs` (`static-backend.mjs` exempt), `server/auth.mjs` and `server/auth-proxy.mjs`, then `/Observo(?:gram(?![-_])|<)|OBSERVO(?:GRAM(?![_A-Z])|<)/` must not match — the protocol spellings (`X-Observogram-*`, `OBSERVOGRAM_*`, `ERR_OBSERVOGRAM_*`, lowercase `observogram…` keys, cookies, paths) pass by design. `tools/test-build-info.mjs`: `renderVersionChrome` without a chrome paints no tooltip, with the default chrome today's, with an acme chrome the brand's.

### Downstream seams, W6: the embeddable studio bundle
The distribution seam (`docs/DOWNSTREAM.md` §10): a downstream that serves the studio behind its own static host builds it as one HTML file that assumes no server. Inert by default: nothing under `server/`, `tools/lib` or the live studio changes; the builder only reads, and the goldens are byte-identical (proven by `tools/test-studio-bundle.mjs` T4, sha256 of every file under `studio/`, `tools/lib` and `server/` before and after a build).
- **`tools/build-studio-bundle.mjs`** (new; `npm run build:studio`) — `node tools/build-studio-bundle.mjs [--pack <file> [--id] [--label] [--description]]… [--pack-url <url> [--id] [--label] [--description]]… [--out dist/studio/index.html] [--no-remote-fonts] [--check] [--json]`, exit 0 / 1 (a pack fails the schema, a specifier cannot be inlined, a file is missing) / 2 (usage). The module graph from `studio/app.mjs` and `studio/static-backend.mjs` is collected by `tools/gen-vendor-manifest.mjs`'s tokenizer (`importSpecifiers`: `import … from`, `export … from`, a side-effect import at a statement start, `import()` — comment-, string-, template- and regex-aware, so a specifier in a comment is neither collected, rewritten nor asserted against, and the `/*` inside a `//` comment at `tools/lib/compile.mjs` does not pair with the `*/` inside a later template literal and hide the code between them from the build guard, as a regex comment strip did); `/lib/x` is `tools/lib/x` as the server's mount resolves it; a bare package, a `node:` module or a module outside `studio/` and `tools/lib` fails the build naming file:line. Each module becomes a `data:text/javascript;base64` address under a bare key (`studio/<file>.mjs`, `lib/<path>.mjs`) in an inline `<script type="importmap">`, with only its import-specifier strings rewritten — no transform of the code (live bindings, cycles, `import().catch`, re-exports untouched); a path specifier that survives the rewrite fails the build. `studio/index.html` is the template: each `<link rel="stylesheet">` becomes `<style data-src>` in place (a stylesheet containing `</style` fails the build), `static-backend.css` follows `reskin.css`, the Google Fonts links stay unless `--no-remote-fonts`, the `app.mjs` script becomes the config JSON (the spec schema and the packs' canonical manifests; `<` and the two JSON line terminators escaped), the import map, the install of the static backend, the import of the app; a `<noscript>` names the browser floor (import maps: Chrome 89, Firefox 108, Safari 16.4); a leftover `href="/…"` or `src="/…"` fails the build. `--pack` is validated against the schema at build time; `--pack-url` refuses userinfo and credential query parameters (`tools/lib/mcp-url-safety.mjs`'s rule) and `--json` prints URLs stripped — the file is distributed. `--check` builds in memory and prints `n modules · k stylesheets · bytes · packs`. `.gitignore` gains `/dist/`.
- **`studio/static-backend.mjs`** (new; imported by nothing in the live studio) — `createStaticBackend(config, { fetchImpl, origin })` answers, from the same `tools/lib` engines the server runs and in the server's exact shapes (each port names its `server/index.mjs` handler): `GET /api/packs` (the catalogue-entry field set), `/api/packs/:id` (+ `?env=`; schema failure → 500 `pack failed schema validation` + details), `/canonical` (JSON with `__effectiveEnvironment`/`__effective`; `?format=yaml|yml` as `application/x-yaml; charset=utf-8`), `/conformance` (`onPlaceholder` exactly when the pack carries library todos — `librarySummaryFor` ported over `tools/lib/library.mjs`), `/compile-catalog`, `/compile-artifact` (400 without `group`; `Content-Disposition`, `X-Pack-Source`, `X-Compile-*`), `/compile/:target` (`?download=1`, `?dashboardId=`; 400 on error), `/export.zip` (`server/pack-registry.mjs`'s `slugify` copied with its source named; `X-Bundle-Files`), `/api/compile/targets`, `/api/maturity-rubric`, `/api/version` (`label: v<version> · static bundle`), `/healthz`, `/api/taxonomy` (unconfigured), `/api/examples` and `/api/references` (empty), `/api/live-status` (`present: false`), `/auth/me` → 404 `{ ok: false, error: 'identity not configured' }` (the open posture, no sign-in); unknown pack → 404 `unknown pack: <id>`. **Every other `/api` or `/auth` path and every non-GET → `501 { ok: false, denied: 'no-backend', error: '<Feature> needs the Observogram server; this studio is a static bundle built without one.' }`** — Refresh from MCP, Scan a repo, Draft from a live MCP server, Uploading a pack, Compare (`/api/diff`, retrofeed), Deploy, Journeys, Build, Administration, Sign-in, else `This action`; `studio/api.mjs` shows a `denied` body verbatim. `handle(string | URL | Request)` returns null for anything not ours (a pack URL, a font), which goes to the page's own fetch. A `--pack-url` is fetched once at the first catalogue read; one that fails is an entry with `ok: false` and the error. **Compare is deliberately not answered**: the server's diff carries `comparePackBranches` (`tools/lib/traceability-graph.mjs`), whose PromQL parser is the bare node dependency `@prometheus-io/lezer-promql`, and a diff without the graph would grade differently from the server for the same packs. `installStaticBackend(config, win)` captures `win.fetch` once as `upstream` and never spells a fetch call (the `server/test-authz.mjs` studio guards hold); the **Export** button is taken over in the capture phase (the studio's own handler navigates to `/api/packs/:id/export.zip`, which no static host answers) and downloads the ZIP as a Blob under the server's file name; the header's `api` link is hidden and the Advanced "Pack catalogue API" item disabled with its sub text saying why; **the notice** `<div class="no-backend-notice" role="status">` — "Static studio — no Observogram server behind this page. Discover, Diagnose, Compile and conformance read the N pack(s) built in; Scan a repo, Draft from MCP, Compare, Deploy, Journeys, Build and sign-in need the server." — with a dismiss button remembered in `localStorage` (`studioNoBackendDismissed`, every access in try/catch). The `tools/lib` imports are static and relative so the module links headlessly under Node (the parity suite); the live studio keeps its `import('/lib/…')` idiom.
- **`studio/static-backend.css`** (new) — the notice, pinned to the bottom of the window like `.toast`, the `--og-*` tokens only; `tools/test-studio-layout.mjs` EXEMPT gains its row. Inlined by the bundle after `reskin.css`; the live studio never links it.
- **The live server now serves two more studio files publicly**: `GET /static-backend.mjs` and `GET /static-backend.css` answer 200 (JS, CSS) through `express.static(studio/)` where they used to fall through to the SPA shell — the one default-behaviour change, benign, no route-table entry (the `/` mount is already public), no golden.
- **Tests**: `tools/test-studio-bundle.mjs` (new, in `npm test`; `npm run test:studio-bundle`) — the graph and the rewrite over fixtures (every import form; a string ending in "import" is not an import; a commented import is not collected), a real build in a tmpdir (every `data:` module `node --check`ed, the 15 + 1 stylesheets in order, nothing pointing at the server, the config, the escaping, the `</style` guard, the CLI's exits and refusals with the secret in no output), the inert proof, **parity against a child server** (open posture) for every ported route over `payment-service` and a library-built fixture registered through `POST /api/validate` (status, content type, body; `onPlaceholder`; export.zip headers and entry names), the denial table, and **the real 3.8 MB bundle booted in headless Chromium**: the notice, the pack opened through the home flow, the api link hidden once the toolbar shows (`hidden` plus `display: none` — the author `display` on `.ctrl-link` beats the UA rule), `#build-label` reading "static bundle", the API menu item disabled, `POST /api/refresh-live` → 501 from the page, Export → a Blob download `payment-service.bundle.zip` with its entries and no navigation, the dismissal remembered across a reload, zero page errors, zero requests off the loopback. Playwright is an environment-provided tool, never a dependency: `OBSERVOGRAM_PLAYWRIGHT=<path to playwright/index.mjs>` (else the bare `playwright`); absent or not launching → the smoke skips; `OBSERVOGRAM_BUNDLE_SMOKE=require` makes that a failure for CI with browsers. `server/fixtures/serve-child.mjs` STRIP gains the two names. 745 → 753 tests.
- Docs: README "Serve The Studio Without The Server (static bundle)" and the Repository Map rows; `docs/DOWNSTREAM.md` §10 "Embedding the studio" and the §9 row; `docs/VENDORING.md` row for `studio/static-backend.mjs` (the bundle as the reference host of the table); `docs/UI_CONVENTIONS.md` §4 (the notice as a bottom status, the split along the bundle seam); `docs/UPDATE_JOURNEY.md` decisions.

### Downstream seams, W5: reverse-proxy identity passthrough
The auth seam (`docs/DOWNSTREAM.md` §9): an enterprise deployment that terminates SSO at a reverse proxy can have the server trust the identity the proxy forwards, instead of running OIDC or local users itself. Opt-in and inert by default: without `OBSERVOGRAM_TRUST_PROXY_AUTH=1` no header is read in any posture (an open-loopback server answers `X-Forwarded-User` as `local`, a local-users server answers it 401 — both proven to write no row), and nothing under `tools/` changes, so every golden is byte-identical.
- **`server/auth-proxy.mjs`** (new) — `proxyAuthEnabled()` (the flag, unless `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off` wins), `parseProxyAuthEnv(env)` (pure; a `TypeError` names the variable and never echoes the secret), `assertProxyAuthEnv()` / `proxyAuthConfig()` (memoised per process), `resolveProxySession(req, { db })` (resolved **once per request** — `req.observogramProxySession` — so the gate and `/auth/me` share one transaction), `initProxyAuth(app, authorize)`, `duplicatedHeader`, `rolesOf`, `describeProxyAuth`, the refusal texts `ACK_REFUSAL`, `OIDC_REFUSAL`, `EXPOSED_REFUSAL`, `PROXY_ACK` and `PROXY_AUTH_ENV`.
- **Env** (legacy `TOMOGRAPH_*` honoured, the modern name in every message): `OBSERVOGRAM_TRUST_PROXY_AUTH=1`; `OBSERVOGRAM_TRUST_PROXY_AUTH_ACK` must equal the sentence `only-the-proxy-reaches-this-port`; `OBSERVOGRAM_PROXY_AUTH_REALM` (`[a-z0-9._-]{1,64}`, default `proxy` → key `proxy://<realm>`); `OBSERVOGRAM_PROXY_AUTH_USER_HEADER` (default `X-Forwarded-User`), `_EMAIL_HEADER` (`X-Forwarded-Email`), `_NAME_HEADER` and `_GROUPS_HEADER` (unset by default) — RFC 7230 token characters only, never `authorization`, `cookie`, `host`, `content-length`, `transfer-encoding`, `x-observogram-csrf` or `x-tomograph-csrf`, no two the same; `OBSERVOGRAM_PROXY_AUTH_GROUP_ROLES` (`sre=admin,dev=operator,*=viewer`; `viewer | operator | admin | owner`, the top rank wins, `*` is every user the header names; needs the groups header); `OBSERVOGRAM_PROXY_AUTH_ORG` (the org the groups rule, default the default org; must be a live org); `OBSERVOGRAM_PROXY_AUTH_JOIN_ROLE` (`viewer | operator | admin | none`, default `none`: the first-sight membership when no groups header is configured; refused beside one — it would otherwise rule every request that omits the header); `OBSERVOGRAM_PROXY_AUTH_OWNERS` (comma list of user values); `OBSERVOGRAM_PROXY_AUTH_SHARED_SECRET` (≥ 32 characters) + `OBSERVOGRAM_PROXY_AUTH_SECRET_HEADER` (default `X-Proxy-Auth-Secret`); `OBSERVOGRAM_PROXY_AUTH_LOGOUT_URL` (http(s)).
- **Refuses to start** — (a) without the ACK, or with any other value: `OBSERVOGRAM_TRUST_PROXY_AUTH=1 trusts identity headers from a reverse proxy, which is safe only when clients cannot reach this port — set OBSERVOGRAM_TRUST_PROXY_AUTH_ACK=only-the-proxy-reaches-this-port once the proxy strips <USER_HEADER>, <EMAIL_HEADER>[, <GROUPS_HEADER>] from every client request, or unset OBSERVOGRAM_TRUST_PROXY_AUTH`; (b) beside OIDC: `OBSERVOGRAM_TRUST_PROXY_AUTH=1 and OBSERVOGRAM_OIDC_ISSUER are both set: one sign-in mode per server — unset one` (checked in `initAuth()` after the off switch and before the OIDC branch, and in `bootContext()` in the same order, so OIDC never silently wins); (c) a bind beyond loopback without the shared secret (boot check F, after C, nothing written): `refusing to bind to <host> with OBSERVOGRAM_TRUST_PROXY_AUTH=1 and no OBSERVOGRAM_PROXY_AUTH_SHARED_SECRET: beyond loopback any client could set <USER_HEADER>. Set the shared secret (the proxy sends it in <SECRET_HEADER>), or bind to loopback (HOST=127.0.0.1) next to the proxy`; (G) an `OBSERVOGRAM_PROXY_AUTH_ORG` the store does not hold; (d) a join role beside a groups header: `OBSERVOGRAM_PROXY_AUTH_JOIN_ROLE and OBSERVOGRAM_PROXY_AUTH_GROUPS_HEADER are both set: with a groups header the groups rule every membership, and a request that omits the header must grant none — set the join role to none, or unset the groups header`. `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off` wins over the flag with one warn line (`[store] OBSERVOGRAM_TRUST_PROXY_AUTH=1 is ignored with OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off — headers are not read`); loopback without the secret warns once (`[store] OBSERVOGRAM_TRUST_PROXY_AUTH=1 on loopback without OBSERVOGRAM_PROXY_AUTH_SHARED_SECRET: every process that reaches this port on <host> is trusted as the proxy`); a non-silent start logs `[studio] identity from the reverse proxy: realm … ; headers … ; shared secret …` once, header names only.
- **The request.** The headers are the session (no cookie; a cookie carried over from another mode is ignored): the secret header first (constant-time, `tokenEquals`), then the user header — required, trimmed, ≤ 2000 characters, no control character — then email (≤ 320 characters, one `@`, recorded as verified: the proxy is the authority), name (≤ 200) and groups (a comma list; an empty value is "no groups"). Any configured header appearing on two raw lines (`req.rawHeaders`; Node would join them into `alice, root`), a missing or wrong secret, a malformed value, a disabled row or a local row holding the login makes the request anonymous (401 `denied: auth`) and writes nothing. The bearer token still wins over any proxy header. The principal is a `session`, so a mutation needs `X-Observogram-CSRF: 1` as before, and the audit actor is the row's login (`proxy://<realm>#<user>`).
- **The store** (`server/store/identity.mjs`): `proxyIssuerKey(realm)`, `isProxyIssuerKey`, `PROXY_REALM_RE`; `createOidcUser` gains `joinRole` / `joinOrgId` (undefined keeps the OIDC rule); **`proxySignIn(db, { issuerKey, issuerDisplay, user, email, name, orgId, joinRole, membershipRole, owner })`** — one transaction: find or create the row `proxy://<realm>#<user>` (kind `oidc`, `user.jit { via: 'proxy' }`; no migration — every store operation and CLI that knows an OIDC row knows these), refuse `local-login` / `disabled`, sync the profile (`user.update`), then **`syncProxyMembership(db, { user, orgId, role })`** when the proxy stated groups: authoritative in that org only — `membership.jit` / `membership.role` / `membership.remove` with `via: 'proxy-groups'`, nothing written when unchanged — and `grantOwner('owner.grant', via 'proxy')` once for a user in `OBSERVOGRAM_PROXY_AUTH_OWNERS` or a group mapped to `owner`; never revoked by a request. The second request of a session writes no row.
- **Wiring**: `authEnabled()` includes the mode and `localUsersEnabled()` excludes it; `resolveSession()` delegates to `resolveProxySession()`; `initAuth()` registers `GET /auth/login` (a 401 explainer, html or JSON: `no sign-in page: this server takes identity from its reverse proxy, and this request carried no <USER_HEADER>` — a request whose headers resolve is sent to `/`), `POST /auth/logout` and `GET /auth/me` (`mode: 'proxy'`, plus `logoutUrl`), and **not** `POST /auth/signout-others`; `server/route-table.mjs` `MODES` gains `proxy` and those three routes list it (`signout-others` stays `['local', 'oidc']`); `identityModeOf` records `proxy:<key>`; `warnNoOwner` has a proxy line; the seed never runs in the mode; `GET /api/admin/join-role` gains `mode` (`local | oidc | proxy`) and, in proxy mode, `proxy: { joinRole, groupsConfigured, org }`.
- **CLIs** (`server/identity-admin.mjs`): `resolveLogin` accepts `proxy://<realm>#<user>` before the issuer rule, whatever OIDC issuer the store records (a bare login from a plain shell keeps refusing while one is recorded); `serverSignInMode` reads `identity_mode` `proxy:<key>` as `{ kind: 'oidc', issuerKey }`; `liveSignInMode` answers the proxy key while the mode runs. `packc store rekey-issuer --clear` disables `proxy://` rows too (they are kind `oidc`); `--to` leaves them alone.
- **Studio** (`studio/app.mjs`): the account menu hides *sign out my other sessions* when `mode` is `proxy`; *sign out* navigates to `logoutUrl` when `/auth/me` carries one, else says it signed out of this app only. Runtime DOM only — no golden changes.
- **Tests**: `server/test-auth-proxy.mjs` (new, in `npm test`): the parsers' table (defaults, every knob, the legacy spelling, every refusal with its text), the inert proofs (open loopback and local users), the three refusals and the bad knobs (the secret never printed), AUTH=off and the loopback warn lines, the enabled path with its exact rows and the second request writing nothing, `/auth/me`, CSRF enforced, groups raised / lowered / removed / absent, the owner grant once and never revoked, a group mapped to owner, the join role (refused beside a groups header, in-process and at boot), a disabled row (`npm run users -- remove proxy://proxy#bob`) and a local row holding the login, duplicate raw header lines, 2001 characters, a tab, a carried-over cookie, the bearer winning, the shared secret on an exposed bind (absent / wrong / duplicated / right; in no output or row), `resolveLogin` / `serverSignInMode` and the owner CLI, and hermeticity (a child never inherits the parent's flag). `server/test-authz.mjs` inventories the fourth mode; `server/fixtures/serve-child.mjs` strips every new variable (and prints REFUSED for a refusal at import); the in-process suites blank the flag; `test-identity-api` and `test-auth-oidc` pin `mode` on the join-role answer.
- Docs: README "Behind a reverse proxy (trusted headers)" (the rule, the ACK, loopback or the secret, the logout URL, the CLI spelling, `rekey-issuer --clear`), `.env.example`, `deploy/k8s/README.md` (the shared secret and a NetworkPolicy), `docs/DOWNSTREAM.md` §9, `docs/UPDATE_JOURNEY.md`.

### Downstream seams, W3: the artefact taxonomy classifier
The third seam (`docs/DOWNSTREAM.md`): packs produced by other toolchains carry typed artefacts (`type: PackSLI | PrometheusRule | …`) with foreign id schemes; one classifier now decides every artefact's family, and an operator file maps foreign type names and id patterns onto it.
- **`tools/lib/artefact-classify.mjs`** (new, zero-import, browser-safe, vendorable — listed in `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json`, 49 modules) — `FAMILIES` (artefact-model's kind vocabulary verbatim), `FAMILY_HOME` (each family's board layer, group, plain-words label and role), `DEFINES_RULES` and `ID_RULES` (the adapter's id prefixes; the ids the adapter numbers once — `OTEL-01`, `PIP-EXP-MET`, `STO-MET-01`, `PROF-01`, `NET-01`, `POE-01`, `BASE-01` — are now prefix rules, so a second id in such a family classifies like the first), `classifyArtefact(a)` → `{ family, via, layer, group, label, role }` in the order **`type` → `defines` → override id rules → `ID_RULES`** (`via` names the rule: `type | defines | override | id | none`), `familyOf`, `compileTaxonomy` / `validateTaxonomy` (the override file: `{ version: 1, types: { <TypeName>: <family> | { family, label?, role? } }, ids: [{ pattern, family, flags?, label?, role? }] }`; refused with `taxonomy: <reason>` on an unknown family, a version other than 1, an unknown key, an unanchored pattern, one over 200 characters, flags other than `""`/`"i"`, a quantified group — `taxonomy: ids[i]: nested quantifier` — an invalid regex or a pattern too slow against a 256-character id; ids are matched on their first 256 characters), `configureTaxonomy(compiled | null)` / `activeTaxonomy()` (process-wide, default none) and `describeTaxonomy`. Type names match exactly (case-sensitive). `defines` — Observogram's canonical symbol — can never be re-homed by an operator regex: the override only has to beat the id heuristic, which is all a foreign pack (no `defines`) reaches.
- **`tools/lib/artefact-model.mjs`** `classify()` delegates to `familyOf` (its export list is unchanged; a downstream vendoring it now vendors `artefact-classify.mjs` too). Its previous `defines`-then-id order is kept, so for every adapted pack the family is the same — `tools/test-golden-board.mjs` (new, `npm run test:golden:board`) pins the Discover board HTML and `{ family, via }` of all 1049 artefacts of the catalogue packs, byte-identical before and after. **The one default-behaviour change:** an artefact declaring a family name in `type` (`{ type: 'sli', id: 'foo' }`) classifies by it with no configuration; adapted packs never do — `adapt()` emits no top-level `type` (guarded by test).
- **The declared type passes through.** A layered item that carries a `type` (a typed pack from another toolchain uploaded in the layered shape) keeps it through the legacy upconvert as `metadata.annotations["observogram.artefact.type.<symbol>"]` (`tools/lib/legacy.mjs`, the symbol the item maps to: `slis.<id>`, `alerting.routes[i]`, `imports[i]` …), and `adapt()` (`tools/lib/adapter.mjs`, new export `DECLARED_TYPE_PREFIX`) carries that annotation through as the artefact's top-level `type`, beside `source` — so a typed pack reaches the board, the drawer and the diff through the one canonical pipeline. Absent, nothing is added: no catalogue pack, no legacy example and no upconvert of one produces a `type` (`tools/test-declared-type.mjs`, new, in `npm test`, guards all three and the pass-through itself), and the board goldens are byte-identical.
- **The studio binds the classifier at boot** (`studio/taxonomy.mjs`, new, zero-import: `bindTaxonomy(mod, json)`, `classifyArtefact`, `requireTaxonomy`): `app.mjs` boot() loads `/lib/artefact-classify.mjs` the house way after the identity, fetches `GET /api/taxonomy` and binds both; a fetch that fails or a document that does not compile binds the default families and warns once, never blocking the boot. `studio/discover-board.mjs` drops its per-group `prefixes`: a group takes the artefacts whose family has its home there (`FAMILY_HOME`), anything else is the layer's "Other" — the board never moves an artefact across layers — and the head reads the otel/backend/imports facts by family; `boardGroups`/`boardHeadHtml` throw `taxonomy unbound: bindTaxonomy() runs in boot() before the first render` when nothing is bound. `card-html.artefactKind()` takes the taxonomy's label/role only for an artefact placed by `type` or by an override and keeps the `ARTEFACT_KINDS` wording for every adapted one; `drawer.renderTypedPanels()` dispatches such artefacts by family (`FAMILY_PANEL`) before the untouched prefix chain. Unbound, `classifyArtefact` degrades to `via: 'unbound'`, so headless renders of adapted artefacts keep working.
- **`OBSERVOGRAM_TAXONOMY=<path.json>`** (new; `TOMOGRAPH_TAXONOMY` honoured, the modern name in every message) — `server/taxonomy.mjs` reads the file once in `start()`, before the store boots: a missing file (the ENOENT text), invalid JSON or an invalid document **refuses the start** with `OBSERVOGRAM_TAXONOMY: <path>: <reason>` and nothing is written; a loaded file is installed process-wide (so `/api/diff`, the traceability graph and the blast radius classify by it) and logged once, path only — `[taxonomy] loaded <path>: N types, M id rules` — never on a silent boot. **`GET /api/taxonomy`** (new, class viewer: `server/route-table.mjs`, `authorize()`, the AuthZ matrix and the tenancy sweep) answers `{ ok: true, taxonomy, configured }` with `Cache-Control: no-store` — the document or `null`, `configured: boolean` instead of the path. In identity mode an anonymous studio boots on the default families until sign-in, as with `/api/examples`. `server/fixtures/serve-child.mjs` STRIP gains `TAXONOMY` (both spellings; every in-process suite deletes that list). `.env.example` and the README ("Classify Typed Packs", the API table) document it; `docs/DOWNSTREAM.md` gains the seam's row.
- **The board families, with the override:** `FAMILY_HOME` — sli → L1/sli, slo → L1/slo, otel → L2/otel, pipeline_receiver + scrape_job → L2/rcv, pipeline_processor → L2/prc, pipeline_exporter_* + backend + storage_* → L2/exp, metric → L2/metrics, profiling → L2X/prof, network → L2X/net, policy_engine → L2X/poe, mesh → L2X/mesh, collection → L2X/col, recording_rule → L3/qry, derived_view → L3/view, dashboard → L3/dash, panel → L3/panel, burn_rate + forecast → L4/pol, alert_route → L4/alr, alert_rule → L4/rule, remediation → L4/heal, baselines → L5/base, chaos → L5/chaos, synthetic → L5/syn, imports → GOV/imp. The override names a family; the family decides the group.
- **Inert by default, proven:** `tools/test-golden-board.mjs` pins the Discover board HTML and `{ family, via }` of all 1049 artefacts of the 8 catalogue packs byte-identical before and after every commit of this seam, with no override and — for the vendored example — with one installed and after it is removed (board, families and self-diff alike). `tools/fixtures/taxonomy/typed.pack.json` (17 typed artefacts, foreign ids) renders as the golden `typed.unmapped.*` with no override (a flat wall of "Other" except the four artefacts declaring a family name) and as `typed.mapped.*` with `tools/fixtures/taxonomy/taxonomy.json` (every artefact in its family's group, none in "Other", the head reading the typed OTel contract, the self-diff keyed by family). `test:golden` and `test:golden:compile` are untouched.
- **`server/test-taxonomy.mjs`** (new, in `npm test`): `readTaxonomyConfig` against plain env objects, the unconfigured and configured answers (`no-store`, no path in the body), the one log line, the three refusals before the store is opened, and the STRIP proof (both spellings). `tools/test-discover-rows.mjs` extended: unbound behaviour, a `type: 'sli'` artefact on L1 and on L2, a foreign type name with and without the override, the override's label on a typed row and the unchanged wording on an adapted one.
- **`tools/test-artefact-classify.mjs`** (new, in `npm test`): the rule order, the `defines`-beats-regex proof over the vendored example, the ID_RULES order, every family's home being a board group and every board group being a home, every adapter id template being covered by `ID_RULES` with the family `classify()` gives it, the override's exact refusal texts, first-match ids, label/role overrides, `configureTaxonomy(null)` restoring the defaults, `classify()` ≡ `familyOf` over two adapted packs, and the module's purity.

### Downstream seams, W2: the MCP transport hook
The second seam (`docs/DOWNSTREAM.md`): a downstream behind a gateway with its own auth, extra headers, a proxy or a private CA can shape every MCP request Observogram makes without forking the client.
- **`tools/lib/mcp-client.mjs`** (new, vendorable, listed in `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json`) — `createMcpClient({ mcpUrl, mcpAuth, timeoutMs, transport })` moved out of `tools/fetch-live-pack.mjs` behaviour-for-behaviour: one private `send()` replaces the two `fetch()` blocks (`rpc` and `notify` built the same headers twice), and takes an injectable `transport` (`null`, an object or a promise of one — the factory stays synchronous). Exports `TransportHookError` / `isTransportHookError` (name-based), `INERT_TRANSPORT`, `normaliseTransport`, `MCP_PROTOCOL_VERSION`. `tools/fetch-live-pack.mjs` keeps exporting `createMcpClient` (the recorder and `server/routes/deploy.mjs` import it from there unchanged) as a wrapper that fills in `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_TIMEOUT_MS`. With no transport the request is byte-identical to before: same URL, same header keys in the same order, `globalThis.fetch`. `tools/test-contract-guard.mjs` guards the new file too (its `callTool(name, …)` carries no tool-name literal).
- **`tools/lib/mcp-url-safety.mjs`** gains the fetch policy that was `server/mcp-url.mjs`'s alone: `mcpUrlPolicy(raw, { allowLocal })` → `{ error } | { safeUrl, local }` (http(s) only; a local/private host refused when `allowLocal` is false — the same texts), with `isLocalOrPrivateHost` and `redactCredentials` moved in beside it. `server/mcp-url.mjs` re-exports all three (`server/test-mcp-url.mjs` keeps its imports), reads `OBSERVOGRAM_ALLOW_LOCAL_MCP` into the flag (`allowLocalMcp()`) and adds the stderr note — `validateMcpUrl()` is byte-identical in behaviour and text. The module still imports nothing (browser-safe for the studio's `/lib` load).
- **`OBSERVOGRAM_TRANSPORT_HOOK=<path.mjs | file:URL>`** (new; the legacy `TOMOGRAPH_TRANSPORT_HOOK` spelling is honoured, the modern name wins and is the one every error text spells) names a module exporting `prepareRequest({ url, headers }) → { url?, headers? }` (sync or async; omitted fields keep the input, returned headers are merged over the built ones so `Mcp-Session-Id` survives a hook that adds one header) and/or `fetchImpl(url, init) → Response-like`. `tools/mcp-transport.mjs` (new, Node-only) loads it once per process (`mcpTransport()`; `loadTransportHook({ env })` for suites), resolving a relative path against the working directory; it never logs — the CLI (`[fetch-live-pack] transport hook: …`) and the server each print the path once. The hook sees the `Authorization: Bearer` header and the raw caller URL and may replace both; the URL it returns passes the same http(s)/local-address policy as the caller's (`OBSERVOGRAM_ALLOW_LOCAL_MCP=0` still binds). Applied to every MCP request of `npm run fetch-live`, `npm run record-fixtures`, the live probes (`buildAndValidate`), the journey engine and the studio server.
- **Hard fail, no partial output.** A hook that fails to load, exports neither function, or breaks its contract at call time (throws, returns a non-object / non-string url / non-object headers / a header with CR or LF, a non-http(s) URL, a URL the policy refuses, a `fetchImpl` answer that is not a Response) is a `TransportHookError`: `fetch-live-pack` exits 1 before writing (the previous OUTPUT is kept — every swallowing catch in the probes, `safe`/`quiet`, the `quietly` defaults, initialize/notify and `observeInventory` rethrows it), the recorder exits 1 with `[record-mcp-fixtures] FATAL:` before its `--write` block, and `tools/lib/journey.mjs` treats it as a configuration error (no run record, no `vantage-lost` record, no `failed` inventory record). A hook's own error text — a `prepareRequest` throw, a `fetchImpl` rejection or the text of a Response the `fetchImpl` returns (a non-OK body, a JSON-RPC or SSE error message; a native `fetch` answer is left as is) — has the bearer, the URL's userinfo and its credential-named query values replaced by `<redacted>`, so an echoed header cannot reach the CLI FATAL line, the journey run record, the server's 502 bodies, its log or `deploys.jsonl`. Ordinary wire failures through a hook — a refused connection, an HTTP 503 on one probe, a rejection from the hook's own `fetchImpl` — stay ordinary: retried once when transient and annotated, exactly as without a hook.
- **The studio server** loads the hook once in `start()` (after the store boots; `[studio] MCP transport hook: …` once, path only — a silent boot prints nothing) and refuses to start on a load failure (`[studio] failed to start: OBSERVOGRAM_TRANSPORT_HOOK: cannot load …`). Every MCP call of `POST /api/refresh-live`, `POST /api/draft-from-mcp`, the three deploy routes and the pre-deploy snapshot goes through it (they already used `createMcpClient`/`fetchMcp`). A call-time hook fault is one `502 { ok: false, error }` for the whole request with nothing written: no live pack, no per-item results and no deploy or rollback record — `server/routes/deploy.mjs` rethrows it from the per-item catches and the initialize catches of deploy-bulk, the single deploy and the rollback, `server/deploy-helpers.mjs` from `discoverMcpToolNames` and the snapshot capture. `validateMcpUrl` on the caller's URL stays where it is; the hook's final URL is re-checked by the client. `server/fixtures/serve-child.mjs` STRIP gains `TRANSPORT_HOOK` (every in-process suite deletes that list), so a developer's exported hook never reaches a test child or an in-process start. **`server/test-transport-hook.mjs`** (new, in `npm test`): refused boot (both spellings, no exports), the single log line, a header hook on every request of refresh-live / draft-from-mcp / deploy-bulk (snapshot reads included), the 502-with-nothing-written case for refresh-live, draft-from-mcp, deploy-bulk, the single deploy and the rollback of a seeded deploy (the bearer redacted, the audit log unchanged), and the STRIP proof.
- **The journey run record persists the stripped MCP URL.** `tools/lib/journey.mjs` wrote `packB.source` as `mcp:<packB.mcp.url as the def spelled it>` — a credential query parameter (`?token=…`) or userinfo on that URL reached `ws/runs/<journey>/<ts>.json` (the `pass`/`fail` record and the `vantage-lost` one) and, through `buildCanonicalPack`'s `mcp.url` annotation, the kept `live/<stem>.json` snapshot. Both now carry `safeMcpUrl()`'s form (userinfo, fragment and credential parameters dropped, every other segment as spelled), the rule the server's draft route and `npm run fetch-live` already applied; a URL without a credential is byte-identical. Pre-existing since the journey engine, found while checking the W2 redaction; `tools/test-journey.mjs` proves both records and the snapshot clean of the token.
- `tools/lib/brand-env.mjs` gains `brandEnvFrom(env, suffix)` (the OBSERVOGRAM_/TOMOGRAPH_ rule against a given object; `brandEnv` is the `process.env` form).
- **`tools/test-fetch-live.mjs`**, **`tools/test-record-fixtures.mjs`**, **`tools/test-journey.mjs`** (extended): a hook that throws at initialize, or only from its 6th call (inside `safe`/`quiet`, after initialize, notify and tools/list succeeded), rejects `fetchMcp`; the same for `observeInventory` (3rd call = tools/list); `sampleStackSelfMetrics`, `observeAlertmanager` and `observeGrafana` called without a `quiet` rethrow the fault while a wire error through a hook's `fetchImpl` stays probe-level; the recorder exits 1 with `FATAL: transport hook …` and `--write` creates no out dir (the bearer and the URL token in no output), a header hook reaches every one of its requests; a journey run with a throwing or unloadable hook exits 2 with no run record (a configuration error, not a vantage loss). All three suites blank `OBSERVOGRAM_TRANSPORT_HOOK` / `TOMOGRAPH_TRANSPORT_HOOK` for themselves and their children, so a developer's exported hook never changes a test result.
- Docs: `docs/MCP_INTEGRATION.md` gains the "Transport hook" section (the contract, an example hook, what the hook sees and the never-log rule, the final-URL policy, the exact error texts, what is and is not a hard failure); the README's "Fetch Live From MCP" and `.env.example` name the variable; `docs/DOWNSTREAM.md` gains "The private plugin layer" with the seam's configuration row; `docs/UPDATE_JOURNEY.md` records the decisions.
- **`tools/test-mcp-transport.mjs`** (new, in `npm test`): inert by default (the inert transport, `null`, a promise of it and `undefined` produce identical request logs; the CLI run with no hook and with an identity hook produces identical normalised request tuples), hook applied (sync, async, in-place, bearer exchange, `fetchImpl`), load and call-time faults with the exact texts and redaction, the final-URL policy, `mcpUrlPolicy`'s table, the CLI exiting 1 with no file written and a pre-existing file untouched, a 503 on one probe annotated the same with and without a hook, and the module's purity.

### A manifest of the vendorable modules
The first of the downstream seams (`docs/DOWNSTREAM.md`): a private distribution that vendors Observogram's pure libraries now has a machine-readable contract to upgrade by instead of the prose table in `docs/VENDORING.md`.
- **`VENDOR-MANIFEST.json`** (new, generated, committed, shipped in the npm package) lists the 47 pure modules under `tools/lib` — every `.mjs` except `journey.mjs`, `brand-env.mjs`, `grafana-mcp-bridge.mjs`, `harness.mjs` and `retrofeed.mjs`, each excluded with its reason in the file — with per module the sha256 and byte size, the exported names (read statically from the source with a tokenizer that skips comments, strings, templates and regex literals; the guard cross-checks them against the real module namespace), the other listed modules it imports and the npm packages it needs (`promql-lezer.mjs` → `@prometheus-io/lezer-promql`, nothing else); the data file `tools/lib/site/inventory.schema.json`; the package version; and the breaking-change flags `changedSinceRelease` / `releasedExports` / `removedSinceRelease`, computed against the previously committed manifest of the same version (a missing or other-version manifest is the release baseline, so the release commit regenerates it — `docs/RELEASE_READINESS.md`). No timestamp: regeneration on an unchanged tree is byte-identical.
- **`tools/gen-vendor-manifest.mjs`** (new) — `npm run vendor-manifest` writes the file, `npm run vendor-manifest:check` exits 1 with the per-module export/sha diff when it is stale (CI runs it after the spec checksum), `--verify <dir>` hashes a downstream copy against its manifest (`ok` / `drift`, with a hint when only the line endings differ / `missing`) and `--smoke <dir>` imports each copied module and compares its exports; both validate every manifest path to stay under the root. The script imports node built-ins only so a downstream copies it beside the manifest.
- **`tools/test-vendor-manifest.mjs`** (new, in `npm test`) — the manifest must be in sync; the 47 listed paths, 5 exclusions and 1 data file are pinned; listed modules pass the purity rule (no `node:*`, no `process`, no DOM or web storage, no `import.meta`, no `require()`; import graph inside the set) and the excluded ones still break it; static exports equal dynamic exports; and **a listed module whose exports changed since the release must be named under `## Unreleased` here** (its path or its backticked basename) — that entry is the breaking-change notice downstreams read. Nothing under `server/`, `studio/` or `tools/lib` reads the manifest (asserted): the default build is unchanged.
- Docs: `docs/DOWNSTREAM.md` (new) — the snapshot → verify → smoke → bump workflow and what upstream promises; `docs/VENDORING.md` points at the manifest; `docs/RELEASE_READINESS.md` gains the regeneration step; `.github/copilot-instructions.md` pins the manifest as a source of truth.
### The store, slice 5: the audit
STORE_PLAN slice 5 (`codex/audit`): every change a principal makes through the server is now a row, and the rows can be read. The file-first routes — deploy, deploy-bulk, rollback, verify, the journey capture and run, the live refresh — write their audit row after their file (`server/audit-after.mjs`: on its own, never inside the operation; a failed insert never fails the operation); `GET /api/audit` reads the table; the actor in `deploys.jsonl` is the principal's login. See the README's The Audit.
- **The rows.** `deploy.run` / `deploy.bulk` (`{ pack: { id, version }, env, target, mode, dryRun, origin, mcpEndpoint, snapshot (bulk), items, ok, failed, tookMs }`), `deploy.rollback` (`{ rollbackOf, pack, env, dryRun, origin, mcpEndpoint, items, ok, failed, manual, tookMs }`, on the rollback's own `deployId`), `deploy.verify` (`{ outcome, alignment, attempts }`), `journey.capture` (`{ packA, packB, live, env, service, scopeMode }` — pack ids, never the paths or the MCP URL the file holds), `journey.run` (`{ startedAt, outcome, alignmentPct, gradeScore, gradePass, breaches, tookMs }` — **one row per attempt past the 404**: a failed run leaves `outcome: "error"`, or `"vantage-lost"` when a live source lost its vantage; never the error's message), `live.refresh` (`{ mcpEndpoint, refreshedAt, servicesDiscovered, toolsFailed }`, on the MCP origin). Every row carries the MCP **origin**, never a URL with a path, an item's error or a tool name; every free-text field is cut to 200 characters (`outcome` to 100); `writeAudit` refuses a `detail` over 8192 characters (`observogram store: an audit detail must serialize to at most 8192 characters`). A request refused before the MCP was contacted writes no row. `deploys.jsonl` stays the deploy file of record.
- **When a write fails.** A row the store refused leaves the operation standing: the response carries `auditError` with the store's message and one stderr line (`[deploy-bulk]   audit row failed: … (the operation stands; deploy.bulk dep_… is not on the record)`). A `deploys.jsonl` line that could not be appended still gets its row, flagged `detail.fileError: true` — the verify's 500 included — so the record of a deploy never vanishes with the file. A blank actor (a bug: every principal past the guard has one) writes neither the line nor the row (500).
- **`GET /api/audit`** (`admin`; `server/routes/audit.mjs`, the rule once in `server/audit-admin.mjs`, a sibling of `identity-admin.mjs`): an org admin reads the rows of the request's org (`scope=org`, the only scope an admin may ask for — never a deployment row, never another org's); an owner reads the deployment's — `scope=all` (the default for an owner), `scope=org` (the org selector, `X-Observogram-Org` / `?org=`, picks the org; `?org=` is never a filter) or `scope=deployment` (the rows with no org). Filters `actor`, `action`, `kind` (every action of a kind, `listAudit`'s new prefix predicate), `targetKind`, `targetId`, `since` / `until` (a date or a UTC time), `limit` 1–500 (default 100), `before` (the `next` of the previous page); newest first; `next` is the last row's `seq` when another page exists, else `null` (no more, never "try again"). Every refusal is a 400 naming the way out (`the deployment's audit (scope=deployment, scope=all) is an owner's: as an admin of org 'acme' you read its rows (scope=org, the default) — drop scope, or ask an owner`). No field-level redaction: the scoping is the redaction — an org admin sees the actor of every row of their org as is, an owner who acted there without a membership included. The route is closed where the member and user lists are (the route table's `exposed: 'refuse'`, `direct`, `closedAs: 'the audit API'`): 403 `posture` on an exposed server without sign-in; on a loopback one without sign-in only a request sent straight to a loopback address is answered. A read: it writes no row. The route table has no `later` field any more (no row waits for a later slice).
- **Changed: the actor in `deploys.jsonl` is the principal's login** — a local username, or `<issuerKey>#<sub>` for an OIDC user — as the audit's is (STORE_PLAN §5: it replaces `session.email || session.sub`, an unverified claim). An OIDC deployer who was `ada@acme.test` is `https://idp.example/realms/acme#9a3e0f`; a local user with an email on her row is `ada`. The bearer's label and `local` are unchanged; old lines are never rewritten. No audit row carries an email: a reader joins the `users` row by `login`.
- **Changed: `GET /api/deploys` and the Neuron evidence string show the deployer's login to viewers** (an OIDC deployer as `<issuer>#<sub>`) where they showed an email. The issuer is not a secret (the sign-in page sends every browser to it); a local login is the name the member list shows.
- **Roles:** `admin` gains the org's audit; an owner the deployment's. The bearer is an operator and cannot read it.
- **`packc store audit`** (`tools/store-admin.mjs`): the audit from a shell — a shell on the pod or a CI job, which cannot read `GET /api/audit`. Read-only (it writes no row; WAL makes it safe beside a running server), an owner's view: every org's and the deployment's rows by default, `--org <id>`, `--deployment`, `--all`; the route's filters as flags (`--actor`, `--action`, `--kind`, `--target-kind`, `--target`, `--since`, `--until`, `--limit`, `--before`) through the same rule (`parseAuditQuery`'s `cli` surface spells the refusals with the flags: `--limit must be an integer from 1 to 500`). One JSON row per line on stdout, newest first; the `store:` line and `next: <seq>` (when more exist) on stderr. Refuses `:memory:`, a workspace with un-imported legacy files and a missing database (never created) like its siblings. The direct-rule refusal of `GET /api/audit` on a loopback server without sign-in now names it (`…, or list it from this machine with packc store audit`).
- Tests: `server/test-audit-api.mjs` (new: the scope by principal, every refusal text, the org selector attacked from the route, every filter and their AND, paging exact at the cap of 500, the slice's rows end to end by a local user with an email — the login in the row and in the line, never the email; `auditError` under a blocking trigger; `fileError` with `deploys.jsonl` a directory, and the verify's 500 with its append faulted (one flagged row; `auditError` on the 500 when the insert failed too); the journey run's 502 row; the non-member owner's actor shown to the org's admin; no URL beyond an origin and no `@` in the slice's rows); test-store (`kind`, paging at the clamp, the size guard), test-deploy-helpers (`deployAuditRow`), test-smoke and test-tenancy (the rows per org; the sweep writes no row of the other org's), test-authz (the posture cells, the identity matrix, the README rows), test-auth-oidc (the OIDC deploy line says `<issuerKey>#<sub>`), test-chain-history (a `#` login in the evidence string verbatim), test-store-guards (`audit-admin.mjs` and `audit-after.mjs` SQL-free).

### The store, slice 4b: services, environments, MCP endpoints
STORE_PLAN slice 4, second of two PRs (4a, PR #140, put the pack registry on the store). The service records 4a backfilled, their environments and the org's MCP endpoints are managed over HTTP; every rule lives once in `server/service-admin.mjs` (a sibling of `identity-admin.mjs`, the same refusal kinds: 400 / 404 / 409, each naming the way out), the routes in `server/routes/services.mjs`. See the README's Services, Environments And MCP Endpoints.
- **The services and environments API** (operators; every member reads): `GET` / `POST /api/services`, `GET` / `PATCH` / `DELETE /api/services/:id`, `GET` / `POST /api/services/:id/environments`, `GET` / `PATCH` / `DELETE /api/environments/:id`. A service is `{ slug, name, owners, tier, description }` with its `environments` and the `packs` linked to it; the slug is fixed (packs link by slug); `tier` is `tier-1`, `tier-2`, `tier-3` or `null` (graded by the pack). An environment is `{ name, tier, effectiveTier, bindings, endpoints, mcpEndpoint: { id, name, origin } | null }`; its `endpoints` are http(s) links every member reads (a credential-looking URL is refused). A deleted service stays deleted across restarts; registering a pack that names it re-creates it. Audit rows: `service.create` / `update` / `delete`, `environment.create` / `update` / `delete`, the caller as actor; an unchanged PATCH writes none.
- **The MCP endpoints API** (admins; `GET /api/mcp-endpoints` to every member — `url` and `readTokenEnv` to operators and above, `null` to a viewer): `POST /api/mcp-endpoints` `{ name, url, readTokenEnv? }`, `PATCH` / `DELETE /api/mcp-endpoints/:id` (the delete unbinds the environments checked through it, `unbound`). A record is where the server will send the org's read token, so its three changes take the identity API's defences: `X-Observogram-CSRF: 1` in every posture (`missing X-Observogram-CSRF: 1 — changes to the MCP endpoint API need it in every posture, …`), a direct loopback request only on a server without sign-in, closed on an exposed one (`the MCP endpoint API is closed on a server bound to …`). The route table gained `direct` and `closedAs` for it; the identity API's texts are unchanged. Audit rows `mcp_endpoint.create` / `update` / `delete` record the origin and the variable's name, never the URL's path or query.
- **The MCP URL word rule.** `mcp_endpoints.url` refuses a query parameter whose *name* looks like a credential by 3a's word rule (`tools/lib/mcp-url-safety.mjs` `credentialParamName`: `token`, `api_key`, `sig`, `%74oken`, `tier=x;pwd=…` refuse; `signal`, `design`, `author`, `tier` pass), naming the parameters and never the URL: `observogram store: an MCP endpoint URL may not carry credentials in its query — the parameter(s) "token" look like credentials; remove them and name an env var in readTokenEnv`. The substring list is gone.
- **The per-org token variable.** `readTokenEnv` must be `OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<ORG>_<NAME>` (`ORG` the org id upper-cased, `-` → `_`; `NAME` of `[A-Z0-9_]+`) and must be the request's org's: the owning org of a name is the one whose prefix `OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<ORG>_` is the longest match among every org, live or removed (`OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_ACME_EU_X` is `acme-eu`'s, not `acme`'s), re-checked at each request that reads it. Without it an org admin could have the server send any variable of its process to a URL of their choice. Set it on the studio (the k8s Deployment's `env`, from a Secret), never on the CronJob; `.env.example` has the block. A removed org's variables stay its own through `purge-org`.
- **An endpoint picked by id.** `POST /api/refresh-live` and `POST /api/draft-from-mcp` take `mcpEndpointId` in place of `mcpUrl`: the record's URL, and — when the request sends no `mcpAuth` — the read token from the variable the record names, read at request time, never logged, returned or stored; a variable that is not set is a 400 naming it, before anything is fetched (`MCP endpoint "<name>" reads its token from <variable>, which is not set in the server's environment — set it on the server (the k8s Deployment's env), or send mcpAuth with this request`). The answer gains `mcpEndpoint: { id, name } | null`. `deploy`, `deploy-bulk` and `rollback` take `mcpEndpointId` for the URL only — write tokens stay per request. `mcpUrl` with `mcpEndpointId` is 400; neither is `mcpUrl or mcpEndpointId required in JSON body`. The SSRF rule (`validateMcpUrl`) still runs where the URL is fetched.
- **Roles:** `operator` gains the org's services and environments; `admin` gains its MCP endpoints. The bearer is an operator: it reads the endpoints with their URLs and never changes one.
- **Changed: `GET /api/packs/:id/conformance` grades an uploaded pack at its service record's tier** when one is set — the environment row's for `?env=`, else the service's (STORE_PLAN slice 4 §9). `declaredTier` is the tier the report was graded at, so `scorePercent`, `mustPercent` and each clause's `applies` follow it; the new `tier` object says where it came from: `{ graded, pack, from: 'environment' | 'service' | 'pack', service: { id, slug } | null, environment: { id, name } | null, mismatch }`. A mismatch between the record and the pack's own `criticality` is shown, never blocked. A client that compared `declaredTier` with the pack's `criticality` reads the pack's tier from `tier.pack` from now on. Until a tier is set on a record (`PATCH /api/services/:id`, `PATCH /api/environments/:id`) every report is as before but for the added `tier` object; a catalogue or example pack has no record and is graded at its own tier. `tools/lib/conformance.mjs` is untouched (`packc validate` and the goldens unchanged): the tier goes in through a copy of the canonical.

### The store, slice 4a: the pack registry on the store
STORE_PLAN slice 4, first of two PRs (4b — services, environments and MCP endpoints over HTTP — above). The pack registry — a pack's label, source, timestamps and the services it names — moves from `<org root>/packs/index.json` into the store (`packs` and `pack_services`; `server/pack-registry.mjs` over `server/store/packs.mjs` and `server/store/pack-links.mjs`); the pack file `packs/<id>.pack.yaml` stays the artefact, and `GET /api/packs` is byte-identical (entries, order, labels). See the README's Upgrade And Roll Back and Stale Import.
- **`index.json` is read once, then frozen.** The first start of this build imports every live org's `packs/index.json` at boot step 5 (`server/store/pack-import.mjs`), in one transaction with one `store.packs-import` row and a `pack.register { imported: true }` row per pack, and prints `[store] imported the pack registry of N org(s) into <database> (store <id>): <org> (<root>) k packs from packs/index.json · …`. The file stays where it is and no server path writes it again: it is hashed under `schema_meta.pack_index_hashes` — a key of its own, never `legacy_hashes` or the marker — in a canonical form (the entries by id, each `{ label, source, createdAt }`; `lastUsedAt` dropped) and compared at every start. An `index.json` that cannot be read (anything but absent) aborts the start naming the path, before any write — a transient `EPERM` must not become an empty registry; a corrupt one rebuilds that root's rows from the pack files, labels lost, the path in the report; a `createdAt` that is not a timestamp takes the file's mtime; a label or source over 200 characters is kept cut, never refused. A `:memory:` boot imports the same way every start and writes nothing under an org root — no `index.json`, no `packs/` directory (`session-secret` as before); a pack registered at runtime still writes its `packs/<id>.pack.yaml` — the row is forgotten with the process and the next start adopts the file (`pack.register { imported, adopted }`, label `null`, source `workspace`), as before.
- **Services and environments backfilled.** The import creates one `services` row per service the registered packs name (`tools/lib/service-keys.mjs`'s rule: the primary and the members; an aggregate has no primary), `tier` and `owners` unset, one `environments` row per environment a pack lists, and links each pack to them (`pack_services`); the report's second line counts them (`[store]   services: s created, e environments, l pack links; c packs name no service`). Catalogue packs (`examples/`, `reference-packs/`) belong to the deployment and get no rows. Their API is 4b's.
- **Every pack operation is a row.** A scan, a draft, an upload or a library register writes `pack.register` by the caller — plus `service.create`, `environment.create` and `pack.link` rows for what is new; the quick-start dedup by label writes `pack.replace`, the 200-pack cap `pack.evict`, the same content under another label or source `pack.update`; the rehydrate adopts a hand-copied pack file (`pack.register { adopted }`, label `null`, source `workspace`, actor `system`) and prunes a row whose file is gone on positive evidence (`pack.remove { reason: 'file gone' }`); a deleted service is never re-created by the rehydrate, only by a register that names it. The `lastUsedAt` touch is debounced per org into one audit-free write. A register writes the new file, then deletes the replaced and evicted files, then the rows: a crash in between leaves rows the next start prunes, never an unlabelled duplicate it would adopt.
- **RESET keeps the services.** `DELETE /api/uploads` drops the org's packs — rows, links and files, one `pack.clear` row — and the services and environments they named stay: a reset of the working set is not a deletion of the org's services.
- **`packc store export` writes `index.json`.** In place, after reconciling each live org's rows with its pack files — a file with no row adopted (`pack.register { adopted, via: 'export' }`, actor `cli`, its services linked), a row whose file is gone removed (`pack.remove { reason: 'file gone', via: 'export' }`) — it writes `<root>/packs/index.json` in the shape every earlier build wrote (ms times, mode 0644) and records its canonical hash, so 0.5.0 or a pre-store build finds every pack with its label and has nothing to adopt or prune. The report gains `index.json: N org root(s) — <path> (k packs; adopted …; pruned …)` or `index.json: none (no org holds a pack)`; a recorded index whose entries changed is refused as the identity files are, one changed in `lastUsedAt` only is overwritten; a directory export writes none. `purge-org` drops the removed root's key from `pack_index_hashes` too (`dropped from pack_index_hashes: …`).
- **Changed: the way back after a rollback that touched the registry.** A rollback to 0.5.0, or to a pre-store build after the export, restarts freely: it reads and rewrites `index.json` as it always did and never reads `pack_index_hashes`. The re-upgrade after a rollback that only read packs (`lastUsedAt` rewritten) or restarted starts with one line — `[store] <path> was rewritten by a build before slice 4 (lastUsedAt only — bookkeeping, not a change); the store's registry stands`; one that only **adopted** pack files (a rollback without a fresh export: packs registered here since have files the frozen `index.json` does not list) the same, with its own parenthesis — `(it says exactly what the store holds — bookkeeping, not a change)` — an adoption, label null and source `workspace`, records nothing the store does not know, so the store's row and its label stand and the replace never relabels from such an entry. After one that **registered, relabelled, removed or RESET** a pack it refuses — `<path> changed since store <id> last imported or exported it: a build before slice 4 registered, relabelled or removed a pack during a rollback (the registry it wrote: k entries, the store's: n)` — until `packc store import --replace`, which now re-imports each root's `index.json` with the reconcile rule (a file with no row added with the entry's label and source, a relabel taken as `pack.update { via: 'replace' }`, a row whose file is gone removed on positive evidence; a corrupt index keeps the store's labels; the report line `[store]   packs: <org>: added … · relabelled … · removed … (file gone)`), or the file is put back (the refusal prints the canonical SHA-256 — the SHA-256 of its bytes for a file that was corrupt when recorded) or moved aside (the rollback's registrations are then adopted with no label). The identity texts are unchanged.
- **The studio's service catalogue** reads `tools/lib/service-keys.mjs` (`normalizeServiceKey`, `serviceMetadata`, `isLiveAggregatePack`, `servicesForPack`, `catalogEntryOf`) — the rules the server's backfill and every register use, one browser-safe copy; `server/index.mjs` imports `serviceMetadata` from it. No behaviour change: a service literally named `mcp-gateway` still makes its pack an aggregate, as before.
- **Tests.** `tools/test-service-keys.mjs` (new): the rules and the studio's use of them. `server/test-store.mjs`: the pack repositories (upsert, the removal actions, `touchMany`, `clear`, the org-wide lists, idempotent links). `server/test-store-boot.mjs`, `test-store-import.mjs` (the pack fixtures: labels kept, a corrupt and an unreadable index, the `:memory:` trees), `test-workspace.mjs`, `test-smoke.mjs`, `test-tenancy.mjs` and `test-authz.mjs` (the register routes' exact rows) ported. `server/test-store-ops.mjs`: the Export gate asserts the written index, the hashes and the labels as v0.4.0 read them (`server/fixtures/pre-store-build.mjs`'s `catalogWithLabels`, a verbatim copy of its index reading), the rollback round trip (a read passes with the bookkeeping line; a register, relabel, removal or RESET refuses until the replace), v0.5.0's guard loop (`server/fixtures/store-050-guard.mjs`, verbatim) over an exported workspace, and `purge-org`'s drop. `tools/test-store-prestore-live.mjs` (the real v0.4.0 build, CI `store-prestore`): labels through `/api/packs`, the read-touch case, and a rollback that registers a labelled pack and is taken in by the replace.

### Spec 1.4: operational alert rules are kept in `alerting.rules`, and the repository's rules pair with the live ones
The crawler discovered every Prometheus and Grafana alert rule in a repository and kept only the burn-rate alerts (those referencing a recorded SLO series) in `spec.policy.burn_rate_alerts`; every other rule was dropped with *Excluded N operational alert(s) from burn-rate policy — … They remain available as alerting signals* — a promise nothing kept, since spec 1.3 had no field for them. Reconciled against a live Grafana snapshot, the crawled pack matched zero alerts although the repository held the very provisioning files those rules came from, with identical titles. RFC-0003 (upstream MoebiusX/otel-observability-pack, merged into develop as `e64e5813` via PR #9, proposed 2026-10-03) adds `spec.alerting.rules[]` — `name` and `expr` required; `severity` (the pack's SEV1–SEV4), `for`, `labels`, `annotations`, `engine` (`prometheus` default, `mimir`, `thanos`, `victoriametrics`, `loki`, `grafana`, `alertmanager`) and `source` optional; `routes` still the only required key, so every 1.3 pack validates unchanged. Observogram adopts it end to end:
- **Vendor.** `vendor/observability-pack-spec/v1.4/` holds the four upstream files byte for byte at that commit, `VERSIONS.json` records spec 1.4; `v1.3/` is removed (1.4 validates every 1.3 pack unchanged; git history keeps it). `SPEC_VERSION = '1.4'` and every derived path, banner, studio text and link follows (33 compile goldens and the build fixture moved by their version line alone). `tools/test-validator.mjs` pins the 1.4 schema's shape, its acceptance of `alerting.rules` and its refusal of a rule without `expr`, an unknown `engine` or a non-SEV `severity`, and every 1.3-shaped pack of the repo validating unchanged.
- **Crawler.** Operational alerts are declared in `spec.alerting.rules` with their exact names (never slugged — the key a live listing is reconciled on), expression, canonical `for`, the pack's severity from `labels.severity` beside the engine's own label, labels and annotations verbatim, `engine` (Grafana-managed by shape, Loki by path or LogQL, Prometheus otherwise) and `source` (`<file>#<group>/<name>`). The burn-rate classification is unchanged. Grafana provisioning YAML is detected by path (`provisioning/alerting/`) or shape, and a Grafana rule's expression is read from its query node. The warning became a true summary line (*N operational alert rule(s) kept in alerting.rules … M burn-rate alert(s) in policy.burn_rate_alerts*); `summary.discovered.alertRules` counts them. The crawl golden is byte-identical (its fixture has no operational rule).
- **Adapter and Compare.** Each rule projects as an L4 alerting artefact (`RULE-NN`) titled by the rule's name, tool by engine (`PrometheusRule`, `Grafana alerting`, `Loki ruler`), beside the routes; the artefact model keys an `alert_rule` on its name and compares expression, wait, labels and severity — not `engine` or `source` — so a repository's rule and the running rule are one artefact. The live fetcher declares the ruler's non-SLO rules the same way (`operationalAlertRule`, one reading for both sides), engine from what the answering tool attests, each `mcp.verified.alerting.rules[i]` unless unhealthy. Conformance is unaffected. The studio shows them in the drawer, the Discover board and the Build model.
- **Tests.** `tools/test-crawl-alerting-rules.mjs` with the fixture `tools/fixtures/crawl/operational-alerts/`: six operational rules across a Prometheus rule file, Grafana provisioning YAML and a Loki rule file, three burn-rate rules folding into two policy entries — validates against 1.4, exact names, engines, severities, `for`, provenance, YAML round-trip, six adapter artefacts keyed by name. `test-scan-live`: the repository's `DiskAlmostFull` and the ruler's pair aligned although one side says prometheus and the other attests victoriametrics; a rule whose recorded series is not deployed reads as a live operational rule — a true "live, not declared". `test-adapt`: pairing, drift on `expr`, a live-only rule.

### Proposed: the UPDATE journey — changing a pack that already exists
Docs only; nothing is built. Build is for a team with no pack, and once a pack is registered the studio has no way to adapt it: add an SLO, an alert, a route, change an objective. `docs/UPDATE_JOURNEY.md` is the user journey for it, *Build or update a pack*: one journey with two starts, where `update` opens an existing pack on Build's own screen, lists the changes against the saved version, shows the saved pack beside the pack with the changes, saves the next version of the same pack and hands the difference to deploy. It records what the code allows today (Build is a generator and cannot load a pack; one SLO per SLI, one burn-rate alert per SLO, three fixed routes; a pack's id is its content hash and its version a constant), what each stage reuses and what is new, three slices — adding an SLO, an alert or a route arrives with the second, as additions recorded in the pack on top of Build's output — and eight decisions for the owner. `BUILD_JOURNEY.md` and `USER_JOURNEY.md` link to it.

### Compare says what reads a metric only one pack holds
*Only in A 38* was a count. On the platform this is checked against — a repository scan beside the live draft of the same system — those 38 are metrics the repository declares and the live system does not report, and the scan already records on each one what in the repository reads it. Compare now says it: **6 alert rules, 36 dashboard panels and 6 recording rules read 30 of them**, the alert rules by name; the other 8 are emitted by code and read by nothing. Studio only: no engine, diff or output change.
- **`studio/metric-readers.mjs`** (pure, zero-import). `metricReaders` reads `spec.used_by` (`alert:<rule>`, `dashboard:<dashboard>/<panel>`, `recording_rule:<record>`) and `spec.origin_kind` across every artefact that stands for a metric — a family's `_bucket` / `_count` / `_sum` series are separate artefacts, and a query names the series. A recording rule is not a reader of its own output. `readersDigest` counts each reader once however many metrics it reads; `readersSentence`, `readerLine` and `readerRank` are the words and the order.
- **Side by side.** The *Only in* group prints the sentence under its title, leads with the metrics an alert rule reads, then those anything reads, and each card says its readers (*Read by alert rule RabbitMQDown*) or, when nothing reads it, where it comes from (*Emitted by code; nothing in this pack reads it*).
- **Summary.** *Quality gaps* gains a line: how many alert rules, dashboard panels and recording rules read a metric not seen live, the alert rules by name.
- **Changes needing review.** The missing list is in the same order and each row says its readers.
- **What it does not say.** "Reads a metric Pack B does not hold" is a fact of the two packs. "This alert can never fire" is not — a rule built on `absent()` fires exactly then — so no such verdict is printed. A pack that records nothing about a metric (a live draft, a hand-written pack) gets no line: unknown is not "unread".
- **Tests.** `tools/test-metric-readers.mjs` (in `npm test`): the readers of one metric and of a family, the digest, the words, the order, a scanned repository carrying the record, and the view's three places pinned in source.

### Pick a folder walks the repository itself: its dependencies are never listed
**Scan a repo → pick a folder** used a folder input, which hands the page every file under the folder before the scan rule can apply: 105,810 files in 11,874 folders for a repository whose scan reads 631 files in 157. Since 0.5.0 the rule kept the rest from being *read*; the browser still listed them all before the page saw the first one.
- **`walkScanFolder`** (`tools/lib/crawler.mjs`) walks a directory handle with the shared rule (`scanSkipsName`, `SCAN_EXT`) and does not enter a skipped folder, so `node_modules`, `.git`, build output and agent worktrees are never asked for their entries. Paths are rooted at the picked folder's name, as `webkitRelativePath` is, in name order; the picked folder is entered whatever it is called.
- **The studio** uses `showDirectoryPicker` where the browser has it (Chromium, on localhost or https) and keeps the folder input as the fallback: Firefox, Safari, plain http on a LAN address, a frame the API is refused in. The staged line counts the files as they are read. *Drop a folder* already walked this way.
- **Tests.** `tools/test-crawl.mjs`: the walk over a stand-in handle tree — what it reads, in what order, and that a skipped folder's entries are never asked for — and the studio's use of it with both fallbacks, pinned in source.

### Fixed: the account menu is reachable on every screen
Studio only. The account menu is on every screen: it moved from the context bar — hidden on the home and Build screens, so until a pack was open a signed-in user had no way to sign out or change a password — to the OBSERVA bar beside Advanced, which every screen shows. The same menu everywhere (who you are, change password…, sign out my other sessions, sign out), still none without sign-in; the ORG chip is unchanged (the OBSERVA bar already carries its own on every screen). Beside the tabs the chip's name gives way — ten characters at a laptop width, the glyph alone at phone width (its title and menu say it in full); the glyph and the caret that marks it a menu always show — so the tab titles keep their room and the popover stays on screen. Opening Advanced closes it (that toggle stops its click from bubbling, so the two menus could sit open one over the other). `server/test-authz.mjs` pins the mount, the give-way rules, the closer and that no mode rule hides its bar.
### Fixed: one scan or one live draft is one pack in the catalogue
**Scan a repo → use this pack** and **New from live → use this pack** each left two entries in the pack catalogue: *krystalinex-core (scanned)* and *krystalinex-core*, *production-live (live MCP draft)* and *production-live* — so the home screen read *krystalinex-core · 2 packs* and every live-discovered service *2 live drafts*. The route that answers a scan or a draft registers the pack (`registered.id` — `POST /api/crawl`, `/api/crawl-github`, `/api/draft-from-mcp`); the studio's adopt then posted the YAML to `POST /api/validate`, which registers what it validates, under a second id. Studio only (no server, engine or output change):
- **Adopt selects the registered entry.** `registeredOrValidated` (`studio/api.mjs`) answers `registered.id` while the catalogue lists it, and goes through `/api/validate` only when the route registered nothing (the pack failed validation — its errors are the toast) or the entry has gone since (a reset). `adoptCrawlResult` and `adoptDraftFromMcpResult` (`studio/app.mjs`) use it, so the folder scan, the GitHub-URL scan, the *New from live* panel and the home screen's **Render the manifest** all load the entry under its label (*… (scanned)*, *owner/repo (repo scan)*, *… (live MCP draft)*).
- **The post-deploy check too.** Each check drafted the live state and validated it again: one more unlabelled *production-live* per check, up to four per deploy. It now uses the draft's own entry, which replaces the previous check's.
- **A replaced pack is followed.** A scan or draft carrying the label of an earlier one replaces it on the server (as before). The adopted pack is now that labelled entry, so a re-scan, a re-draft or a post-deploy check can replace the pack on screen: Pack A or Pack B then moves to the new entry and says so (*Pack B now shows the newer production-live (live MCP draft) — it replaced the one that was loaded*) instead of naming a pack that no longer exists (`followReplacedPack`). After a deploy, a Pack B that is the live draft of the same name therefore shows the post-deploy state without pressing **Load post-deploy live state as Pack B**.
