- **Tests.** `server/test-smoke.mjs`: one scan + adopt and one live draft + adopt each leave one catalogue entry; a re-scan + adopt still one; a pack that failed validation answers its errors and adds none; a result whose entry is gone registers through `/api/validate`; and no call in `studio/app.mjs` hands a scan's or draft's YAML straight to `validateUploaded`.

## 0.5.0 — 2026-10-01

**The build-and-compare release.** A pack can now be built as well as inspected: the Build journey (Define, Compile, Verify) with a seed, an editor and spec 1.3. The studio stores its state in a database, with local and OIDC sign-in, roles enforced on every route, organisations and deploy profiles. The monitor-of-monitors work continues with early-warning delivery, the neuron view and inventory coverage. The studio was reviewed and redrawn: the pack operations in the header, Compare as Pack A beside Pack B, Discover as a board that judges nothing, one visual language as a design system downstream apps can vendor. And the use case the product stands on now holds: a repository scan and the live draft of the same system match, with what the live side could not look at reported as not checked instead of missing. Also: routes match case-sensitively (the 0.4.1 hotfix on `main`, #118, is the same fix).

### Scan a repository, draft from live, compare: the same system now matches
The use case the product stands on — scan a service's repository, draft a pack from the live system it deploys, compare — reported 68% overlap on a real platform (253 of 371 artefacts in both, 88 "only in the repo", 30 "only live", 1 drifted). Almost none of that was drift. The two readers described the same system in different artefacts, and the comparison called everything the live side could not see "missing". On the same platform it is now 281 in both, all aligned, nothing "only live", 15 reported as *not checked*, and 38 only in the repo — metrics the repository declares or queries that have no series in production, each confirmed against the metrics store.

**One reading for both sides.** What a scan and a live fetch both read, they now read through one module (docs/MCP_INTEGRATION.md, *One reading for the repository and for live*):
- **SLIs are inferred by rule name, not arrival order** (`tools/lib/sli-inference.mjs`). A repository lists rules in file order, a ruler in group order; taking "the first" recorded series made the same rule set infer a different expression for one SLI on each side — a false "drifted".
- **Alerting rules → burn-rate entries** (`burnAlertsFromAlertRules`, same module, moved out of the crawler). The live fetcher only recognised rules shaped like the compiler's own; a platform with seventy hand-written alerting rules read as having no burn-rate policy, so every alert its repository declares read "not live". A rule whose expression references a recorded series now binds to that series' SLO on both sides. `for: 600s` in a file and `duration: 600` from a ruler are one window (`canonicalRuleDuration`); a rule with no `for` no longer gets an invented `5m`. Entries read this way from live rules are real but not stamped `Verified` (`mcp.derived.*` names the rules).
- **Alertmanager routes** (`tools/lib/alert-routes.mjs`, moved out of the crawler). A new `alerting_routes` probe reads the configuration the running Alertmanager reports with its status and takes the routes out of it — the live pack used to carry one invented SEV1 route. Secret addresses Alertmanager prints as `<secret>` stay channels with `redacted:secret`; a repo webhook that is only `${VAR}` stays a channel with `unresolved:<VAR>` (it used to be left out, so the route read "live, not declared"). The compiler sends `unresolved:<VAR>` back out as `${VAR}` and references a redacted webhook by `url_file`. Nothing else of the configuration is kept.
- **Backends are what runs, not what the MCP supports** (`tools/lib/backend-products.mjs`: one product table for container images and scrape jobs). The fetcher minted a `Verified` backend for every row of `backend_capabilities` — a catalogue of what the MCP server can speak to. A platform running VictoriaMetrics read as also running Prometheus, Mimir, Thanos, Cortex, Cilium, Consul and Graylog (29 backends, 11 "extended surfaces"), while its collector, Promtail, node-exporter and vmalert — all with a scrape target up or a tool answering — were absent. A backend now needs evidence: a version the product reports, a scrape job of its own with a target up, or a product tool that answered (the registry's new `attests`). The catalogue still supplies version policy and stays in `mcp.capabilities.*`; `mcp.capabilities.unobserved` lists what is supported and was not seen, `mcp.backends.evidence` what attested each backend.

**The comparison says "not checked" where it could not look** (`tools/lib/diff.mjs`). The fetcher names every artefact family it had no way to observe in `observogram.unobserved.<family>` with the reason — no tool for it (the collector configuration, the SDK block), or the tool failed (a Grafana that refuses the login). An artefact the other pack holds in such a family goes to a new `notObserved` bucket instead of "declared, not live", outside the union. Seven declared dashboards behind an HTTP 401 are no longer seven missing dashboards.

**Metrics pair as families** (`foldMetricFamilies`, `tools/lib/artefact-model.mjs`). A histogram declared in source and the `_bucket` / `_count` / `_sum` series a store lists for it are one metric; so are a counter and the `_total` / `_created` it is exposed as. Paired name for name, the declaration read "not live" and its own series "live, not declared".

**Smaller readings corrected.** An SLI's `good` / `total` are normalised like any expression (a ruler returns rule bodies with a trailing newline). A Promtail or Alloy `scrape_configs` entry — log files to tail — is no longer declared as a metrics scrape job. Metrics a script writes in the exposition format (`# TYPE name gauge`) and pushes are declared like a client-library call (not from test files).

**In the studio.** Compare shows a *Not checked in B* group in each pane with the reason printed under it, a `not checked` chip on those cards and a sixth summary tile when there is something to say; every series of a metric family takes the family's status. A group's number is artefacts, as in the summary; when a metric spans several series the cards are said beside it (*Only in A 38 · 44 cards*). The Summary lists how many could not be checked. The live draft summary lists the backends found and, apart, what the MCP supports but was not seen running, the alerting routes read, and the families it could not observe.

**For saved journeys.** `alerting_routes` is a sixth probe family. A journey against an MCP that does not offer an Alertmanager status tool now lists it under *not exposed* (vantage `restricted`), and one whose status answer carries no configuration under *failed* (vantage `partial`), so its next run reports the vantage change once. Nothing about how a run is scored changed.

**Goldens.** `tools/fixtures/golden-crawl.pack.json`: the fixture's pure-`${VAR}` webhook route is now declared with `unresolved:GOLDEN_HOOK_URL` (one route added). `tools/fixtures/mcp/adapted/alert_rules.json`: the rule with `duration: 0` loses the invented `for: 5m`. New: `adapted/alerting_routes.json` over `synthetic/alertmanager_status.config.json`. The compile goldens are unchanged.

**Tests.** `tools/test-scan-live.mjs` (in `npm test`) is the use case offline: one system written as a repository and as the answers an MCP gives for it — groups in another order, `duration` for `for`, `<secret>` for addresses, series for histograms — scanned, drafted and compared with nothing on one side only and nothing drifted; then one rule, one route and one metric removed from the live side, each of which shows, and only it. `test-diff`, `test-crawl`, `test-fetch-live` and `test-contract-shapes` cover the pieces.

### The studio in one visual language, as a design system downstream apps can vendor
The screens had grown their own treatments: three typefaces (a serif for numbers and headings, a monospace for every label and button, a sans for the rest), a gradient per journey, a different card, pill and button on each screen. One system now sits over all of them, after the *Observogram Studio* revamp prototype. It restyles and moves nothing: layout, markup and behaviour stay in the screen stylesheets.

Observogram is upstream of other apps, so the look is not buried in this studio's stylesheet. It is four files, three of them vendorable (docs/VENDORING.md, *The design system*):
- **`studio/design-tokens.css`** — every value and nothing else: palette, type and measures as `--og-*` custom properties, light and dark. Names are semantic (`--og-accent`, `--og-second`, `--og-warn`, `--og-fail`), so a host re-themes by overriding tokens. `studio/design-tokens.json` is the same values for code that cannot read CSS (`node tools/gen-design-tokens.mjs --write`).
- **`studio/design-kit.css`** — the pieces as `.og-*` classes over the tokens: button (primary, second, text), panel, callout, stat tile, pill, input, steps, the A/B letter. No global rule, no element selector, no positioning.
- **`studio/design-bridge.css`** — defines the theme properties the already-vendored `verdict-ui.css` and `proto.css` expect from their host (`--card`, `--ink`, `--mono`, `--CMP`, `--pr-green` …) from the tokens, so a downstream gets those widgets in the same look without a theme of its own.
- **`studio/reskin.css`** — this studio's adapter, not portable: it maps the chrome's own names (`--obs-*`, `--ux-*`) and the classes the screens grew onto the tokens. It decides no colour of its own.

`tools/test-design-system.mjs` (in `npm test`) holds each file to its role: tokens only in the tokens file, every colour in both themes, the JSON equal to the CSS, the kit and the bridge reading tokens only, no colour literal in the adapter, no stylesheet reading a token that does not exist, and the load order. `studio/discover-board.mjs` joins the vendorable set (pure builders; one import, `escapeHtml`).

What the system is:
- **Type.** One sans throughout (`Inter`, then the system UI face); `--serif` and the label `--mono` resolve to it, and nothing is set in italics for style. Monospace is kept for code only — queries, YAML, `pre`/`code` — as `--code`.
- **Colour.** One page colour, one panel, one line. Lime is the primary action and "where I am" (the current step, the pressed option, Pack A); purple is the second voice (Pack B, the Build journey); amber is attention, rose is what fails. The studio's existing tokens (`--paper`, `--card`, `--ink…`, `--obs-*`, `--ux-*`, `--CMP`, `--BLD`, `--cmp-a/b`) are mapped onto the palette, so every rule that reads them is re-skinned without being rewritten. Both themes are defined; the layer colours (L1–GOV) stay, as data.
- **Surfaces and controls.** A panel is a 1px line and a 9px radius, with no glow or gradient; a tone is a line down the panel's edge and the colour of its numbers, not a wash. One primary button (lime), one secondary (outlined), one text button; pills with a 5px radius; stat tiles as a quiet label, one number and one line; the stepper as plain steps with the current one underlined.
- **Home.** The two journeys are cards with their own call to action (*Inspect a service*, *Create a pack*).
- **The chrome's height is measured** like the context bar's (`--observa-chrome-h`), so what pins under the two bars follows the real heights whatever the type.

### Fixed: Discover is a catalogue again — no verdict before there is a reference
The UX review made every screen answer "what needs my attention, what can I do next", Discover included. But Discover is the catalogue of one pack: with only Pack A loaded nothing live was checked and nothing was compared, so *0 with live evidence* and *N need attention* read as findings against a reference that does not exist. Removed from Discover (`studio/layers-view.mjs`): the decision sentence with its **Review what needs attention** and **Open the assessment** buttons, causes and measures (*Live evidence found*, *Declared only*, *Template values to complete*); the **Show** task filters (*Needs attention · Missing evidence · Template value · Live evidence · All*) and their default to *Needs attention*, which hid most of the catalogue on arrival; each layer's evidence split, *N need attention* / *Nothing needs attention* and *required checks not met* chips; the rubric line inside an open layer. `DISCOVER_TASKS`, `matchesTask` and `state.discoverTask` are gone with the filters; `tools/test-discover-rows.mjs` now holds the screen to this (no verdict, evidence count, task filter or next step in the Discover source).

**In their place: the pack board** (`studio/discover-board.mjs`, pure builders; styles in `studio/ux-discover.css`). Discover now draws the pack the way the *ObservabilityPack realisation* sheet does. The head says which pack this is — *ObservabilityPack — service*, name, version and size — with the facts the manifest states (criticality, environment, owners, OTel semantic conventions, languages, backends, imports; a fact the pack does not state is not drawn). Under it, one band per layer in the layer's colour: its code, its spec name and plain name, the artefact count, and its **groups** — L1 *SLIs* as tiles and *SLOs · targets* as dials filled to the objective with the indicator and window; L2 as the pipeline in flow order, *Instrumentation → Receivers → Processors → Exporters & storage* (signal → product), with the metric inventory beside it; L3 *Recording rules → Derived views → Dashboards*; L4 *Policy & detection → Alert routing* (severity → channels) *→ Remediation*; L5 *Baselines & performance*, *Chaos experiments*, *Synthetic checks*; GOV *Imports*. Every item is an artefact of the pack and opens its record in the drawer; a family the pack has nothing in reads *None in this pack*, without a tone; an id no group claims lands in *Other*, so the board hides nothing. A group draws six items, then *+N more*, which opens the layer's full list, one layer at a time. **Refine** (domain, search) stays above the board and marks each band with what it matches; **View** (List · Tiles · Cards · Details) moved into the open list's own bar, beside *Showing N of M* — above the board it changed nothing until a layer was open. Assessment, comparison and remediation are unchanged in Diagnose and Remediate.

### Fixed: the pack operations are back in the header, and Compare opens on Pack A beside Pack B
The UX review (2026-09) was asked to simplify the pack view — the layers and their artefacts. It also folded the pack *operations* away: loading a pack moved into an **Actions** menu, Pack B became a **Baseline** picker shown on some screens only, and Compare opened on a written summary with the two packs at the bottom of the page. Loading Pack A, loading Pack B and comparing them took more steps and read worse than before. Restored, studio only (no server, engine or output change):
- **The header.** `PACK A` · `ENV A` · `PACK B` · `ENV B` again, the Pack B picker on every workspace screen (Discover included), and **upload**, **scan a repo**, **new from live**, **export**, **mcp**, **reset** and **api** as header buttons. The Actions menu is removed (`setupHeaderActionsMenu`, `#hdr-actions`, the `.hdr-src` rule that hid the buttons); the same entries under Advanced → Administration stay.
- **Compare.** The two pack cards (pickers, swap) and the set arithmetic — `only in A · in both · only in B · union · jaccard` — lead the screen in every view, and it opens on **Side by side** (was *All differences*, third): the slice pills (`Only in A`, `Only in B`, `In both`, `A − B`, `A + B`), lens, live scope and search over the two columns of cards. *Changes needing review* and *Summary* follow in the same switch; the decision sentence and its tiles now live in Summary. Card badges and layer heads say `only in A` / `in both` / `36 in A vs 10 in B`; the role of each side (declared pack, live pack, baseline) is on the pack cards and in the tooltips.
- **Side by side is two panes.** Pack A on the left, Pack B on the right, each under a head that names it (`A · Pack A · k8s v0.1.0-crawled (prod)` / `B · Pack B · …`) and stays pinned under the context bar while the layers scroll; each pane carries its pack's colour, with a rule between them. In a pane, what both packs hold comes first, in the same order on both sides, under *In both N*; then *Only in A* / *Only in B*; then *Not compared* (template placeholders, out-of-scope live inventory, dashboard panels). Before, the two halves were unlabelled grids of identical cards, and once the pack cards scrolled away nothing said which side was which.
- **The default is not carried over.** `state.compareFocus` starts at `all` and is no longer persisted, so a browser that had saved `summary` opens side by side too.
- **A View for Compare.** Packs run to thousands of artefacts, so the side-by-side bar has its own *List · Tiles · Cards · Details* switch (`COMPARE_VIEWS`, `state.compareDetail`, persisted; Cards, the earlier drawing, is the default). List is one line per artefact — ID, name, comparison status.
- **Nothing opens behind the header.** *Refresh from MCP*, *Draft a pack from a live MCP server* and *Scan a service repo* were pinned 78px from the top, behind the stepper and the pack bar, so their titles and first fields could not be seen. Under the chrome they now open below both (`--observa-chrome-h` + the measured `--ux-context-h`), above the bar in the stacking order, and never taller than the room left (`studio/ux.css`). The artefact drawers started at the top of the window, so their heads — the artefact's name and the close button — sat behind the two bars: they now start below both. The deploy dialog and its scrim were stacked under the chrome and are now over it; *About Observogram* had no rule at all and drew as loose text at the foot of the page, and is a dialog now. `applyModeChrome` re-measures the bar as soon as its controls change, so nothing pinned under it is a frame behind the observer.
- **Scan a repo → pick a folder reads the repository, not its dependencies.** The folder picker lists every file under the folder and the panel staged all of them with a scannable extension: on a real repository 75,231 files (431 MB), 73,949 of them under `node_modules`, so the scan never started. The drop zone had an ignore list, the CLI walker another (and skipped dot-folders), the picker none — one folder, three different packs. Which files a scan reads is now one rule in `tools/lib/crawler.mjs` (`scanReadsPath`, `scanSkipsName`, `SCAN_EXT`, `SCAN_IGNORE_DIRS`, `SCAN_MAX_FILE_BYTES`) used by all three: no dot-entry except `.observability` (so `.git`, `.github`, and `.claude` with its worktree copies of the whole repository are left alone), no dependency or build folder, and the rule is applied to the path before a file is read. The same folder now stages 628 files in two seconds and the panel says how many entries it did not read. `tools/test-crawl.mjs` covers the rule and pins all three callers to it.
- **A guard for that class of bug.** `tools/test-studio-layout.mjs` (in `npm test`) classifies every `position: fixed | sticky` rule of `studio/*.css`: it either clears both bars (a `top` that adds `--observa-chrome-h` and `--ux-context-h`) or is exempt with a reason, and a modal must be stacked over both. A new pinned element fails the suite until it is classified.

### Fixed: deploy target profiles keep no credential — per user, stripped like the remembered URL, cleared at sign-out
A saved deploy target kept its MCP URL as typed — `?token=…`, userinfo and all — under one browser-wide `localStorage` key (`deployProfiles.v1`) that no sign-out cleared, while slice 3a (below) had already given the remembered MCP URL the safe treatment. Now, in `studio/api.mjs` beside the remembered URL:
- **Per user.** Profiles live under `deployProfiles.v2:<login>` (`deployProfiles.v2:local` without sign-in); another user signing in to the same browser sees none of them. Not per org: a profile is a destination the user deploys to, not the org's data.
- **Stripped on save.** A profile's MCP URL — and its target URL (Grafana, kept for the profile's notes and never sent), by the same rule — is stored in its safe form (the rule of `tools/lib/mcp-url-safety.mjs`: no userinfo, fragment or credential-named parameter; nothing at all when what was typed is no URL). The deploy modal's status line says what was not kept, URL by URL, in the refresh panel's words, and for the MCP URL points at the auth field: `profile "Prod" saved · not kept in the profile: the "api_key" parameter of the MCP URL, which looks like a credential — put a token in the auth field instead`. The URL field keeps what was typed: that deploy still sends it.
- **The old key adopted once.** At boot, once the login is known — not on the unauthenticated boot of a signed-in deployment, which only redirects to sign-in — `deployProfiles.v1` is read, every URL stripped, the map written under the user's key and the old key removed — no saved profile lost, nothing the rule can name carried over (a secret in a URL's path, or under a name the rule does not know, stays — as for the remembered URL), and never read again. A name the user already has under the new key keeps the stored profile. Deleting a profile never writes an empty map over one that could not be read.
- **Cleared at sign-out**, with the remembered MCP URLs: every `deployProfiles.v2:*` key and the old key.
- **The rule's load is retried.** A failed `import('/lib/mcp-url-safety.mjs')` — a server mid-restart at boot — is not kept for the page's life: the next save loads it again, as the remembered URL's loader did per call.
- **Tests.** `server/test-authz.mjs`: the key (per user, not per org), the stripping on save (both URLs), the status text, the migration (what wins, garbage), the sign-out sweep against a storage stand-in, and in the studio source the modal's save path, the adoption's write and the boot-time adoption behind its sign-in guard.

### The layers are named by what sits on them
Users disliked the spec's abstract layer names and the questions beside them (*L1 Contract · What should we measure?*). Every screen now names a layer by its artefacts: **L1 SLI/SLO · L2 Metrics/Logs/Traces · L3 Dashboards/Recording Rules · L4 Alerts/Policy/Self-healing · L5 Self-check** (L2X *Extended* and GOV *Governance* are unchanged), with no question after the name. The spec's names stay one hover away on the Discover layer overview (*In the ObservabilityPack spec: L1 Contract*) and as `spec` on `LAYER_DEFS` in `studio/constants.mjs`, which is now the one place a layer's name lives: `LAYER_NAMES` feeds Discover, Compare, Conformance, Remediate, Verify, the atlases and the Build stack, replacing seven private copies. The arbor, transit and skyline atlases widen their label gutters for the longer names. Build's sheet questions (*What should we measure?* on the slab head) are unchanged, as are the Build section switch labelled *Validation* and the compiled Grafana dashboard's row title *§1-2 · Contract — SLIs and SLOs* (golden-gated).

### Discover: four views of an open layer
Discover's full rows say everything about an artefact and take a third of the screen's width each, so a 36-indicator layer ran to three screens. A **View** control beside *Show* and *Refine* now draws the same artefacts at four degrees of detail, least first:
- **List** — the name and one status mark, six across on a wide screen. Shape carries the meaning, building on the chips' dot and cross (a filled dot for live evidence, a hollow one for declared only, a dashed box for a template value, a cross for an unresolved reference), a legend above the rows names the marks on screen, and the kind, bound and status in words are each line's tooltip and its text for assistive technology.
- **Tiles** — the name, then its kind and bound, with the status chips in short form (*Live*, *Declared*).
- **Cards** — the card grid Discover drew before the UX review, back as a view: the ID and the adapter's source word (*Verified*, *Declared*, *Scaffold*) on each card's face, then the name, bound, summary, type and tags. It is the card the Build stack still draws (`artefactCardHtml`), with its title as the button that opens the record. The layer overview stays: one layer open at a time, where the old screen stacked them all.
- **Details** — the full row, unchanged, and the default.

Every view lists the same artefacts under the same filters, keeps the row's state (open in the detail panel, template value, unresolved reference, outside the filter) and opens the full record on a click. A lighter view draws more rows before *Show all N* (480, 180, 120 and 60). The choice is persisted (`state.discoverDetail`). `DISCOVER_VIEWS` and `artefactLightRowHtml` sit with the row in `studio/card-html.mjs`; `tools/test-discover-rows.mjs` covers them.

### The store, slice 3b: the identity API — users, orgs, members, the join role, sign out everywhere
STORE_PLAN slice 3, second of two PRs (after 3a, below). Owners and org admins manage identity over HTTP (`server/routes/identity.mjs`): every rule is `server/identity-admin.mjs`'s — the one `npm run users` and `npm run orgs` apply — called with `surface: 'api'`, which changes only the way out a refusal names (a route instead of a command); the CLIs' texts are unchanged. Each route is classified in `server/route-table.mjs` with the audit rows it writes, the caller's login their actor (`local` without sign-in) — except the first local user's owner grant (`owner.first-local-user`), which keeps `system`. See the README's The Identity API.
- **Owner routes** (`/api/admin/*`, whatever org the request is in): `GET` / `POST /api/admin/users` (a local user; the first local user while no enabled local owner exists becomes an owner where local users can sign in), `POST …/users/:id/disable`, `…/enable`, `…/password` (a temporary password: every session ends and the next sign-in must replace it, no skip; local users only, never the caller's own row), `…/signout` (sign out everywhere; an IdP user can sign in again at the IdP — disable to keep them out), `PUT …/users/:id/owner` (grant or revoke), `GET` / `POST /api/admin/orgs` (the creator is the new org's first admin; `adopt` takes over a non-empty `orgs/<id>/`), `DELETE /api/admin/orgs/:id` (soft; the files stay), `GET` / `PUT /api/admin/join-role` (`admin` only with `"confirm": true`; it backfills nobody). Paths name users by `users.id`.
- **Admin routes** (`/api/org*`, the request's org; no path names an org, so an org admin never reaches another one): `PATCH /api/org` (the name), `GET /api/org/members` (admins only: the list carries logins and emails), `POST /api/org/members` (an existing user by exact login or by the one enabled user whose sign-in verified that email — never a new row; a member already gets the role asked), `PATCH` and `DELETE /api/org/members/:userId`. The org's last enabled admin is demoted or removed only by an owner, on all three paths (the upsert included); a shell's `orgs -- add-member` / `remove-member` still can. To an org admin, no such user, a disabled one and several email matches are one 404; an owner gets the detail.
- **Sign out my other sessions.** `POST /auth/signout-others`, the account menu's new item (local and OIDC sign-in): the caller's own session only — never the pwflow cookie of a pending forced change, never the bearer (401) — with `X-Observogram-CSRF: 1`; every other cookie of the user is refused from its next request, and this browser's is re-issued at the new epoch. The re-issued cookie keeps the session's own expiry rather than getting a fresh TTL (the design re-issued it as a sign-in does): signing out elsewhere never extends a session, so it cannot keep one alive past the moment the IdP stops letting its user in.
- **Identity changes need the CSRF header in every posture.** Every identity-API mutation and `POST /auth/signout-others` answer 403 `csrf` without `X-Observogram-CSRF: 1` — from a session, and also from `local` on a server without sign-in; the bearer is exempt, and the identity API refuses it anyway (it is an operator: 403 `role`).
- **The open postures.** Without sign-in the caller is `local`, an owner. On a loopback server the identity API answers only a request sent straight to a loopback address (`Host` `localhost`, `127.x.x.x` or `[::1]`; no `Forwarded`, `Via`, `X-Forwarded-*`, `X-Real-IP` or client-IP header such as `CF-Connecting-IP` or `True-Client-IP`; an `Origin`, if any, naming that host), else 403 `posture` naming the CLIs — a DNS-rebinding page or a proxy cannot plant an owner that outlives the posture. Beyond loopback (`OBSERVOGRAM_INSECURE_NO_AUTH=1`) it is closed (403 `posture`). A direct request to `POST /api/admin/orgs` answers 409 in both — a second org needs identity; under `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off` the text says to restart without it. Every existing route is unchanged there.
- **Refusals by kind.** A rule's refusal is 400 (bad input), 404 (no such user, org or member) or 409 (the state forbids it), naming a way out that works; 403 stays an authorization denial, with `denied`.
- **Reserved logins.** `system`, `cli`, `local` and `token` — actors the audit log writes — are refused as a new local login, by the API and by `npm run users -- add`; the API also refuses the configured `OBSERVOGRAM_API_TOKEN_LABEL`. Existing rows are untouched; the token's label must not be a user's login.
- **`owner.grant` / `owner.revoke`, and the grant's `from`.** Granting owner over the API writes `owner.grant` (an owner and an admin of the default org, as `npm run users -- owner` does); revoking writes its twin `owner.revoke` and touches no membership — the answer's `memberships` and `note` say when the user is still the default org's admin. Every grant row (`owner.grant`, `owner.bootstrap`, `owner.first-local-user`) now records `from`, the default org's role before it (`null` when the membership was added). The last enabled owner, and the last owner who can sign in under the server's mode, can be neither disabled nor demoted.
- **The join-role boot warning** (`OBSERVOGRAM_OIDC_JOIN_ROLE is read at the first start only; the store records X`) now ends `— an owner changes it with PUT /api/admin/join-role`.
- **Tests.** `server/test-identity-api.mjs` (new): every identity route's success with its exact response and audit rows, every refusal with its status and text and no row, revocation (sign out everywhere, sign out my other sessions, the owner's reset), the last owner and the last admin on every path, what a refusal reveals, reserved logins, the owner round trip, the join role's `confirm`. `server/test-authz.mjs`: the identity-API rows of the AuthZ matrix (an owner with no membership reaches the owner routes; an org admin never another org's members), the direct-loopback cells over `node:http`, the open-loopback identity writes and their rows, Arming (the API half), the self rows, and the README's API Surface and Identity API tables against the route table. Additions in test-store (every rule on both surfaces), test-tenancy, test-auth-oidc (add a member by a verified email; sign out my other sessions), test-store-boot and test-store-import (the warning).

### The store, slice 3a: roles enforced on every route, the live pack per org
STORE_PLAN slice 3, first of two PRs (the identity API — users, orgs, members, the join role, sign-out everywhere over HTTP — is 3b). Every route the server registers is classified in `server/route-table.mjs` and registers `authorize('<METHOD> <path>')` (`server/authz.mjs`) as its first handler, per method; an unclassified route fails the server's import, and `server/test-authz.mjs` fails on an unguarded, unprobed or reclassified one. Roles are managed from a shell, as before (`npm run orgs -- add-member <org> <login> --role …`, `npm run users -- …`), and from 3b over HTTP too.
- **Members mapped to `viewer` lose write power.** A `viewer` (`viewer`, `read`, `readonly`, `read-only` in an imported `orgs.json`, or added `--role viewer`) keeps every read and gets a 403 on every other `/api` request: `requires the operator role in org 'X' (you are viewer) — ask an admin of X`. In the studio that is Scan (repo and GitHub), Draft from MCP and the MCP panel's live refresh, dropping or uploading a pack file (even only to view it: the upload registers it), Build (its preview computes on the server, and Save registers), Compare's retrofeed, journey Capture and Run, Deploy / Verify / Rollback, and RESET. Nobody else loses a power: every existing mutation is `operator` (`member` and unknown roles were imported as `operator`; an OIDC deployment's join role is `operator`). The import reports' viewer lines now say "read-only: roles are enforced".
- **Who is what.** An owner acts as `admin` in every org; the bearer (`OBSERVOGRAM_API_TOKEN`) acts as an `operator` of its org and never reaches an admin or owner route; token-only anonymous callers are viewers (their writes still answer 401); the open postures (`OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off`, `OBSERVOGRAM_INSECURE_NO_AUTH=1`) are `local`, an owner — every route, as before. The role is the membership of the request's org (`X-Observogram-Org`, `?org=`), never the first one.
- **Every refusal says why.** The bodies of the auth gate's, the org middleware's and the guard's refusals gain `denied` (`auth`, `csrf`, `org`, `role`, `posture`); role refusals also carry `need`, `role`, `owner` and `org`. Statuses and the existing texts are unchanged.
- **`effectiveRole`.** `GET /api/orgs` and `/auth/me`'s `orgs` keep `role` (the membership's; `null` without one; `service-account` for the bearer) and add `effectiveRole`, the role the guard applies there; an owner's `GET /api/orgs` also lists its active org when it is no member of it.
- **Cross-site form posts.** `POST /auth/login`, `POST /auth/change-password` and `…/skip` refuse a post the browser marks `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site` or `same-site` (a sibling subdomain): 403, naming the page to use. Clients that send no `Sec-Fetch-Site` (curl, scripts) are unaffected.
- **The live pack moves, per org.** The studio's live pack is `<org root>/live/production-live.pack.yaml` (the default org at `.` → `<workspace>/live/…`), written atomically by the MCP panel's refresh (`POST /api/refresh-live`) and read by `GET /api/live-status` for that org only — before, one file in the install that any operator of any org overwrote and every org read. The studio no longer reads `examples/production-live.pack.yaml`, which is not moved (it belonged to no org and may carry a credential), is still `npm run fetch-live`'s default output and still `npm run detect-drift`'s and the dry run's input; a boot line says so until the default org's first refresh. Each org's badge reads absent until its first refresh. `OUTPUT=<org root>/live/production-live.pack.yaml npm run fetch-live` feeds an org's badge from the CLI. On k8s the live pack is now on the workspace volume (it persists).
- **The MCP URL keeps no credential parameter.** Userinfo, the fragment and query parameters named like credentials (a word of the name — split at camelCase, digits and separators — or the whole name run together, in any case, that is or ends in a credential word: `token(s)`, `key(s)`, `secret(s)`, `pass`, `passwd`, `password`, `passphrase`, `passcode`, `pwd`, `auth`, `authorization`, `sig`, `signature`, `credential(s)`, `jwt`, `session`, `sessionid`, `sessid`, `cookie`, `bearer`, `sas`. So `api_key`, `apiKey`, `X-Amz-Signature`, `access_token`, `sessionId`, `token1`, `apitoken`, `APISECRET`, `appkey`, `DBPASS`, `basicauth`, `PHPSESSID` go, and `sortkey` with them; not `signal`, `design`, `author`, `keyspace`, nor a name ending in an ordinary word that merely ends in one: `bypass`, `compass`, `overpass`, `surpass`, `monkey`, `donkey`, `turkey`, `hockey`, `jockey`, `whiskey`, `hotkey`, `obsession`), or smuggled as `;`-separated pairs, are never kept in the live pack, a draft (`POST /api/draft-from-mcp`: `summary.mcpUrl`, the registered pack's `mcp.url`), `npm run fetch-live`'s output, `deploys.jsonl` or a log line; the refresh answers a `note` and a draft a `summary.warnings` entry naming what was dropped. Put a token in the auth field (`mcpAuth`, `MCP_AUTH`) — never in the URL's path, which no name rule can see. A journey captured from such a draft re-drafts from the safe URL: an MCP that accepts a credential only in the query needs `packB.mcp.authEnv` (a header token). `GET /api/live-status` serves `origin` to every reader and the safe `url` to an operator and above only — a viewer (and in the token posture any anonymous caller) never sees a path.
- **The remembered MCP URL** is per user and per org (`localStorage` `mcpUrl.v2:<login>:<org>`), stored without credential parameters and cleared at sign-out; the unscoped `mcpUrl` key is no longer read. The prefill stays remembered > the server's URL for this org > empty.
- **Studio fixes.** Every `/api` call and link carries the CSRF header and the active org — RESET cleared the browser but not the server's registry in the identity posture (silently), and the compile, OTLP and schema views and the export, download and `api` links read the user's first org. A denied upload or adopt showed a `TypeError`; RESET sends its `DELETE` first and, when refused, shows the server's text and clears nothing.
- **Tests.** `server/test-authz.mjs` (new): completeness per method over three modes, the independent classification, the decision over synthetic entries, and the AuthZ matrix — every `/api` route × every principal (13 in the identity posture, plus the `?org=`, `X-Tomograph-Org` and `X-Tomograph-CSRF` vectors) × every posture (identity, token, open on loopback, open exposed twice), refused requests writing no audit row, the CSRF, form, public, case and org-list rows and the fresh `admin`/`admin` flow; ports in test-smoke, test-auth-oidc, test-auth-local, test-tenancy (the live routes are org-scoped), test-store-import, test-store-ops (`live/` moves with the default org in an in-place export), test-workspace and test-mcp-url.

### Fixed: routes match case-sensitively — `/API/…` no longer slipped past the auth gate (#117)
The auth gate and the org middleware tested the request path as written (`/api/`), but Express matched routes case-insensitively, and the nested deploy router did not inherit the app's setting. Any other spelling of the prefix (`/API/…`, `/Api/…`) reached the handlers with no session check, no CSRF check, no org context and, in the token-only posture, no bearer check — anonymously: the live pack read and overwritten, `draft-from-mcp` and `crawl-github` fetching, the library and the deploy matrix read, the deploy POSTs run up to their pack lookup (on `v0.4.0`, the pack registry listed, registered to and wiped). Routing is now case-sensitive in the app and in every router (`express.Router({ caseSensitive: true })`): `/API/…` answers the studio's HTML shell (a GET) or 404.

### The studio UX review: every screen leads with context, decision and one next action
The team's review of the studio found a strong model behind screens that asked people to learn it before they could finish a task. Every working screen now follows one order — context (service, environment, pack and version; both sides named where packs are compared), a one-sentence decision, one primary action, a few explained measures, then the detail — through a shared grammar in `studio/ux-kit.mjs` and `studio/ux.css`, written up in `docs/UX_SCREEN_GRAMMAR.md`.
- **One journey at a time.** Home asks *What would you like to do?* with two equal choices; the Discover → Diagnose → Remediate stepper appears only once a journey is chosen. *Check* opens the user's services (environment, packs, tier, when last opened here, the one issue worth knowing), a search and the import sources. A greeting uses a person's name, never a role such as *Admin*.
- **A working context bar.** The pinned header keeps service, pack and environment (the baseline only on the screens that compare); upload, scan, draft and export sit in one **Actions** menu; the MCP connection, the catalogue API, reset and theme move under **Advanced**. Build carries no toolbar. Tabs read *How reliable is this pack?* and *Resolve gaps*.
- **One status vocabulary.** Origin, completion, evidence and assessment are four separate properties, each with its own chip shape; *VERIFIED*, *scaffold*, *pass on placeholder* and *conformant* are no longer interchangeable badges. Plain words come first, the formal term on hover (*Update repository from live* (retrofeed), *Template value needs completion* (scaffold), *Requirement represented; real value still needed*, *Meets tier rubric*).
- **Verify** asks *What is ready, and what remains?* and shows schema valid, meets tier rubric, implementation and deployment ready independently, under a verdict such as *Ready for team completion; not ready for deployment* — meeting the rubric never masks placeholders. It hands off with **Open pack in Discover**. **Compile** asks *What did the pack produce?* (these two questions replace the step copy in the entry below) and leads with the result and a needs-review queue. **Define** is four substeps (service, criticality with each tier's consequences, technology with *Adds N suggested SLIs*, review suggestions). The **SLI editor** opens on a sentence in real units, groups its fields, validates relationships as the user types and ends on **Save SLI** with *Include in this pack*.
- **Discover** opens on a layer overview, one layer at a time, with task filters (needs attention, missing evidence, template values, live evidence, all). **Assessment** (was *Diagnostic Grade*) leads with the result, its causes and *Review N required gaps*, the ladder and formula folded into *How the grade is calculated*; **Compare** opens on a summary that separates structural difference from quality gap. **Remediate** recommends a strategy named by its effect with source → destination, and deploy gets a review panel. **Neuron** leads with what the latest check found and keeps not run, unable to observe, check failed and notification failed apart. **Conformance** states the governing result; **Traceability** is a worst-first requirement list where a link is proven only when a verification stamp confirms it.
- **Readability.** The type floor rises (9–13px → 11.5–14px), muted text meets WCAG 2.2 AA contrast, and progress is announced through one `#ux-status` live region (the main view is no longer an `aria-live` region).
- **Fixed on the way.** Bulk deploy sent selected rows a type filter hid; it now deploys exactly what the review shows.
- **`GET /api/packs/:id/conformance` carries `onPlaceholder`** for a pack with `library.todo.*` annotations (from `validationSummary`, for the requested environment), as `/api/validate` and `/api/library/register` already did — Conformance lists the clauses that pass only on a placeholder across reloads instead of hedging; the key is omitted, never `[]`, when the list is not known.
- **Recording-rule inference names both rules.** A good/total SLI inferred by the crawler (`tools/lib/sli-inference.mjs`) now reads *Inferred from recording rules `<svc>:<metric>:good_5m` and `<svc>:<metric>:total_5m`.* instead of the `…:good/total` shorthand; Discover links both rules and still resolves the shorthand in packs crawled before. No golden changed (none holds a good/total pair).
- **Removed** the Remediate compiler's unreachable inline deploy panel and Discover's never-produced *Missing* artefact paths (what a pack lacks is a failing rubric clause, which Conformance and Assessment report).
- **Tests.** `tools/test-ux-kit.mjs`, `tools/test-discover-rows.mjs`, and additions to the build, neuron, traceability, library and verify-deploy suites.

### The Build journey's step copy
The maintainer's copy for the three steps of building a pack. The header tabs (`BUILD_TABS` in `studio/build-model.mjs`) and each step's heading now read:

| Step | Question | Supporting text (the tab's tagline) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Define | What are we building for? | Choose a service, tier, and starting point |
| Compile | What will the pack include? | Build SLIs, alerts, dashboards, and checks |
| Verify | Can we use this pack? | Review coverage and resolve gaps |

Before, they read *What are we observing? · Service, tier & library*, *What should we watch? · Pack & deployable artifacts* and *Is it ready to use? · Conformance & placeholders*. The tab labels are in title case with articles and short prepositions lowercase, as the previous Build labels were (*Is It Ready to Use?*): *What Will the Pack Include?*. A tab's accessible name and title follow the tagline (*Define — Choose a service, tier, and starting point*). The README and `docs/BUILD_JOURNEY.md` follow. Only copy changed; the ledes under the headings are unchanged.

### The store, slice 2b: the offline operations — export, import --replace, rekey-issuer, purge-org
STORE_PLAN slice 2, second of two PRs (after 2a, below). It gives the store build a clean rollback and a way back: `packc store export`, `packc store import --replace`, `packc store rekey-issuer` and `packc store purge-org` (`server/store/ops.mjs`; the replace itself is boot step 3, `planReplace` / `applyReplace` in `server/store/import.mjs`). Every one runs with the server stopped — `assertNotInUse`, restore's probe, now shared, refuses while anything holds the database — except a directory export, which only reads. Each prints `store: <path>` first, refuses `:memory:` and a missing database (none creates one), and prints one line and exits 1 when refused. Slice 2 is complete once 2a and 2b merge; `develop` is held from promotion until then.
- **Roll back only after `packc store export`.** `packc store export <dir>` writes the `users.json` / `orgs.json` a pre-store build boots on: the enabled local users with their password records (only once stand-alone sign-in is armed; OIDC users never), and an `orgs.json` when the deployment had one, has more than one live org, or keeps the default org off the workspace root — live orgs, enabled members (OIDC ones by `sub`, under the recorded issuer), `operator` written as `member`. The report lists every viewer and operator (a pre-store build enforces no roles: they regain full write), each org an owner is no member of (a pre-store build has no owners: the owner loses it) and the cookie note (users revoked in the store stay signed in on a pre-store build until their cookies expire; rotating `OBSERVOGRAM_SESSION_SECRET` signs everyone out). **In place** (`<dir>` = the workspace, a store the server has started) it writes to the recorded users file and the workspace, records what it wrote (`legacy_hashes`, the marker `by 'export'`, one `store.export` row), so a store build starts on those files without refusing; a `users.json` / `orgs.json` it would overwrite that differs from those hashes (a pre-store build's edits, as on a second rollback) is refused naming `import --replace`, never overwritten. When it writes `orgs.json` while the default org is at the workspace root it makes the pre-store build's move itself — every check first (an existing `orgs/default/<entry>` refuses, naming each), then the renames, the default org's journey `file:` paths rewritten, the files, and the root `orgs/default` (`org.root`) in one `tx()`; it prints the CronJob change, and a failure part-way puts everything back and says so. An in-place export proceeds only into a workspace that is provably this store's: its marker names this store, or there is no marker, this store's database lives inside the workspace and no `*.db` directly in the workspace or its `db/` (each opened read-only, never migrated) holds another store (with the database outside the workspace, as in k8s, a missing marker refuses: one start on the workspace rewrites it; a workspace that does not exist refuses too) — another store's marker or database is refused naming both stores and the command that exports that workspace's own store, an unreadable `*.db` is refused naming it, and a corrupt marker is refused naming it with the way out (with the server stopped, move it aside, then export again). A directory export writes only into a directory that does not exist or is empty (a symlink is followed; what it names must be absent or empty): anything else is refused, naming the two ways out — an empty or new directory, or, for this store's own workspace, `OBSERVOGRAM_WORKSPACE=<it> packc store export <it>` with the server stopped; a directory inside the workspace or holding it is refused naming the in-place export. A replace that cannot remove an empty leftover after its commit warns once, naming it, and the start goes on.
- **Re-upgrade with `packc store import --replace`.** After a pre-store build changed users or orgs, the store build refuses to start (step 2 (d)); the refusal now also names `packc store import --replace`. The command records a request (`replace_requested`, only for the store the marker names, already imported; `packc store import` alone is a usage error), and the next start re-imports the files as they stand with the unit's own environment and prints a `[store] replaced from …` report: a local user absent from the users file is disabled, one in it updated and re-enabled, a new one created; an OIDC user is never disabled for being absent; the owner flag is never re-derived; orgs are created, renamed and removed (never the default) to match `orgs.json`, memberships follow the file exactly (a user disabled before and after keeps theirs, since the export leaves disabled users out of `orgs.json`), a removed org that reappears is skipped as a conflict; a file that is absent keeps what the store has. Every changed or disabled user's epoch is bumped (their cookies end). After a pre-store `npm run orgs -- create` and restart on a flat deployment, the replace moves the default org's root to `orgs/default` with that build's migration (journey paths rewritten, empty pre-store leftovers removed, the CronJob line printed); its memberships follow the file, whose `default` entry has none, so only owners reach it until members are added. It refuses, moving nothing and leaving the request pending, when a flat entry with data sits beside its `orgs/default/` twin, a rewritten journey would not parse, or no enabled owner who can sign in under the unit's mode would be left (and the store had one). A request made for another store, or with the marker missing, refuses at step 2 (c) with its ways out.
- **`packc store rekey-issuer --to <issuer> | --clear`.** The step-2 (b) refusal of a changed OIDC issuer now names both. `--to` (the same IdP at a new URL) rewrites every OIDC login to the new key, `oidc_issuer` and an `oidc:` `identity_mode` with it, refused when a rewritten login exists: users keep rows, roles, memberships and the owner flag; store sessions signed in under the old key end, and a fresh sign-in finds the same row. `--clear` (another IdP) disables every enabled OIDC user with an epoch bump — the owner rows keep their flag, disabled — and clears the issuer and an `oidc:` `identity_mode`; set `OBSERVOGRAM_BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN` before the next start to name the owner. One `issuer.rekey` row each; earlier audit rows and `deploys.jsonl` keep the old logins; a pending replace stays pending and runs under the new key.
- **`packc store purge-org <id>`** deletes the files of an org removed with `npm run orgs -- remove` (which now prints the command): only a removed, non-default org whose root is `orgs/<id>`; a symlinked root, a root outside `<workspace>/orgs` and a workspace whose marker names another store are refused. It drops that root's `legacy_hashes` keys, writes one `org.purge` row and rewrites the marker (`by 'purge-org'`); the org row stays, so its id is never reused. It cannot be undone and asks for no confirmation: the org's packs, `deploys.jsonl`, `snapshots/`, `runs/` and `journeys/` go, and no `store backup` holds them, so the docs say to copy the directory first.
- **`packc store restore` warns** on stderr, exit 0, when the workspace's `.store-imported` names another store than the one restored (or cannot be read): the next start refuses until they agree.
- **Docs.** README: the Security Posture's refusal bullet, and new sections Upgrade And Roll Back (the upgrade, the clean rollback, the re-upgrade and the replace's rules), Stale Import (the step-2 refusals and their ways out), Move The OIDC Issuer, Purge A Removed Org; `deploy/k8s/README.md` (the export from a one-off pod with the studio at 0, the image change, forward again with `import --replace`, `rekey-issuer` for an IdP move); the build status in `docs/STORE_PLAN.md`; `docs/HANDOVER.md`.
- **Tests.** `server/test-store-ops.mjs` (the Export gate through `server/fixtures/pre-store-build.mjs`, a frozen copy of the pre-store semantics; the Stale-import round trips export → pre-store edits → refuse → replace; rekey-issuer; purge-org; the restore warning; the CLI), and `tools/test-store-prestore-live.mjs` (`npm run test:store:prestore[:strict]`; the CI job `store-prestore` boots the real `v0.4.0` build over HTTP on exported workspaces). The step-2 (b) and (d) assertions in `server/test-store-import.mjs` / `server/test-store-boot.mjs` follow their new texts.

### The store, slice 2a: identity on the store — the boot order, the import, revocable sessions, tenancy always on
STORE_PLAN slice 2, first of two PRs. Users, orgs, memberships and the owner move from `users.json` / `orgs.json` into the embedded store (`observogram.db`), which the server now opens at every start (`server/boot.mjs`). The offline operations — `packc store export`, `import --replace`, `rekey-issuer`, `purge-org` — are the second PR (2b, above); `develop` is held from promotion until 2b merges, because `export` is the rollback path. Behaviour changes:
- **The import.** The first start of a store build imports `users.json` and `orgs.json` once, prints a `[store]` report on the boot log (also stored as `import_report`) and leaves both files in place, hashed (`legacy_hashes`, the `.store-imported` marker) and never read again. Names and fields the store cannot hold are dropped and listed in the report — never a refused start. `orgs.json` roles are mapped: `admin` / `owner` → `admin`, `viewer` / `read` / `readonly` / `read-only` → `viewer`, anything else (`member`, empty) → `operator`; the members mapped to `viewer` lose write power once roles are enforced (Stage 3). Data under `orgs/default/` with no `default` entry in `orgs.json` (a retired default org, or a migration that stopped before its `orgs.json` write) is never made an org: the report and every boot name it as left behind, with the ways out — with the server stopped, move its entries into the default org's root, or move the directory aside.
- **Edited legacy files refuse the start.** A `users.json` or `orgs.json` changed after the import (a pre-store build during a rollback, config management) refuses the next start with the imported SHA-256 and the ways out: put the file back, or move it aside and make the change with `npm run users` / `npm run orgs`. A marker that names another store refuses too; a vanished marker or file is repaired and logged.
- **Revocable sessions.** Each user row carries a session epoch: a password change or a disable signs that user out everywhere. Cookies issued before the upgrade stay valid. A disabled row cannot sign in.
- **Tenancy is always on.** Every `/api` request runs in an org and the response echoes it in `X-Observogram-Org`; a flat workspace is the default org, at the workspace root, and a created org's root is `orgs/<id>`, fixed at creation. The fixed flat-workspace migration (a legacy `orgs.json` deployment) never moves an empty directory and refuses a corrupt `orgs.json` naming its path instead of replacing it.
- **The header ORG chip** shows for a user in more than one org, or whose only org is not the default one (`orgChipModel` in `studio/api.mjs`, both `studio/app.mjs` sites). A user whose only org is the default org — including an `orgs.json` deployment's default org — no longer sees the static label.
- **One org without identity.** A one-org `orgs.json` with only a bearer (`OBSERVOGRAM_API_TOKEN`) now boots token-only, and a one-org `orgs.json` with neither a bearer nor identity now boots like a fresh install — on loopback it seeds `admin`/`admin`. Both refused before; two or more orgs without identity still refuse.
- **OIDC.** Users are recorded as `<issuerKey>#<sub>` (the issuer canonicalised: a bare origin's slash, the well-known suffix and a fragment folded; a path's trailing slash is kept, so `…/v2.0` and `…/v2.0/` are different keys); an OIDC deployment without `orgs.json` records `oidc_join_role = operator`, so every IdP user keeps today's access — set `OBSERVOGRAM_OIDC_JOIN_ROLE=none` before the first start to start closed; it is read at the first start only. `OBSERVOGRAM_BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN` (`<issuer>#<sub>`, or an email that counts only with `email_verified: true`) names the first owner, or `npm run users -- owner <login>` later. A store whose OIDC users were recorded under another issuer key refuses the start and names the spelling to set back.
- **The CLIs write the store.** `npm run users` and `npm run orgs` are thin entry points over `server/identity-admin.mjs`: `--role member` is refused (the roles are `viewer`, `operator`, `admin`; `member` is now `operator`), `users -- remove` disables instead of deleting (the audit references the row) and the new `users -- enable` undoes it (a still-seeded `admin` / `admin` row needs `passwd` first, and even disabled it refuses a start beyond loopback, naming `users -- passwd <login>` as the way out), the first local user becomes the owner (not while the server runs OIDC, where it is created without owner and the CLI says why) and arms stand-alone sign-in, which stays armed once armed (removing users never reopens a server; only `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off` does); `users -- remove` keeps the last owner who can sign in under the server's mode — the issuer a shell's `OBSERVOGRAM_OIDC_ISSUER` names, else the mode the server's last start recorded (`identity_mode`, rewritten by a start whose mode changed once it listens — a start that fails to bind records nothing; on a store no start of this build booted, its local owners and those under the issuer it records, and no A-16 owner while it records an issuer); and `orgs -- remove` is soft (its row and files stay).
- **Journeys.** Over the API a journey loads by name only, and a studio-run journey never reads another org's part of the workspace — a `crawl:` root, a `file:` pack or an inventory site there is refused. The schedule snippets are per org: the k8s form points `OBSERVOGRAM_WORKSPACE` at the org's root and labels the CronJob with the org; the component CronJob's comment says one CronJob per org (`/workspace/<orgs.root>`), pinned by `tools/test-deploy-manifests.mjs`.
- **Docs.** README Security Posture and store section, `.env.example` (`OBSERVOGRAM_USERS_FILE` is an import source only; `OBSERVOGRAM_BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN`, `OBSERVOGRAM_OIDC_JOIN_ROLE`; tenancy has no knob), `deploy/k8s/README.md` (the first start imports; the `store restore` recipe from a one-off pod with the studio at 0; the per-org CronJob), plan statuses in `docs/PRODUCTIZATION_PLAN.md`.
- **`:memory:` over flat data.** An `OBSERVOGRAM_DB=:memory:` start over an `orgs.json` and a not-yet-moved flat workspace moves nothing and plans the default org at `.` (an `orgs.json` `default` entry too), so it serves the flat data in place; the report line says so, and no "left behind … merge it by hand" warning names the base. Over a half-migrated workspace (`orgs/default` already holds data) it plans the default org at `orgs/default`, as a file store does, instead of refusing with check E; the unmoved flat entries are named as left behind until a file-store start finishes the move.
- **Tests.** `server/test-store-import.mjs` (readers, hashes, marker, migration, the Import gate, the seed and check tables, `bootStore()` in-process) and `server/test-store-boot.mjs` (the Boot order gate through `start()` in child processes, stale import, the CLIs, arming, removed orgs, per-boot warnings, OIDC boot switches, `:memory:`); test-auth-local, test-tenancy (with a sweep that fails on an unclassified `/api` route), test-auth-oidc, test-smoke and test-workspace ported; every suite that imports server code clears the store's boot variables first. Guards: only the boot, the migration, the store's import / CLI modules and the suites import `server/store/legacy-files.mjs`; no `tools/lib` module imports from `server/`.

### Spec 1.3: `good_when` — a threshold SLI's bound has a direction
The maintainer's request of 2026-09-23: the MQ lab could not say that fewer connected channels or running listeners is bad — a floor had to be encoded as a ratio SLI or as an inverted "headroom" ceiling — and the burn-rate generator could only warn that a ratio-shaped threshold "suggests a floor, which the spec cannot express". RFC-0002 (upstream MoebiusX/otel-observability-pack, accepted the same day, merged on develop as 98be4ae) adds one optional field to `threshold` and `distribution` SLIs, `good_when: below | above`, default `below` — today's meaning: a sample is good at or below the bound — with `above` a floor; the schema refuses the field on `ratio` and `custom` SLIs through a `not` sub-schema, the form this repo's validator enforces. Absent means below, so every 1.2 pack validates and means what it meant. Observogram adopts it end to end:
- **Vendor.** `vendor/observability-pack-spec/v1.3/` holds the four upstream files byte for byte (each sha256 is the git blob's), `VERSIONS.json` records spec 1.3, ref `develop`, commit `98be4ae8…`; `v1.2/` is removed (1.3 validates every 1.2 pack unchanged; git history keeps it). `tools/sync-spec.mjs` reads the version directory from the fetched spec's own header (`| Spec version | 1.3 |`), fetches bodies untrimmed and defaults to the upstream default branch, develop. The studio’s links into the upstream repo (the footer’s “spec v1.3”, the Schema view’s schema and spec, the Conformance view’s maturity rubric) open that commit, not `main` — upstream main still serves 1.2 — and `tools/test-validator.mjs` holds them to `VERSIONS.json` `upstream.commit`.
- **Version.** `SPEC_VERSION = '1.3'` in `tools/lib/validator.mjs`, with `SPEC_DIR` and `SPEC_SCHEMA_PATH` beside it; every path that spelled `v1.2` (journey, crawler, gen-site, upconvert, the server's example catalogue, /healthz, a dozen tests), the compile banners, the CLI help, the studio's header, footer and texts, `package.json`, CI and the docs that state the current version follow the constant. `tools/test-validator.mjs` (new) pins the constants against the manifest and the one directory on disk, the 1.3 schema's acceptance of `good_when` on threshold and distribution SLIs and its refusal on ratio and custom ones (`$.spec.slis[N].good_when: matches forbidden 'not' schema`) and of a bad value, every 1.2-shaped pack of the repo validating unchanged, and a drift scan that fails on any other spec version in code, studio, workflows or current-version docs.
- **One helper.** `tools/lib/good-when.mjs`: `goodWhen(sli)` (absent means below — the one place that says so), `badComparator`, `boundGlyph`, `boundText` (`≤ 0.5 seconds`, `≥ 2 consumers`); every reader asks it, never the raw field. The studio cannot import `tools/lib`, so `studio/sli-direction.mjs` re-spells it and a test holds the two together input for input.
- **Burn-rate generator.** A floor's legs count the samples UNDER the bound — `sum_over_time((max(series) < bool t)[w:step])` in the error-ratio record, both burn legs and the two-bad-samples floor — a ceiling keeps `> bool t`; the comparison stays strict either way, so the bound itself is good. "Non-negative upper bound" became "a finite number" (a floor at −1 is a bound); the floor guess ("unit ratio suggests a floor the spec cannot express") became "looks like a floor — declare good_when: above (or good_when: below to state the ceiling)" and is raised only while the SLI declares no direction. Distribution SLIs keep no error-ratio form. `tools/lib/sli-inference.mjs` reads `< bool` back as `good_when: above`. compile.mjs builds no leg itself.
- **Boards.** A threshold tile colours `okAbove` (higher is worse) for a ceiling, `okBelow` (amber under the bound, red under half of it) for a floor (`thresholdSteps`); the dashed line sits at the bound either way; the descriptions name the side: `Good when ≤ 0.5 seconds.`, `The dashed line is the threshold — good when ≥ 2 consumers.` The reference boards and the dashboard goldens moved by those description strings alone. A bound of 0 has no amber band: the two steps coincided and Grafana (last step ≤ the sample) painted the good 0 of a ceiling red under a description that says `Good when ≤ 0 messages` — a ceiling at 0 is now green through 0 and red from `Number.MIN_VALUE` (5e-324 in the JSON), a floor at 0 red under 0 and green from 0; the only 1.2 tile this moves is a 0-bound one (the library's `dlq_depth`).
- **Cards.** The adapter gives a threshold or distribution SLI a `subtitle` — its bound with its glyph — which Discover's layers view and the Build stack draw under the title (`.card-sub`); the Discover drawer prints `bound ≤ 0.5 seconds` and `good when below`.
- **Library engine and CLI.** A template may declare `good_when` (threshold only); the index rows carry it normalised; instantiation copies the declaration — the override's, else the template's — and never synthesises one (a pack that says nothing stays 1.2-shaped byte for byte); `good_when` joins `OVERRIDE_FIELDS` and `CUSTOM_FIELDS`, refused on a ratio SLI and for any value but the two sides; the retired `comparison` now says "the direction of a threshold is good_when …, spec 1.3 — use good_when"; `packc init --override <sli>.good_when=above`. `DEFAULT_BURN_PROFILE` is unchanged.
- **The editor.** The Bound row gains a two-segment *good when: below · above* control in the tier control's idiom (`role=radiogroup`, arrow keys, a sliding thumb, focus keys per segment, `↺ library default` when overridden), for threshold SLIs in edit and create modes, committing live like every field — picking the library's own side clears the override; the Bound hint now reads "the bound in the SLI's unit; good when below (a ceiling: latency, lag) or above (a floor: replicas, consumers)"; the read-only face prints the side; the rolodex card, the DEFINE candidate ghost and the stack card print `≤` / `≥`; the create dialog's type hint names both sides. The stylesheet's rules use the tokens only and sit in the block the AA scan reads.
- **Conformance.** `L1.MUST.latency_slo` counts threshold and distribution SLIs by type; a floor satisfies it, as the upstream maturity model says; no rubric change (`docs/CONFORMANCE.md`).
- **Compatibility, measured.** A 1.2 pack compiled to Prometheus rules and to its unified board at the base commit and at HEAD differs in the banner comment line (`compiled from ObservabilityPack v1.2` → `v1.3`) and in the tile / trend descriptions that gained the glyph, and in nothing else; the compiled rules of every 1.2 fixture are byte-identical below the banner. The MQ lab (mq-observability-pack) re-vendors the lib next and can then express its own floors directly.

### The editor — one pop-up over an SLI and its SLO; the stack's cards open it; the sheet is live on Define
The maintainer, trying the copies of PR #104 on a test server: *the functionality is not there, I can't update an SLI. This looks half baked … updating should be easier — one has to scroll up and down to modify something that, if it's just 5 variables, maybe a pop-up would do.* The in-card Customise face and the '+ Custom SLI' form card are gone; one design replaces them (`docs/BUILD_JOURNEY.md` "The editor"):
- **One pop-up editor** (`studio/build-editor-view.mjs` over the pure `sliEditorModel` in `studio/build-copies-model.mjs`; `buildEditorModel` assembles it): a centered modal dialog — `role=dialog aria-modal=true` (the Tab trap covers it and leaves the non-modal sheet underneath alone), labelled by the SLI id, a scrim over everything, Esc leaves the field and closes, focus on the first field on open and back on the opener on close, one at a time, UI state only (`build.editor`). A title row (the id large, the product and its evidence, the type pill, the *customised* / *custom* / *from the tier-1 profile* / *was <key>* chips); a two-column grid — Id · Description (prefilled with the library default), Objective · Window, Bound · Unit for a threshold SLI, Metric · Type (fixed: a different shape is a new custom SLI) — then the PromQL as two-row textareas that grow, showing the RESOLVED expression (the parameters in, read from the instantiated pack; never `${…}`) with the line *parameters: job=orders-api, duration_metric=… — edit them in L2*; the evidence line; a status line (*applying…* → *applied · SLO availability_99_9 · 2 burn alerts · rule …*, the engine's error — also under its field — *not in the pack*, *as compiled* on Verify); per-field *↺ library default*, *Reset all*, *Done*, the SLI's add / remove switch in the footer. No scrolling at 1920×1080 or 1366×768 for a library SLI. Create mode is the same dialog over the custom form (Name → id, Type, the fields per type, *Add to the pack* from `canSubmit`; a success morphs it into edit mode over the new SLI); on Verify the same dialog opens read-only (values, provenance, no input).
- **Live apply**: every field commits on input (`setOverride` / `updateCustom` with `live: true` — the debounced instantiate, a keystroke is never a request of its own); the editor lives in a persistent host outside the re-rendered view; the same editor already mounted is redrawn in place (the dialog node, the scrim and the status live region stay, so the *applied · …* answer is a text change assistive technology announces, and the entrance never replays) and its render keeps the focused control's text, focus and caret across the pack's answer — three quick characters lose none, a value the engine rejects stays as typed beside its message; a field is found by its key, else by its name when a rename changed the key under it (a custom SLI's `cu:<id>:` keys), the footer controls by their own keys (`editor:done` …), and Esc closes the editor from `<body>` too (one document listener per host). The id field is the exception — a rename moves the SLO, the rule, the boards and the burn alerts — so it is pre-checked on every keystroke (`checkEditorId`, `existingSliIds`: the message under the field, the status *not applied — …* or *rename to <id> — Enter, Tab or Esc applies it*) and committed when the field is left; an invalid id stays in the field with its message, never sent, and keeps it across the pack's answer.
- **Rename and metric** (`tools/lib/library.mjs`): `id` and `semconv_metric` join `OVERRIDE_FIELDS`. A rename follows through the SLO id, the recording rule, the boards' bindings, the burn alerts, the chaos / remediation references and the provenance (under the new id, `customised: id`), keeps the library evidence (the expression is still the library's) and stays keyed by the id the library gives the SLI (`entryFragment` carries `key` beside `id`), so a renamed SLI stays attached to its library row; `checkSliIds` refuses a rename onto another SLI in the pack, onto an un-ticked library SLI (it would clash the moment it is ticked) and a custom id equal to a renamed id; `checkSloIds` spells its collision on the override's key; a rename to the id the pack already carries is no rename. `semconv_metric` is one bounded token; restated beside an edited expression it stays, left alone an edited expression still drops the template's claim; a custom SLI may state one. The index rows carry the library's metric; `packc init --override <sli>.id=` / `.semconv_metric=`; the API's shape message names both. No library SLI carries a percentile, so there is no percentile field.
- **Where it opens from**: every L1 SLI / SLO card on the stack is a real control (a focusable `role=button`, Enter / Space, `aria-haspopup=dialog`, a hover and a focus ring; an SLO card lands on the objective; the candidate ghosts on Define too; `stackCardActions`, a Build-only wrapper around the card body Discover shares), a rolodex card's *Edit* (*View* on Verify), the *+ Custom SLI* card, a product chip on the seed card (the L1 sheet on that product's first card). The sheet is live on **Define** — its pack is instantiated already — so the preview mode and *Compose in Compile →* are retired (`sheetModeFor`: edit on Define and Compile, verify on Verify). The header step tabs' accessible names read *Define — Service, tier & library* (and Compile, Verify): `BUILD_TABS` moved to `build-model.mjs`, `tabName` is the tab's `aria-label` and `title` (it read *Library — …*).
- **Tests**: `tools/test-build-editor.mjs` (new, 13: the model over a library ratio SLI, a threshold one with a unit, an above-tier, a customised, a renamed and a custom one, create mode, read-only, the helpers; headless renders per mode; the handlers incl. three quick characters and the id clash; the focus-caret-text preservation; the create form's live draft; the Tab trap with the sheet underneath; the modal rules), `tools/test-build-model.mjs` 59 → 58 (the four face / form tests retired; the rolodex's Edit and create card, a renamed id on the card and the ghost, the stack cards' action and wiring, the live Define sheet, the seed chips, the tab names), `tools/test-library.mjs` 89 → 91 (the rename everywhere, every clash, the metric, the CLI); `npm test` 265 → 279; lint 187 / 0. Driven live on 8013 as a user (the browser pane for real key and mouse input, headless Chrome for the pictures): a Define card opens the editor without seeding, `99.9` typed key by key loses nothing and the status names the SLO, a rename follows through the stack card, the SLO and the burn alerts, an edited good PromQL turns the evidence custom, Esc returns to the card, Tab → Enter → edit → Esc end to end, the threshold editor shows Bound and Unit without scrolling, a custom SLI lands on the stack, the edits survive a reload, Verify opens read-only, 1366×768 without scrolling; the console carried only the documented `/auth/me` 404 and the favicon.
- **After review** (each reproduced with real key events before the change, one commit each): the Id field committed every valid prefix of a rename typed key by key and left the SLI at `error_rat` when the final `error_rate` clashed, its message wiped by the next render — the id is pre-checked on every keystroke and committed when the field is left, after the browser's own focus move (a Tab computed from a field the synchronous render had replaced landed nowhere), and a typed id the model does not carry keeps its message across the pack's answer; typing the key back persisted `{ id: '<key>' }` (the studio said *customised: id*, the engine saw no rename) — the key clears the rename; renaming a custom SLI lost the focus after the first keystroke (its keys carry its id) and the pack answering while Done had the focus dropped it to `<body>`, after which Esc did nothing — the render keeps the focus by the field's name when its key changed and the footer controls carry `editor:*` keys, and one document listener closes the editor on Esc from `<body>`; the editor sat UNDER the sticky header (z 91 vs 100: at 1366×768 the title row of 35 of 56 library editors was hidden and the header took the clicks) — the scrim is at 150, the dialog at 151; the status said *applying…* forever when the text meant the committed value — `setOverride` / `updateCustom` answer whether anything changed; an older request answering while a newer edit waited on the debounce read *applied · SLO <stale id>* — `editorDirtyAfterAnswer`; the *applied · …* answer arrived by replacing the whole dialog, so the polite live region never announced it and the entrance replayed — the same editor is redrawn in place, the status node kept; the Verify card was named *Edit …* over a read-only dialog — *View … (as compiled)*; the DEFINE status still said the edits wait on Compile; two transitions missed the reduced-motion list; the fixed Type cell hand-wrote the read-only row (now `editFieldHtml` with `showHint`); the dead `.build-rolo-needs` rule (a sweep over every `.build-edit*` / `.build-editor*` / `.build-rolo*` class now runs in the test). `tools/test-build-editor.mjs` 13 → 14 (the debounce answer) with the handler, render and stylesheet tests extended; `npm test` 279 → 280; lint 187 / 0. Re-driven live on 8013: `error_rate` key by key sends nothing and keeps its message through another field's answer, Enter / Tab / Esc commit the rename (Tab lands on the next control), `99.90` after `99.9` sends nothing and the status stays applied, the same status and dialog nodes survive the answer, Done keeps the focus, Esc from `<body>` closes, a custom SLI renamed key by key keeps the focus on the next field, no dialog under the header at 1920×1080 or 1366×768, the Verify card reads *View … (as compiled)*.

### The tier is a seed, the library values are copies, the definition is a wizard stage
After trying the axis (PR #103) the maintainer found three constraints wrong: an SLI above the tier was disabled ("needs tier-1"), objectives and windows were read-only library values, and the definition column stayed a live form on every step — *selecting the tier is just a template, obviously users know what they need; we are constraining so much it's not useful … library entries need to be customisable once copied over into the current pack — they are not links, just a local copy.* Three principles (`docs/BUILD_JOURNEY.md` "The seed and the copies"):
- **The tier is a seed, not a constraint.** It decides what a pack starts with (`defaultToggles`, the scaffold) and which rubric grades it (`tierRequirements`); it never forbids an SLI. The engine's gate is gone: `instantiatePack` takes any SLI of the passed entries at any tier (the `sli-excluded` warning is retired), a value declared per tier resolves for an above-tier SLI by walking towards the stricter tiers to the first declared value (`perTier`; `validateLibraryEntry` lets a per-tier map leave out a tier below the SLI's `minTier`), an SLI's own tier features (forecast, chaos, remediation) keep their gating, and the rubric still grades at the chosen tier — an extra SLI simply counts. `packc init --slis` / the new repeatable `--sli` take any SLI of the entries. In the studio the rolodex never disables a card: an above-tier one carries an informational chip (*from the tier-1 profile*) and the objective it starts with, `addSliSelection` never refuses, `retargetSlis` keeps every SLI the user has across a tier change (with its overrides) and only refreshes the tier's defaults, the L1 count reads *N from a higher tier*, `provenance.slis[id].aboveTier` / `profileTier` say so on the result.
