Retention is unchanged (`JOURNEY_RUN_FILE_RE` untouched, `readJourneyRuns` keeps ignoring `live/`) and now also removes orphaned snapshots — after the record prune, `pruneLiveSnapshots(recordFiles, liveFiles)` (pure) names the run-shaped `live/` files whose record is gone, never a survivor's, never a non-run file; `readLivePack(name, record)` reads a snapshot back (`null` when none was kept, the path is not the `live/<stem>.json` shape, or the file is gone — a pruned snapshot reads as absent, never as an error). `renderJourneyMarkdown` adds a `Requirement chains` table (`chain | verdict | ladder | integrity | ladder integrity | worst node`, cap 24, only when the record carries chains — no table means none were declared or recorded, never that every chain is intact; the worst-node cell carries the ladder detail and `blinds N SLOs`) and a `Transitions since previous run` section (`title — intact/healthy → broken/broken (worse); newly degraded: …; recovered: …`, `appeared` / `disappeared`, then the `live pack: kept (…) — reason` / `not kept — reason` line).
- **Candidate-cause ranker.** `chain-history.mjs` gains `rankCauses({ previous, current, deploys })` → `{ transitions, causes: [{ rank, kind, score, evidence, chains, nodes }], vantage: { changed, from, to, detail } | null, note }` with `note` = `candidate causes ranked by evidence — not a root-cause verdict`. Only chains whose transition reads `worse` (plus chains that appeared already partial / broken / degraded) are considered, and only their nodes that moved (new on the current side, or the same identity with a different status / ladder status; every degraded node when the record cannot say); nodes whose ladder status is `unobserved` are dropped last, so a chain whose only movement is the vantage looking away yields no cause. Four kinds, scored by fixed `CAUSE_SCORES` so a rank is explainable by reading the table: `observogram-deploy` — a deploy inside the window whose item `artifact` names a moved node 0.9 (an artefact id on the node — `aId` / `bId` — matches exactly, otherwise the artefact must be contained in the node's label or key case-insensitively with a `dash:` prefix dropped; only that direction, so a rule deploy cannot blame every SLI whose short name it embeds; stubs under three characters and the wildcard `all` name nothing; the persisted degraded-node record carries no ids today, so on a real run the match is by label / key), else a deploy that wrote the journey's pack (by `pack.id` / `pack.name` against the record's `packA`) 0.6; `config-drift` — a moved node that drifted on a decision-bearing field 0.8, on cosmetic or unrecorded fields 0.4; `backend-version` — a product both records reported with a different version, 0.6 when a moved node is a backend / metric / recording rule / burn-rate alert, 0.3 otherwise; `stack-self-metric` — a current-record stack row that answered data and reads as a signal (`nonzero` hint, `lower` above 0, or a `higher` ratio below 1) in the family feeding a moved node's kind per `FAMILY_FOR_KIND` (scrape → `scrape_job`; ruler → `recording_rule` / `burn_rate` / `sli` / `slo`; notify → `alert_route`; tsdb → `backend` / `storage_metrics`; collector → `pipeline_*` / `otel`; dashboards → `panel` / `dashboard`; synthetic → `synthetic`) 0.5. One cause per distinct evidence, aggregating every chain and node it explains; ordering score desc → kind order → evidence; a verify-type record or a dry run is skipped; malformed input never throws. `deploysInWindow(deploys, since, until)` windows Observogram's own audit lines to `(previous.startedAt, current.startedAt]` on `at` and merges the latest verify onto its deploy so the evidence can name the outcome (`deploy dep_x by carlos at … (upsert) touched <artifact>; verify: pending`); `journey.mjs` reads `deploys.jsonl` beside `runs/` under the same workspace root without importing server code (missing file → nothing, a torn line skipped). `vantage` reports what changed about the vantage itself — the vantage word, a probe family that newly failed (with its error), is no longer exposed, no longer probed, answers again or is now exposed, and the exposed tool count — beside the causes, never among them. The record carries `causes` (`null` on the first run: nothing to explain yet); `topCause(record)` reads the rank-1 cause. Markdown: `### Candidate causes — ranked by evidence, not a root-cause verdict` (`_no previous run_` / `_no chain got worse since …_`, `N. [kind] score — evidence (chains: titles)`, then `vantage changed: …` / `vantage: unchanged`).
- **Surfaces.** `packc journey list` appends `chainStatusLine(record)` after the stack segment — `chains 8/10 intact · ladder 7 healthy · 2 degraded` (zero ladder buckets other than healthy omitted; `chains none` for a record without declared chains) — and, only when a chain got worse or a cause was ranked (`transitionGotWorse`), `causeLine(record)` (`top cause: [kind] evidence` / `no candidate causes`); a vantage-lost line carries neither. `GET /api/journeys` `lastRun` gains `chains` (`chainSummary(lastRun)`, `null` without chains), `transition: { any, changed, worse } | null` (counts, not the full diff), `topCause` (the rank-1 cause object or `null`) and `vantageChanged` (`true` / `false` / `null` without a causes block); `GET /api/journeys/:name/runs` hands the record through unchanged. The Journeys view prints one plain-text chains line per card — `requirement chains: N/M intact · ladder: h healthy · d degraded · b broken · u unobserved · top exposure: <label> (<kind>) blinds N SLOs` with a muted `changed since previous run (W worse)` marker — and one cause line — `candidate cause: [kind] evidence — not a verdict` with a muted `vantage changed` marker; counts and markers, no colours, no new CSS. In the Diagnose chain cards (`compare-view.mjs`) `evidenceFor` names `present but unhealthy`, `present but stale` and `unobserved — <detail>` nodes (the ladder reading replaces `missing live` / `aligned` for that node) and appends `· blinds N SLO(s)` to `declared_only` / `drifted` nodes with `blastRadius.slos > 0`; the card meta shows `ladder: <ladderVerdict>` and the section rollup appends `· ladder NN%`.
- **Suites.** New `tools/test-blast-radius.mjs` (`npm run test:blast-radius`, 58 assertions — the vendoring guard, the tables, hop distances along the full chain, the protection relation, scaffold exclusion, cycle safety, determinism, the listing cap, the accepted input shapes, no input mutation) and `tools/test-chain-history.mjs` (`npm run test:chain-history`, 122 assertions — the guard, the tables and caps, which nodes are recorded and in what order, the truncated markers, the summary and its top exposure, transition directions, appeared / disappeared and node lists, the deploy window and verify merge, every cause kind and score, the one-way artefact match, the moved-node rule, the vantage block including a vantage-lost previous record and file-sourced sides, determinism, malformed input); both in the aggregate (34 suites). `tools/test-traceability-graph.mjs` adds the `graphShape` projection, the `blastRadius` pins per node kind, the ladder block (healthy / unhealthy / stale rules, an unhealthy burn rule linked by name, a down target, unsupported and failed probe families, absent for real, withheld from Pack B) and the byte-identity pin on `integrity` / `verdict` / `counts` / node `status` / `rollup.integrityMean` with and without the annotations. `tools/test-journey.mjs` drives a fake MCP through the snapshot policy (every branch of `livePackDecision`, the files under `live/`, pruning, `readLivePack`), the record shape and its round-trip through the history file, the markdown sections, the CLI lines and the ranker against a seeded `deploys.jsonl` (a deploy inside the window, one outside, a torn line, the vantage block). `server/test-smoke.mjs` seeds a record with `branches` and `causes` and pins the `lastRun` shape.
- **Review corrections (step 4).** Two fix passes after the step-4 reviews; nothing scored changed on either side (`integrity`, `verdict`, `counts`, node `status`, `rollup.integrityMean`, the grade — byte-identical, still pinned). *Graph side.* The fetcher stamps its own clock: `mcp.fetchStartedAt` (the instant the fetch began) and `mcp.observedAt.<family>` (the instant each probe family answered, data or empty; `dashboards` re-stamped after detail enrichment), and the ladder's "now" is the family's `observedAt`, else `fetchStartedAt`, else the caller's `mcp.refreshedAt` (older packs — named in the detail only on that fallback), so a `refreshedAt` trailing a fresh observation by a minute no longer reads stale. A scrape job is `present_unhealthy` only when EVERY target is down (the fetcher's `scrape_jobs_down` rule); some targets down reads `alive` with `n/m targets down: <instances>`, and a down target's `lastError` is not the job's. Fractional burn factors (`_burn_14_4x_` ↔ 14.4) parse in the graph and the fetcher; alert observation entries carry `interval` (the group evaluation interval — the alert adapter keeps it now, and burn-rate freshness uses the longest linked one) and `labels: { slo, burn_rate, window_short, window_long }` (present keys only, omitted when none), and the ladder links a live rule to its declared window by labels first, name second; the `alert_rules_unhealthy` list is narrowed to the declared windows like the rule set and the detail names the matched / failing rule(s). An unknown interval reads `healthy` / `alive` only within a 1 h ceiling ("interval unknown; 1 h ceiling applied"), older reads `present_stale`; a timestamp more than 1 s past "now" reads `alive` with "timestamp <n>s in the future (clock skew); freshness not judged", never `healthy`; a reported health of `unknown` stays `present_unhealthy`; `ladderVerdict` ignores `live_only` nodes as the scored verdict does; "no fetch timestamp on the wire (mcp.refreshedAt missing); staleness not judged" when the observation has a timestamp and the pack has no clock. `compare-view`'s `evidenceFor` orders the evidence line before the 5-cap — declared_only load-bearing, present_unhealthy, present_stale, drifted, declared_only (other), unverifiable, unobserved, live_only — and a `declared_only` node whose live twin sits under another chain reads "on the wire and in Pack B under another chain: …" (or "in Pack B under another chain; not reachable from this branch"); "withheld from Pack B" only when no twin exists. The golden `alert_rules.json` gains the two `interval` lines; the largest example pair's payload grows +88 % (spec §5c). *Journey side.* **Deploy attribution is exact.** `runJourney` resolves every windowed deploy item's artifact selector against Pack A (`resolveDeployArtifact`, exported: `declared:<i>` → the i-th declared recording rule's name, `slo:<id>` → the SLO id, its SLI base and the SLI the pack binds it to, `dash:<id>` → the id, a bare name → itself, `all` → nothing; the item carries `resolved` beside `artifact`) and the ranker matches a resolved name exactly — against the node's `aId` / `bId` (now persisted on every degraded node), its label, or an identity handle of its `kind::{json}` key (`id`, `name`, `record`, `slo`, `job`, `uid`) — never by substring, and only when the item's group can write the node's kind (`DEPLOY_GROUP_KINDS`: dashboards / restore / delete → dashboard, panel; rules → recording_rule, burn_rate, sli, slo; alerts → burn_rate, alert_route; alertmanager → alert_route; pipelines → pipeline_*, otel; an unknown group constrains nothing); the `< 3` stub rule is gone; the evidence reads `touched declared:0 → payment:api_availability:ratio_5m` and, for a wildcard-only deploy of the journey's pack, `wrote pack X (all rules) — no item names a moved artefact` (0.6 — needs the audit line's `pack.id`, the registry id = the pack file's stem, or `pack.name` to equal the record's `packA.name`). Without a caller's resolution the ranker knows the pack-free part (`deployArtifactNames`). **Versions.** A flip to or from the literal `live` (the fetcher's word for "answered without a number") is a vantage matter — reported in `vantage.detail` as `mcp.versions.<product> changed to/from live`, never a cause — and `backend-version` scores 0.6 (`versionFeedsMovedKind`) only when the changed product feeds a moved node's family (`PRODUCT_FAMILIES`: prometheus / victoriametrics / thanos / mimir → scrape, ruler, tsdb; grafana → dashboards; alertmanager → notify; otel, otelcol → collector; loki, promtail → logs; jaeger, tempo → traces; unknown → none), else 0.3 — a Grafana-only change is no longer 0.6 on a Prometheus rule. **Baseline.** The chain diff, the versions and the deploy window are compared against the newest record that CARRIES chains (`BASELINE_SCAN_LIMIT` 25 back), the vantage against the immediately previous record (`rankCauses` gains `baseline`); `transition` is never null on a new record: `{ reason, since: null, changed: [], appeared: [], disappeared: [], any: false, skipped }` with `reason` `first run` | `previous run lost its vantage` | `previous runs carry no chain record` when nothing could be compared, else the diff with `reason: null` and `skipped: [{ startedAt, outcome }]` naming the records passed over (cap 8); the report says "previous run <ts> lost its vantage — comparing against <baseline ts>" in both sections and never "no previous run" when one exists. **No broadening.** A node whose status and ladder status are identical on both sides is never blamed; when nothing recorded moved, the changed transition entry carries `note` — `declared side: missingRoles none → action`, `degraded list truncated (cap 16) — a node that moved may be unrecorded` — and the chain yields no cause. **Escaping.** Every value the transitions, causes and breaches sections interpolate goes through `mdCell` (line breaks collapsed, `|` escaped, a line-leading `#` / `-` / `*` / `+` / `>` / `1.` neutralised) and the ranker's evidence collapses whitespace; a label carrying `\n\n### Gate breaches` cannot forge a section. **Also:** `readDeployLog` (exported) reads only the trailing 8 MB of `deploys.jsonl` (`DEPLOY_LOG_TAIL_BYTES`, the cut first line dropped); `readJourneyRuns` reads only run-shaped files (a stray `notes.json` is never the previous run); an unparseable baseline start gives an EMPTY deploy window, never all of history; a snapshot write failure reads `livePack: { kept: false, reason: 'snapshot write failed: …' }` beside `historyError`; `packc journey list` appends `vantage changed: <detail>` (`vantageLine`) whenever the vantage block says so, with or without a top cause; `pruneLiveSnapshots` names only run-shaped `live/` files OLDER than the oldest surviving record (retention victims), never a newer stem whose record a concurrent writer has not landed yet; `stackSignalRows` reads direction + value only, never the stored `nonzero` hint; the `causes` block no longer carries a second copy of the transition (`{ causes, vantage, note }`); `chainStatusLine` distinguishes `chains none (pre-step-4 record)` / `chains none (vantage lost)` / `chains 0 declared`; `keepLivePack: never` writes none from now on (earlier snapshots age out with their records); `rankCauses` accepts only `type: 'deploy'` (as `deploysInWindow` did). `cause.chains` carries the branch titles and `rootKeys` the identity keys; `chainSummary.degradedNodes` / `topExposure` count and rank the declared chains only, and `undeclaredNodes` counts the live-only nodes of undeclared chains — the Journeys view appends `N live-only in undeclared chains` only when > 0. Pinned: the server's real selector strings (`declared:0`, `slo:checkout`, `all`, a rollback ref under `restore`), `dash:payment` blaming neither `scrape_job payment` nor the rule embedding it, a three-letter dashboard id, wrong groups, `branchRecordsFromGraph` output satisfying the id match, the `live` flips, a Grafana-only change on a Prometheus rule, the stale hint, missing-role / truncation notes, baseline vs previous, the wording after a vantage-lost run (and with no earlier chains, and after a pre-step-4 record), `notes.json`, the 8 MB tail with a cut first line, the empty window, markdown forgery through labels / actor / evidence / breaches / vantage / live-pack reason, the CLI vantage segment, the smoke seed made consistent and the `vantageChanged: null` API case; the fake-MCP e2e deploy now carries `declared:0` end to end.

### Stack-health evidence per journey run (step 3)
Step 2 sampled the observability stack's own self-metrics on every live fetch as point-in-time signals; step 3 turns those samples into **evidence over time** by keeping them per journey run, so the run history becomes the time series the journeys surface reads. Nothing in this block creates a `Verified` stamp, touches the grade, or turns a sample into an SLO verdict.
- **Run-history retention (prerequisite).** A journey on a cron cadence used to grow `runs/<journey>/` without bound. `writeRunRecord` now prunes the directory to the newest `OBSERVOGRAM_JOURNEY_RUN_RETENTION` files after every write (default `1000`; `0` = unlimited; a value that is not a non-negative integer falls back to the default; legacy `TOMOGRAPH_*` spelling honoured via `brandEnv`). The policy is the pure, exported `pruneRunFiles(files, keep)` (names to delete, oldest first — filenames are ISO start times so lexical order is chronological; non-`.json` entries are never candidates) plus `parseRunRetention` / `journeyRunRetention()` (read at write time). A deletion failure lands on the record as `historyError`, never thrown; `readJourneyRuns` is unchanged. Scheduling stays external by decision (VALUE_BACKLOG item 11).
- **`stackEvidence` on the run record.** `liveEvidenceFacts(canonicalB)` parses `mcp.observed.stack_metrics`, `mcp.observed.alertmanager` and `mcp.observed.grafana.*` into `stackEvidence = { status, reason, rows: [{ id, family, product, value, unit, direction, outcome, hint, at, referenceSli, reason? }], alertmanager: { version, clusterStatus, silencesActive, error } | null, grafana: { datasources, unhealthyDatasources, contactPoints, error } | null }` — `null` when Pack B carries no `mcp.stack.status` (a file-sourced B is honest absence), rows enriched with the contracts table's `referenceSli` and display-only `hint` (a row the table no longer declares keeps `referenceSli: null`), `expr` dropped, cap 64, malformed JSON degrading to no rows / `null` surfaces rather than a fabricated panel. The step-2 `stack` counts block is unchanged for compatibility. Suite: `tools/test-journey.mjs` (retention policy + effect, env parsing, evidence from a fixture-built annotated Pack B, null for file-sourced B, retired row ids, malformed JSON, advertised-but-failing surfaces, round-trip through history).

- **Gate key `stack` — thresholds on the samples.** `gate.stack: { requireSampled?, rows?: { <rowId>: { min?, max? } } }` turns the samples into an early warning for a business owner, never an SLO verdict: `requireSampled` breaches `stack` when `stackEvidence` is `null` (`Pack B is not a live draft`), the panel was `not-attempted` (with the reason), or no row answered `data`; `rows.<id>` breaches `stack.<id>` with `no sample for <id> (<outcome>[: reason]) — threshold cannot be checked` when the row is absent or did not answer data (a file-sourced B with a declared threshold breaches honestly instead of passing by absence), and with `<id> = <value> <unit> outside [min … max] — point-in-time sample, not an SLO verdict` when the number falls outside the band (equality passes; an `info` row may carry a threshold). `loadJourneyDef` validates the block through the exported `validateGateStack(stack, name)` — unknown row ids (case-sensitive against `STACK_SELF_METRIC_PROBES`) throw with the known ids listed, `min` / `max` must be finite numbers, an entry with neither (or `min > max`, or a non-boolean `requireSampled`) is refused. Pure `formatStackValue(value, unit)` renders `83.3%` / `0.004/s` / `0.0/h` / `7.4s` / `1`. `renderJourneyMarkdown` adds a `Stack self-metrics — point-in-time samples` table (`id | family | value unit | outcome | hint | reference SLI`, cap 24, only when rows exist) and lists stack breaches with the others; `packc journey list` appends `stackStatusLine(record)` (`stack sampled N` / `stack not attempted` / `stack none`). The capture default gate and every existing key are unchanged; nothing touches the grade. Pinned in `tools/test-journey.mjs` (every branch of the gate, the validation errors, the formatter, the table and cap, the CLI line).
- **History helpers, listing summary and the journeys chips.** New browser-safe `tools/lib/stack-evidence.mjs` (imports only the contracts table; vendorable, listed in `docs/VENDORING.md`): `stackSeries(runs, rowId)` (oldest → newest per row, gaps skipped never interpolated, non-data outcomes kept with `value: null`), `latestByFamily(record)` (the data row per family, ties by table order, retired rows last), `stackSummary(record)` (`{ status, reason, sampled, families }` or `null` without evidence), `nonzeroRuns(series)`, `stackPostureBudget(series, { objective, cadenceMs, windowMs, isBad? })` (the cadence heuristic — a sampled posture is only `measurable` when the window allows ≥ 10 bad samples; every note reads "signal, not verdict") and the shared `formatStackValue` / `stackOutcomeLabel` (journey.mjs now re-exports the formatter from here — one vocabulary for CLI, report and studio). `GET /api/journeys` adds `lastRun.stack = stackSummary(lastRun)` (`null` for a file-sourced B); the Journeys view loads the module from `/lib/stack-evidence.mjs` at call time and renders a `stack self-metrics — point-in-time samples` line per card — one chip per family, the `nonzero` hint as a muted marker, id + reference SLI in the title, `nonzero in N of last M runs` for lower-is-comfortable rows over the fetched history, one muted chip with the reason when not attempted; no chip carries an ok/error colour. New suite `tools/test-stack-evidence.mjs` (`npm run test:stack-evidence`, in the aggregate); `server/test-smoke.mjs` seeds a record with `stackEvidence` and checks the listing shape.
- **Docs reconciled with the branch.** README's journeys section carries the gate example with the `stack:` block, the retention env var and the studio chips, and its API table / repository map now list the journey routes and modules (`tools/lib/journey.mjs`, `tools/lib/stack-evidence.mjs`, `studio/journeys-view.mjs`); `docs/MCP_INTEGRATION.md`'s journey section documents the record fields, the gate keys with their breach table and honesty rules, retention, and the history helpers with the cadence heuristic; `docs/VENDORING.md` lists `stack-evidence.mjs` + the contracts table as a vendorable pair; `docs/VALUE_BACKLOG.md` item 11 gains a status note (drift over time now includes the stack samples; scheduling stays external).
- **Review corrections (step 3).** Reproduced against a seeded workspace and the fake MCP: `latestByFamily` ranked data rows by contracts-table order alone, so whenever a family's first table row is `higher` / `info` (`scrape_success_ratio`, `tsdb_active_series`) and answered data, the family's lower-is-better rows — and with them the `nonzero` marker and the `nonzero in N of last M runs` history the chips exist for — never reached the surface: the card read `scrape 95.0%` while the run breached on `scrape_targets_down`. Data rows now rank by `nonzero` hint first, then declared-in-table before retired, then `lower` before `higher` / `info`, then table order (`tools/test-stack-evidence.mjs` pins each rung; the old "ties by table order" assertion pinned the hiding). Also from the review: a `rows.<id>` threshold on a row the fetcher never wrote names why — `(not-attempted: <tier reason>)` on a not-attempted panel, `(not attempted by the sampler — call budget exhausted or row not observed)` on a sampled one — instead of a bare "absent from this run" that read like a fetch hole; `evaluateStackGate` no longer trusts an unvalidated gate object (a bound that is not a finite number, an entry with neither bound, `min > max` or a non-mapping entry breaches `stack.<id>` as `threshold invalid (…) — cannot be checked`, never passes by silence); a breach whose display rounding equals its bound appends the raw number (`0.000/s (raw 0.0004)`); an outcome the contracts do not declare is kept verbatim (missing → `unknown`) rather than relabelled `failed`; `POST /api/journeys/capture` runs `validateGateStack` before saving (400) and `GET /api/journeys` / `packc journey list` / the studio card carry `loadError` for a definition that cannot load; `pruneRunFiles` only treats the run-record filename shape (`JOURNEY_RUN_FILE_RE`) as a run, so a hand-dropped `notes.json` neither displaces a record nor is deleted, and the deletion-failure branch (`historyError`, never thrown) is now under test. README and `docs/MCP_INTEGRATION.md` reworded accordingly (`requireSampled`'s third branch, "the only *gate* reader", the raw-number fallback of the chips without the helper module).

### Stack self-metrics (step 2)
Observogram monitors the artefacts that monitor the system; step 2 of the roadmap starts **acquiring the observability stack's own health signals** through the MCP so later steps can build stack-health evidence and early warning. Three things are true of everything in this block, by contract and pinned by the suites: **every sampled number is a point-in-time signal, not a verdict** — no `Verified` stamp, no `Scaffold` marker, no SLO verdict and no grade criterion or grade input changed anywhere; **a restricted MCP tier reads `not-attempted` with the reason**, never "absent"; and **the alias table reads only names a real product exposed** — it was documentation-grounded when written (every metric name following the product's upstream docs, named on the row) and now carries three live evidence sources: the pinned Docker stack (`npm run test:stack:live`, every alias stamped with the image it was verified on) and the public (2026-09-07) and authenticated (2026-09-08) Krystaline tiers (`npm run record-fixtures`); a product a given stack does not run reads an honest `not-in-inventory`.
- **Alias table (contracts).** New data-only `tools/lib/contracts/stack-self-metrics.mjs`: `STACK_SELF_METRIC_PROBES` — 24 rows over nine families (scrape, ruler, notify, tsdb, collector, dashboards, synthetic, logs, traces), each with its signal, unit, display-only direction, the reference-pack SLI whose vocabulary it follows, ordered product aliases (`{ product, expr, requires }`) and the upstream docs the names come from — plus lookup resolvers (`probeRows`, `rowsForFamily`, `eligibleAliases`, `productPreferenceOrder`, `displayHint`, `bestOutcome`) and the allowed sets (`STACK_FAMILIES`, `STACK_UNITS`, `STACK_DIRECTIONS`, `STACK_OUTCOMES`). One known discrepancy is documented on the row rather than fixed: the reference pack's `scrape_duration_p99` names `scrape_duration_seconds_bucket`, which Prometheus never exposes (the metric is a per-target gauge), so `scrape_duration_max` samples `max(scrape_duration_seconds)`.
- **Registry rows + shapes.** `mcp-capabilities.mjs` gains `stack_self_metrics` (carrier for the table, rides `metrics_query`), `alertmanager_status`, `alertmanager_silences`, `grafana_datasources`, `grafana_datasource_health` (enrich) and `grafana_contact_points`; `build_info_versions` now declares its `instant-vector` shape. `response-shapes.mjs` adds `instant-vector` (`{ result }` and `{ data: { result } }`), `silences`, `datasources`, `contact-points`, and a new **object** shape kind (`object: true`, `objectAt`, `anyOfKeys`) for `status-object` and `health-object` — `validateResponseShape` locates the first candidate object carrying every key group, tolerant of extras and of a `{ data: {…} }` envelope. Five tool names join the pinned wire surface.
- **Sampler (fetcher).** `tools/fetch-live-pack.mjs` exports `sampleStackSelfMetrics({ callTool, quiet, metricsQueryTool, inventory, seenProducts, discoveredToolNames, hasToolsList, refreshedAt, maxCalls })` → `{ status, reason, rows, callsMade }`, run by `fetchMcp` after the version probes. Policy: attempt only when `metrics_query` is advertised (a server with no `tools/list` is attempted), otherwise `not-attempted` with the reason `metrics_query not exposed by this MCP (restricted tier)` and zero calls; with a `metric_names` inventory an alias is eligible only when every `requires` name is present (rows with none are `not-in-inventory`, no call); eligible aliases ordered generic-first then products already seen; at most 2 calls per row, 48 per panel (`call budget exhausted` beyond it); every call through `quiet()` under the family `stack_self_metrics`; instant vectors parsed from `{ result }` or `{ data: { result } }` by `sampleFromInstantVector` (exported — the recorder reads answers the same way), `NaN`/`±Inf`/missing samples are `empty`. Outcomes are exactly `data | empty | failed | not-in-inventory | not-attempted` — never "ok".
- **Alertmanager / Grafana status rows.** `observeAlertmanager` (`alertmanager_status` → version / uptime / clusterStatus; `alertmanager_silences` → active + total) and `observeGrafana` (`grafana_datasources` → `[{uid, name, type}]`, `grafana_datasource_health` per uid ≤ 10 → `ok | error | unknown` + a message trimmed to 200 chars, `grafana_contact_points` → count + ≤ 32 names), each tool guarded by `tools/list` when present, tolerant parsing through the response shapes. `fetchMcp` returns `stackSamples`, `alertmanagerObserved`, `grafanaObserved`; answered tools leave `unmatchedTools`.
- **Annotations.** `buildCanonicalPack` (new params `stackSamples`, `alertmanagerObserved`, `grafanaObserved`, all `null`-safe — a caller that predates step 2 writes nothing) emits `mcp.stack.status`, `mcp.stack.reason` (only when not attempted), `mcp.stack.sampled` / `empty` / `failed` / `notInInventory` / `notAttempted`, `mcp.stack.families` (`<family>:<best outcome>`), `mcp.observed.stack_metrics` (attempted + not-in-inventory rows, cap 64), `mcp.observed.alertmanager`, `mcp.observed.grafana.datasources`, `mcp.observed.grafana.contact_points`, and lists the answering tools in `mcp.toolsCalled`. No `mcp.verified.*` or `mcp.scaffold.*` key is touched by any of it (pinned by the suite).
- **Surfaces.** `POST /api/draft-from-mcp` answers `summary.stack` (`status`, `reason`, the five counts as numbers, `families: { family: best outcome }`, `rows: [{ id, family, product, value, unit, direction, outcome, hint, reason? }]` — `hint` is the contracts' display-only `displayHint`, computed on read and never stored), `summary.alertmanager` and `summary.grafana` (each `null` when not exposed), and warns `Stack self-metrics not attempted — metrics_query not exposed by this MCP tier.` on a restricted tier; `GET /api/live-status` adds `stackStatus` and `stackSampled`. The studio draft summary gains a "stack self-metrics — point-in-time sample, signal not verdict" block: one row per family with the best row's formatted value (or `— empty` / `— probe failed` / `— not in inventory`), a single `— not attempted` row on a restricted tier, then the Alertmanager version + active silences, the Grafana datasource count with the unhealthy names, and the contact-point count. `liveEvidenceFacts` adds `stack { status, reason, sampled, empty, failed, notAttempted }`, carried on the journey run record and printed as one `Stack self-metrics` line in the markdown report — no gate key reads it.
- **Fixtures: synthetic now, recorded later.** `tools/fixtures/mcp/synthetic/` holds hand-written samples for each new shape (an otel-mcp-server and a Prometheus-API instant vector, Alertmanager status and silences, Grafana datasources, datasource health, contact points), each carrying a `_synthetic` marker the shape check tolerates and `tools/test-contract-shapes.mjs` asserts; they pin shape only. The fixtures README now carries a **provenance table** (recorded 2026-06-12 from the Krystaline otel-mcp-server vs synthetic 2026-09-07; the recorded metric-name list is trimmed to 25 names and proves none of the alias table's `requires`), the recorder procedure, and what to re-run afterwards. A recorded `<tool>.json` at the fixtures top level takes precedence over its synthetic copy in the shapes suite (same assertions, plus "no `_synthetic` marker"), and every file in `recorded-stack/` must be a valid instant vector for a declared row.
- **Recorder.** `tools/record-mcp-fixtures.mjs` (`npm run record-fixtures`, `MCP_URL` + optional `MCP_AUTH`) reuses the fetcher's JSON-RPC client and the contract registry for every tool name (the guard suite scans it for literals), is read-only against the MCP, and never prints or stores the token (every string that leaves the process is redacted). Default mode **reports**: the `tools/list` surface with its drift against `allKnownToolNames()`, the metric inventory, and for every alias of every row whether its `requires` are all present plus the value read with `sampleFromInstantVector` (`data` / `empty` / `FAILED` / `not-in-inventory (missing …)` / `not-attempted (restricted tier)`), then the status tools and probe families with their shape verdicts. `-- --write` records: the full inventory to the git-ignored `.tmp-mcp-metric-names.json`, the trimmed inventory (every required name and the `*_build_info` metrics kept, plus the first 25 others, in the server's order, replaced where the adapter found the list), scrape targets / the winning rule tool(s) / dashboard search with every list capped at 6 and structure verbatim, one `metrics_query` instant vector per family under `recorded-stack/<row id>.json` with `_recorded` provenance, and the status tools when advertised; `--out <dir>` redirects everything. New scripts `lint:recorder`, `test:recorder`.
- **First live recording (2026-09-07, public Krystaline tier, `https://www.krystaline.io/mcp/public`, no credentials).** The recorder's first real run replaced the guesswork for part of the table: that tier advertises 43 tools including `metrics_query`, `metrics_label_values` (2,682 names), `alertmanager_status` and `alertmanager_silences`, so `metrics_label_values.json` is now a 40-name recording that evidences `up`, `scrape_duration_seconds`, `vm_*`, `alertmanager_*`, `otelcol_exporter_queue_*`, `grafana_http_request_duration_seconds_count` and `promtail_dropped_entries_total`; `alertmanager_status.json` is recorded (its `config` field, the full Alertmanager configuration, omitted by hand); and `recorded-stack/` holds six real `metrics_query` instant vectors (otel-mcp-server answers a flat `{ status, resultType, result }` envelope). Ten aliases read `data`, one `empty`, twenty-six `not-in-inventory` — the stack runs VictoriaMetrics, not Prometheus, and does not scrape vmalert (its ruler's own health is invisible there: exactly the blind spot the sampler exists to name). Every Grafana-backed tool except `grafana_health` answers `HTTP 401` on that tier (no Grafana credentials), so the Grafana status fixtures stay synthetic until the authenticated tier is recorded; the silences recording was empty and pins nothing, so its synthetic copy stays too. The 2026-06-12 `metrics_targets.json` / `vmalert_rules.json` / `grafana_dashboards_search.json` recordings were kept on purpose (today's targets are all `up`; the `down` Alertmanager target is the liveness case the adapters are pinned against). Contract fix found by the run: `grafana_dashboard_get` was listed as a **search** candidate of the `dashboards` probe, so a failed search fell through to a uid-less call whose `-32602 Invalid arguments` error masked the real search error; it is now only the `dashboard_detail` enrichment tool, and the family reports the 401 it actually got. Suites replay a recorded status fixture in preference to its synthetic copy and read expected values from the fixture they replayed, and the recorder suite's fake inventory is frozen to the 2026-06-12 names so re-recording never moves its expectations.
- **Authenticated-tier recording (2026-09-08, `https://www.krystaline.io/mcp`, `MCP_AUTH` bearer).** The recorder's first run against the authenticated Krystaline tier re-recorded `vmalert_rules.json` (the 2026-06-12 recording predated most of today's rule groups; adapted goldens re-pinned; its annotations carry rule descriptions and runbook URLs into the private krystalinex-core repo — references, not credentials, and the identical payload the credential-less public tier serves), `alertmanager_status.json` (`_recorded.scrubbed` names the hand-removed `config` — receivers, SMTP settings, internal hostnames — same trim as the 2026-09-07 file it replaces) and the six `recorded-stack/` instant vectors (same rows and outcomes as the public tier: 14 aliases `data` · 0 `failed`; the trimmed `metrics_label_values.json` recording came back byte-identical to the committed public-tier file, and both tiers report a 2,682-name inventory — the full lists were not compared). The intended goal — replacing the synthetic Grafana fixtures — was not reachable: the tier advertises **no Grafana-backed tools with any issued key** (its otel-mcp-server deployment carries no Grafana integration; the public tier advertises them but answers HTTP 401), so `synthetic/grafana_*.json` and the 2026-06-12 `grafana_dashboards_search.json` stay, `metrics_targets.json` keeps its 2026-06-12 `down`-target recording (the 2026-09-08 payload is all-`up`), and the empty silences recording again pins nothing. The tier also advertises logs and k8s tools the registry has no rows for (noted in the fixtures README; no registry rows added).
- **Local-stack recording of the Grafana-backed tools (2026-09-08, `docker/stack.compose.yaml` fronted by otel-mcp-server 1.8.0).** The fourth evidence source, and today the only place `grafana_datasources`, `grafana_datasource_health` and `grafana_contact_points` answer: the pinned stack (Grafana 12.4.4, admin-seeded with a `VictoriaMetrics (stack)` datasource, a `Loki (absent)` datasource pointing at a service the stack does not run, the `webhook-oncall` contact point and one dashboard) behind a local otel-mcp-server started with `GRAFANA_URL` + `GRAFANA_AUTH_BASIC` and no MCP auth keys, recorded through `MCP_URL=http://127.0.0.1:3011/mcp npm run record-fixtures -- --write --out .tmp-recorded-local`. The three `synthetic/grafana_*.json` files are gone, replaced by recordings whose `_recorded` carries `scrubbed: []` with what was reviewed (`jsonData` / `secureJsonFields` empty; compose hostnames and the loopback Grafana URL inside a failed check's error kept verbatim) and the server line, added by hand. Two shapes the hand-written files had wrong: **`grafana_datasource_health`** answers `{ datasource, health }` — `health` is `{ supported: true, status: 'OK' | 'ERROR', message, details }` when Grafana's endpoint answered 2xx and `{ supported: false, error: 'HTTP 400: Bad Request — <url>' }` when it did not (Grafana 12.4.4 answers a check that ran and failed, backend unreachable, with `HTTP 400`; an unknown uid with `500`) — so the `health-object` contract now locates `['health', 'data', '']` and accepts a verdict key or the `supported` / `error` pair (one key group: the checker requires every group and the two forms share no key), and `observeGrafana` reads `OK` → `ok`, `ERROR` → `error`, `supported: false` + `HTTP 400` → `error` with the error text as message, any other `supported: false` → `unknown` (not checked, never "not unhealthy"), `supported: true` without a status → `unknown`; and **`grafana_contact_points`** answers the receivers API (`{ count, contactPoints: [{ name, active, integrations: [{ name, sendResolved, lastNotifyAttempt, lastNotifyAttemptDuration }] }] }` — no `uid`, `type` or `settings`, so no webhook URL or secret ever rides in it), not the provisioning shape. The recorder keeps both datasource-health verdicts when several uids answer (the first uid's answer as `grafana_datasource_health.json`; the first answer of the other case as `.ok.json` / `.error.json` with its uid and case, skipped when identical to the primary file). Suites replay the recording with expected values read from the files: `test-contract-shapes` pins both health cases and the envelope location, `test-fetch-live` drives `observeGrafana` with the recorded uids and asserts the unhealthy datasources name exactly `Loki (absent)` (plus the non-400 and no-status `unknown` cases), the smoke and end-to-end fakes answer health per uid, and the recorder suite's host guard is the fake's own host:port. `grafana_dashboards_search.json` keeps its richer 2026-06-12 recording (the stack answers the same `{ count, results }` shape).
- **Live validation tier (2026-09-07, the real products in Docker).** The public MCP tier could only evidence what that stack happens to scrape; `docker/stack.compose.yaml` (`name: observogram-stack`, loopback-only ports 19090 / 19093 / 18428 / 18880 / 18888+14318 / 13030 / 19115 / 19080 / 14269, disjoint from the validate stack) now runs every product the table names at an exactly pinned version — Prometheus v2.55.1 scraping all of them plus a blackbox probe job, Alertmanager v0.27.0 with a dead webhook receiver, VictoriaMetrics v1.113.0 scraping itself and a dead target, vmalert v1.113.0, otel-collector-contrib 0.115.1 with a `debug` exporter beside an OTLP exporter to a dead endpoint (queue + bounded retries), Grafana 12.4.4 with a provisioned Prometheus datasource and one provisioned always-firing alert rule, blackbox-exporter v0.25.0, promtail 3.3.2 tailing a sample file into a dead Loki, and Jaeger all-in-one 1.62.0 — every ruler carrying one recording rule and one always-firing alert so the failure and notification counters move on a fresh stack (`up -d --wait` returns in ~10s). `tools/test-stack-live.mjs` (`npm run test:stack:live[:strict]`, NOT in `npm test` — it needs Docker and skips loudly without it) brings the stack up, recreates the collector and Grafana so neither carries a previous run's stimulus, waits for every scrape job to be up and for the rate windows to hold two samples, then for **every alias of every row**: (a) asserts each `requires` name is a metric family on the product's own exposition (`/metrics`; the Prometheus TSDB name inventory for the scrape-synthesised `up` / `scrape_duration_seconds`, which never appear on Prometheus' own `/metrics`; the blackbox `/probe` output for `probe_success`; the vmalert service for `vmalert_*`), recording separately the presence of any name the `expr` reads that `requires` does not demand (the lazily-registered-counter policy's shape); (b) evaluates the `expr` on the real Prometheus and reads the answer through the fetcher's own `sampleFromInstantVector` (`data` / `empty`; a PromQL error is a FAIL naming the error); (c) records the collector's name set at startup, sends one OTLP/HTTP request per signal kind (and one query through Grafana's datasource proxy), and records the name set again. The ledger (alias | product@version from the image tag | exposition | query) is printed and written to the git-ignored `.tmp-stack-live-ledger.json`; any exposition ✗ or query ERROR exits non-zero. **First ledger, table untouched by design** (the next slice corrects it): 28 of 37 aliases expose every required name and every one of the 37 expressions evaluates without a PromQL error (25 `data`, 12 `empty`); the 9 ✗ are all otelcol — on 0.115.1 the internal telemetry carries **no `_total` suffix** (the bare-name aliases pass), `otelcol_processor_dropped_*` never exist (removed by the processorhelper rework), and `otelcol_exporter_send_failed_*` (like `otelcol_exporter_sent_*` and every receiver/processor counter) are **absent at startup and present only after the first export attempt** — 10 names at startup, 39 the moment `send_failed_*` register, 48 by the time that first ledger's collector had been retrying its dead exporter for some minutes (the counts are timing snapshots of one growing set; the 10 is stable) — while `otelcol_exporter_queue_size` / `queue_capacity` / `otelcol_process_uptime` are the always-present siblings. Also observed: `otelcol_exporter_queue_size` carries a `data_type` label that `queue_capacity` lacks, so `collector_queue_saturation`'s division matches nothing (`empty` on a healthy collector); Grafana's `grafana_datasource_request_total` and `grafana_alerting_rule_evaluation_*` register lazily (after the first proxied request / the first rule evaluation of an org); the two Grafana 5xx rows read `empty` rather than 0 on a healthy Grafana because their label filter matches nothing.
- **Live validation tier — the alias table corrected from live exposition (2026-09-07).** With the stack ledger and the public tier as evidence, `tools/lib/contracts/stack-self-metrics.mjs` now reads only names a real product exposed: every one of its 32 aliases carries a `verified` stamp (`<image:tag> <exposition|TSDB inventory|probe output> + PromQL, 2026-09-07`) that `npm run test:stack:live` checks against the compose image, and the live suite is a green gate — 32/32 aliases expose every required name (lazily-registered counters asserted present after the stimulus), 32/32 expressions read `data` on the real Prometheus (the first run: 28/37 exposition, 12 `empty`). What changed and why: (1) the six otelcol `_total` aliases are gone — neither 0.115.1 (stack) nor 0.154.0 (public tier) suffixes its internal telemetry; (2) the three `collector_dropped_*` rows are retired for `collector_refused_{metrics,spans,logs}` over `otelcol_receiver_refused_<kind>` — `otelcol_processor_dropped_*` exist on no current collector in either spelling (removed by the processorhelper rework), while the receiver counters are the current-generation "collector is losing telemetry" signal (24 rows, 9 families unchanged); (3) the **lazily-registered counter policy**: the collector registers `send_failed_<kind>` with `sent_<kind>` at the first export (0.115.1, observed before/after a forced failure: 10 names at startup, ≥39 after one OTLP request per kind — 39 the moment `send_failed_*` register, more as the dead exporter retries; the never-failing `debug` exporter then carries `send_failed_* 0`) or, on 0.154.0, plausibly only on the first failure (`send_failed_spans` absent while `sent_spans` is present on the read-only public tier — consistent with registration on the first failure, not forced there), so those six aliases `require` the sibling that registers with or before the counter (`otelcol_exporter_sent_<kind>`, `otelcol_receiver_accepted_<kind>`) and end with `or (count(<sibling>) * 0)` — a scraped collector that has exported reads 0 unless the counter exists, one that never exported that signal reads `not-in-inventory`, and a renamed counter renames the sibling so the alias never reads a false 0 (a bare `or vector(0)` was not used: the sampler's bounded cascade queries aliases without a trusted inventory, where `vector(0)` would fabricate a healthy collector on a backend that has none — the reason the count rows already reject it); (4) `collector_queue_saturation` is `max(max by (exporter, instance, job) (queue_size) / max by (exporter, instance, job) (queue_capacity))` — on 0.115.1 `queue_size` carries `data_type` and `queue_capacity` does not (the plain division matched nothing → `empty`), on 0.154.0 both do (`ignoring(data_type) group_left` would be many-to-many on a multi-pipeline collector); the per-exporter max is the fullest queue's fill on either; (5) `datasource_errors` reads the reference pack's `grafana_datasource_request_total{code=~"5.."}` first — registered on Grafana's first datasource request, which is any rule evaluation, `/api/ds/query` or proxied query, so every Grafana with a dashboard or a rule has it — and keeps the pre-registered `grafana_proxy_response_status_total` (`code="500"` at 0 from startup, but counting only the legacy `/api/datasources/proxy/...` path) as the fallback, documented as discrepancy 3 (this slice first shipped the proxy counter first; the review below swapped the order); (6) the two Grafana 5xx rate rows carry the presence-guarded zero (`or (count(<base>) * 0)`) so a healthy Grafana reads `0` instead of `empty`; (7) confirmed unchanged against the expositions: vmalert's plural `errors_total`, `vm_cache_entries{type="storage/hour_metric_ids"}`, the summary labels `slice` / `quantile`, `alertmanager_*`, `promtail_dropped_entries_total`, `probe_success` on the `/probe` output, and `jaeger_collector_spans_dropped_total` on Jaeger 1.62.0's admin port with no flag — every one pre-registered at 0, so strict `requires` stay (the public tier's Jaeger is v2.18.0, an otelcol distribution: it answers through the collector rows and the jaeger alias reads an honest `not-in-inventory` there). Public tier re-run after the correction (read-only): 14 aliases `data` · 0 `empty` · 0 `failed` · 18 `not-in-inventory` of 32 (was 10 · 1 · 0 · 26 of 37), 13 of 24 rows with data; the trimmed inventory fixture grew to 43 names (`grafana_proxy_response_status_total`, `otelcol_exporter_sent_spans`, `otelcol_receiver_accepted_spans`) and `recorded-stack/` holds six instant vectors again, `collector_export_failures_spans` and `datasource_errors` replacing the queue and HTTP-errors files as their families' data rows. Suites: `test-stack-self-metrics` pins the `verified` shape on every alias, the sibling guard on exactly six lazy aliases, the absence of `vector(0)` / otelcol `_total` / `processor_dropped_*`, and the collector row ids; `test-stack-live` asserts lazy counters after the stimulus and the stamp-vs-image match, and strips grouping clauses before reading metric names out of an expr. `docs/MCP_INTEGRATION.md` gains a "Live validation tier" section with the verification ledger per product@version.
- **Live validation tier — review corrections (2026-09-07).** Reproduced on the running stack (Grafana 12.4.4): an `/api/ds/query` — the path dashboards and Grafana-managed rules use — increments `grafana_datasource_request_total` and leaves every `grafana_proxy_response_status_total` series untouched (the proxy counter counts only the legacy `/api/datasources/proxy/...` path; a proxied call increments both), and the sampler stops at the first alias that answers `data`. With the proxy counter first, `datasource_errors` read a pre-registered 0 forever on any Grafana whose datasource queries were failing 5xx through `/api/ds/query` — a systematically blind 0 on the surfaces. The row now reads the reference `grafana_datasource_request_total` first (strict `requires`) and the proxy counter as the fallback for a Grafana whose inventory lacks the reference name (the public tier: its first alias reads `not-in-inventory` and the row falls through to the proxy alias, so `recorded-stack/datasource_errors.json` keeps the proxy expression as its provenance query); `test-stack-self-metrics` pins the new order and the reason. Also from the review: the live suite snapshots Grafana's `/metrics` the moment `--force-recreate --wait grafana` returns (the provisioned 10s rule is Grafana's own first datasource request, so its before column is "before the suite's stimulus", not "at startup" — the collector's is exact), every suite request carries a 10s `AbortSignal.timeout`, the compose header says one run at a time (two concurrent runs recreate the collector and Grafana under each other) and the Grafana provisioning mount is `:ro` like every other mount; the collector name counts are stated as the timing snapshots they are (10 at startup is stable; 39 the moment `send_failed_*` register, 48 minutes later), the 0.154.0 observation is worded as the read-only inference it is, the pre-registered-at-0 citation names the right libraries (client_golang for `prometheus_*` / `alertmanager_*` / `promtail_*` / `jaeger_collector_*`; VictoriaMetrics' own `metrics` library for `vm_*`, and vmalert's `*_rules_errors_total` per loaded rule — per-rule label sets, none on a rule-less vmalert), `SRC.prometheus` no longer reads as if `prometheus_*` were confirmed on the public tier (no Prometheus server there; only `up` / `scrape_duration_seconds` were), and `otel/opentelemetry-collector-contrib:0.115.1` self-reporting `service_version=0.115.0` is noted beside the stamp. The shipped lazy-counter policy deliberately deviates from the brief's wording (an always-present sibling + `or vector(0)`): it requires the per-kind sibling that registers with the counter and ends with `or (count(<sibling>) * 0)`, because the sampler's bounded cascade queries aliases without a trusted inventory, where `vector(0)` would fabricate a healthy collector on a backend that has none — the canonical statement is the "Lazily-registered counters" paragraph of `docs/MCP_INTEGRATION.md`, the contracts header points at it.
- **Review follow-ups (honesty re-audit of the slice).** Six places where the panel could read as assurance for something never sampled, plus the names the review checked against upstream source:
  - *Alias table.* vmalert's ruler counters are `vmalert_recording_rules_errors_total` / `vmalert_alerting_rules_errors_total` (plural — the singular names could never answer on the recorded VictoriaMetrics + vmalert reference stack). `query_latency_p99` now reads `max(prometheus_engine_query_duration_seconds{slice="inner_eval",quantile="0.99"})`: Prometheus registers that metric as a summary, so the `_bucket` the reference pack (and the old row) quantiled over never exists — documented as known discrepancy 2 beside the scrape-duration one. The count rows carry a presence-guarded zero, `count(up == 0) or (count(up) * 0)` (and `sum(up) / count(up)` for the ratio), so a healthy stack reads `0` instead of `empty` while a backend without the metric still reads `empty` — `or vector(0)` was rejected because it would fabricate "0 down" where nothing is scraped. `SRC.otelcol` now says the `otelcol_processor_dropped_*` counters exist only on collectors before the processorhelper metric rework.
  - *Sampler.* A series-only instant vector (metric identities, no sample) is `empty` for every unit — a `count` row used to turn it into "1 target down". The metric-name inventory is evidence of presence, never of absence: only an inventory that carries `up` gates a row to `not-in-inventory` (with the inventory size in the reason); an inventory without `up` — the recorded 25-name fixture, an empty list, a capped server — gates nothing and the rows are queried on the bounded cascade (`queried anyway` in the reason of an empty answer). With a trusted inventory every eligible alias may be tried (the 2-per-row cap applies to the fallback cascade only). `hasToolsList` is whether the `tools/list` RPC succeeded, so an empty advertised list reads `not-attempted` (tier), not 24 × `failed`. New export `stackInventoryTrust(inventory)`; the sampler result carries `inventory: { size, trusted, reason }`.
  - *Shapes + observers.* Object shapes locate **envelope-first** (`objectAt: ['data', '']`, new `locateObjectPayload(shapeId, response)` the fetcher reuses): a Prometheus-API-style `{ status: 'success', data: {...} }` wrapper used to satisfy `status-object` / `health-object` at its root, reading version `null` / cluster `success` and a wrapped `{ status: 'ERROR' }` health verdict as `ok`. `observeAlertmanager` / `observeGrafana` return `null` only when none of their tools is advertised; an advertised tool that fails (HTTP error, timeout, bad shape) yields a non-null result with `error`, annotated as `mcp.observed.alertmanager.error` / `mcp.observed.grafana.error`, carried on `summary.alertmanager.error` / `summary.grafana.error` with a `… status probe failed — <error>` warning, and rendered `— probe failed: <error>` — `— not exposed` is reserved for tools absent from `tools/list`.
  - *Studio.* The datasources row never prints `0 unhealthy` for a surface nothing checked: `N · health not checked (grafana_datasource_health not exposed or did not answer)` when every health is `unknown`, otherwise `N · M error: <names> · K unchecked: <names>` (`summary.grafana.healthChecked` counts the verdicts). The not-attempted row renders `summary.stack.reason`; a family with no observed row reads `— not attempted: call budget exhausted` instead of `not attempted: not attempted`.
  - *Recorder.* Committed fixtures' `_recorded` provenance carries tool + timestamp (+ query) only — the maintainer's MCP hostname stays in the git-ignored review copy; every query-string value of `MCP_URL` is redacted alongside `MCP_AUTH` (a URL-borne token echoed by an upstream error body comes out `<redacted>`); the report prints the fetcher's inventory-trust verdict and the inventory size beside the not-in-inventory count.
  - *Docs + suites.* `MCP_INTEGRATION.md` sampling policy rewritten for the trust rule (the old sentence claimed a trimmed fixture made every alias eligible while the code gated everything); the annotation reference lists `mcp.observed.alertmanager` / `grafana.*` / `grafana.error`; new cases in `test-fetch-live.mjs` (series-only count rows, trusted vs untrusted vs empty inventory, no per-row cap with an inventory, advertised-but-failing and envelope observers, error annotations), `test-contract-shapes.mjs` (envelope location), `test-stack-self-metrics.mjs` (pinned upstream names, guarded zeros, discrepancy 2), `test-record-fixtures.mjs` (URL-only token, no host in provenance, inventory-trust line).
- **Suites**: `tools/test-stack-self-metrics.mjs` (`npm run test:stack`) — unique ids, allowed sets, every alias's `requires` ⊆ the metric names its `expr` reads, every `referenceSli` resolves in its reference pack, resolver purity; `tools/test-fetch-live.mjs` — sampler cases (inventory eligibility, product preference, data/empty/failed, NaN → empty, both envelopes, bounded cascade, global cap, restricted tier, no tools/list, `quiet()` family), observer cases, the annotation contract (schema-valid, no Verified/Scaffold change, adapted layers unchanged) and an end-to-end run against the fake JSON-RPC server; `server/test-smoke.mjs` (draft summary with and without `metrics_query`, live-status fields); `tools/test-journey.mjs` (stack facts, record, markdown); new `tools/test-record-fixtures.mjs` — the recorder against a fake MCP replaying the fixtures: report content and drift, no writes in report mode, every `--write` file's trimming and shape, the token absent from every output and file, the restricted tier, missing `MCP_URL`; `tools/test-contract-shapes.mjs` — recorded precedence and the `recorded-stack/` scan; `tools/test-contract-guard.mjs` now scans the recorder too. Docs: `MCP_INTEGRATION.md` "Stack self-metrics (registry / sampling / surfaces)" with every `mcp.stack.*` / `mcp.observed.*` key listed once in the annotation reference; README "Fetch Live From MCP".

### Live evidence honesty: the fetcher stops asserting what it never observed
The top product risk is false assurance — a live pack reporting `Verified` / aligned / fresh for an artefact that is not doing its job. This entry removes the known false-assurance paths in `tools/fetch-live-pack.mjs`, makes the on-wire liveness signals visible, lets journeys gate on the vantage, and stops two authoring-only differences from reading as drift. **Existing users should expect alignment percentages and grades to drop against the same MCP** — placeholders that used to count as `Verified` now park as `Scaffold`, burn-rate alerts the platform never had no longer align with the ones the repo declares, and scrape jobs whose targets are all down no longer attest scraping. The lower number is the honest one.

**False-assurance fixes**
- **Burn-rate alerts are mapped from discovered rules, never synthesised.** The fetcher used to emit a two-window burn-rate alert for every inferred SLO and stamp `mcp.verified.policy.burn_rate_alerts` whenever *any* alert name came back. `spec.policy.burn_rate_alerts` is now built from the alerting rules the MCP exposes — compiler-labelled rules (`slo`/`burn_rate`/`window_short`/`window_long`/`severity`) and `<slo>_burn_<N>x_<short>_<long>`-named rules, grouped per SLO, deduped, short window first — and each entry gets an **indexed** `mcp.verified.policy.burn_rate_alerts[<i>]` stamp (the unindexed key is gone). A group whose SLO shares the SLI base of an inferred placeholder re-identifies that SLO to the discovered id and replaces its placeholder objective/window from the `slo_objective`/`slo_window` annotations; groups naming an SLO nobody inferred, or carrying a single window, land in `mcp.discovered.alert_rules_unmapped`; forecast rules are skipped; severities outside the `SEVn` enum are mapped from the Prometheus vocabulary or the burn factor and listed in `mcp.discovered.alert_rules_severity_inferred`. When nothing maps, the schema-forced placeholder is stamped `mcp.scaffold.policy.burn_rate_alerts[0]`.
- **Every placeholder the fetcher invents is `Scaffold`, never `Verified`.** `mcp.scaffold.<symbol>` (the live counterpart of `crawler.scaffold.<symbol>`; `tools/lib/adapter.mjs` `sourceOf` now honours both prefixes) marks the hard-coded `spec.otel` block (`system_health` answering used to verify it), collector receivers/processors, the logs/traces exporters, the fallback `logs-elastic` backend, the `platform-overview` dashboard stub, the SEV1 → Teams route, the baselines, and the per-service / `platform_availability` SLI+SLO guesses. `pipelines.exporters.metrics` is `Verified` only when scrape targets or a metric inventory evidenced it; `metrics-prom` only on a `*_build_info` capture; `traces-jaeger` only when the topology names jaeger or `traces_services` answered. SLIs inferred from real recorded rules stay `Verified`; an SLO re-identified by a discovered burn-rate group becomes `Verified`.
- **MTTD is no longer derived from anomaly thresholds.** `spec.baselines.mttd_target_p50` was the smallest `anomalies_baselines` `thresholdMs` (e.g. `90ms`) stamped `Verified` — a latency-anomaly threshold is not a time-to-detect target. Baselines are now the platform default for the declared criticality (`measurement_source: platform-default`, stamped `Scaffold`) until a §5.10 measurement exists; `mcp.baselinesComputed` still counts the anomaly baselines returned, which is evidence the tool answered, not a measurement.

**Liveness evidence (on-wire health)**
- A scrape job NAME used to be enough to stamp `mcp.verified.telemetry.scrape` and `mcp.verified.pipelines.exporters.metrics`, and a rule's presence was enough to verify it, even when the same payload said every target was `down` or the rule was failing to evaluate. The `scrape_configs` probe now adapts to per-job target lists (`[{job, targets:[{instance, health, lastScrape, lastError}]}]`, tolerant of otel-mcp-server's `metrics_targets` and Prometheus `/api/v1/targets`): only jobs with at least one target `up` (or of unknown health) land in `mcp.discovered.scrape_jobs` and attest scraping; jobs whose every target is down go to `mcp.discovered.scrape_jobs_down`; every target's health is kept in `mcp.observed.scrape_targets` (JSON, 200 entries, `lastError` trimmed to 200 chars). When no job is up the metrics exporter falls back to its scaffold marker.
- The `recording_rules` / `alert_rules` adapters carry `health`, `lastError`, `lastEvaluation`, `evaluationTime` (and `state`, `activeAt` for alerting rules); the fetcher strips them before `spec.queries` and writes `mcp.observed.recording_rules` / `mcp.observed.alert_rules` plus `mcp.discovered.recording_rules_unhealthy` / `alert_rules_unhealthy`. Recording rules are stamped **per index** (`mcp.verified.queries.recording_rules[<i>]` — the symbol the adapter actually reads; they used to project `Declared` despite the group stamp) and only when healthy or health-less; a burn-rate group fed by an unhealthy alerting rule still maps but earns no indexed stamp. Absent health is never treated as unhealthy; legacy job-name `string[]` probe results still count as health-less scrape evidence.
- Draft summary (`POST /api/draft-from-mcp`) gains `scrapeJobsDown`, `recordingRulesUnhealthy`, `alertRulesUnhealthy`; the studio lists `N scrape jobs down: …` and `N rules unhealthy: …` when non-empty.

**Probe honesty (what the MCP could and could not answer)**
- A probe family whose every candidate is absent from `tools/list` was recorded as `unsupported` by the probe loop but never emitted; the studio read it as "probed, none found" and warned that the family "returned empty". The pack now stamps `mcp.probesUnsupported` (a restricted MCP tier) beside `mcp.probesFailed` (a hole of unknown size), and `mcp.probeErrors.<family>` carries the last erroring candidate's message (trimmed to 200 chars). `fetchMcp` returns its `probeFailures` map; `buildCanonicalPack` accepts it.
- `partialLiveEvidence` (vendored, zero-import) gains additive keys only: `unsupported`, `errors`, and `vantage` (`full` / `partial` / `restricted` / `lost` / `none`); `partial` keeps meaning outright failures. `GET /api/live-status` returns `probesFailed` and `probesUnsupported`; the MCP badge goes to its error state for failed probe families and names a restricted tier in its title; the draft summary lists unsupported families as "— not exposed by this MCP" (with the probe error inline for failed ones) and warns once about the restricted tier; the partial-evidence banners append the not-exposed families.

**Journeys: studio grade parity, vantage-aware gate, vantage-lost records**
- **One score for one comparison.** `tools/lib/journey.mjs` attaches the requirement-chain comparison (`comparePackBranches`) to the diff as `traceabilityGraph` before grading — exactly what the studio's `/api/diff` does — so the CLI and the studio score Drift-free on the same construct. The run record says which construct scored it (`grade.driftConstruct`: `requirement-chain` / `diff-buckets`) and carries the chain rollup (`traceability`).
- **The vantage is part of the verdict.** Run records gain `probes {attempted, succeeded, empty, failed, unsupported}`, `probeErrors`, `vantage`, `toolsExposedCount`, `scrapeJobsDown` and `unhealthyRules`, read from Pack B's fetcher annotations (a file-sourced B reports empty lists, `0`, `null`, `none`). Two new gate keys: `failOnPartialEvidence: true` breaches when any probe family failed or the vantage is entirely lost; `maxUnhealthy: N` breaches when scrape jobs down + unhealthy rules exceed N. Empty and unsupported families never breach on their own. The markdown report prints `Live probes` and `On-wire health` lines; the README gate example shows both keys.
- **Vantage loss is a point in history, not a hole.** When Pack B is an `mcp:` source and the fetch itself fails, the journey writes an `outcome: vantage-lost` record (error, both sources, timing, no fabricated grade) before rethrowing — exit code stays `2`, the studio route still answers 502. Configuration errors (missing file, unset `authEnv`) leave no record. `packc journey list` and the studio Journeys view render the outcome without a fake `alignment undefined%`.

**Canon: two authoring-only differences stop reading as drift**
- `tools/lib/promql-canon.mjs` removes the spaces around the symbolic binary operators `+ * / % ^ == != <= >= < > =~ !~` (outside strings, parser-proven expressions only), so `a / b` ≡ `a/b` and `rate(x[5m]) > 0.5` ≡ `rate(x[5m])>0.5`. `-` is left alone (unary/binary ambiguity), keyword operators keep the space that bounds them, label-value strings stay byte-identical, and a space *inside* an operator (`a < = b`) is never glued into a different one. Decision-bearing differences (`>` vs `>=`, thresholds, `=~` vs `!~`) still read as drift.
- `tools/lib/artefact-model.mjs` `behaviorFor` strips a leading `ref:` from `slo`, `sli`, `trigger`, `error_budget_policy`, and from `expr` when the whole value is a reference (`ref:slis.x`), via a pure `stripRefFields` copy of the spec — identity already stripped it, so a burn alert declared `slo: ref:x` paired with the live `slo: x` and then reported itself drifted on its own binding. Both modules are vendored downstream (`docs/VENDORING.md`): additive behaviour only, no new imports, signatures unchanged — re-copy the pair together.

**Review follow-ups (false-assurance re-audit)**
- **Placeholders never pair.** The studio construct (`diffPacks` + `comparePackBranches`, which the journey now grades on) still paired a live `Scaffold` placeholder with a declared artefact: a declared burn-rate alert on the live pack's first SLO read `aligned` when it carried the compiler's default windows (exactly the placeholder's) or `drifted` otherwise — never `declared, not live` — and BAK-01 / PIP-PRC-01/02 / the SEV1 route aligned or drifted against scaffold backends, processors and the fallback route. Only the diff-bucket grade path parked them. `diffPacks` now removes `source: Scaffold` artefacts from both sides before pairing into a new `scaffold` bucket (`summary.scaffold`; a repo placeholder is not a declaration either, so a real live route the repo only had a placeholder for reads `live, not declared`); `comparePackBranches` refuses a `Scaffold` live node as evidence (`declared_only`), never emits it as `live_only`, drops a live-only branch rooted on a scaffold SLO, and does not count a scaffold route/dashboard as proof the live connector can see that kind. Journey facts (`alignmentPct`, `aligned`, `declaredNotLive` → `maxDeclaredNotLive`) follow; the run record carries `drift.scaffold`. The studio drift drill, verdict and prototype views read the new bucket beside the older `isScaffoldDiffEntry` filter.
- **Tiered SLOs on one SLI no longer produce a dangling ref.** `mapDiscoveredBurnAlerts` resolved groups in one pass and only tracked re-id candidates as claimed, so an `x_99_9` group could re-identify the SLO an `x_99` group had already bound exactly — `spec.slos` then held `x_99_9` while `burn_rate_alerts[0].slo` still said `x_99` (POL-01 Verified with a ref pointing at nothing; the schema's pattern-only `Ref` never caught it). Exact matches now bind and claim first; base matches re-identify only unclaimed placeholders; the leftover group is reported in `mcp.discovered.alert_rules_unmapped`. Exact matches apply the `slo_objective`/`slo_window` evidence and drop the SLO's scaffold marker like a re-id does; a re-id to an id that is not a schema Slug (length included) is refused.
- **Health follows the evidence down the chain.** An SLI inferred from recorded rules of which one is reported unhealthy is no longer stamped `mcp.verified.slis.<id>` (new `mcp.discovered.slis_unhealthy`); an SLO bound by a burn group fed by an unhealthy rule is not stamped either; rule health is keyed by name with any-unhealthy-wins semantics (a duplicated name reported `err` in one group and `ok` in another stamped both spec entries Verified while being named unhealthy); the requirement traceability chain lists an unhealthy live alerting rule with `verified: false` / `health: err`, does not let it (or a scaffold burn-rate entry) close `missing_alert_evidence`, and counts `withAlerts` on evidence only — the studio chain rows and the requirement-trace drawer show `(unhealthy)`.
- **Under-reporting fixed too.** Discovered dashboards are stamped per id (`mcp.verified.dashboards.<id>`, the symbol the adapter reads) beside the aggregate key, so they project `Verified` instead of `Declared`. `mcp.probeErrors.<family>` is written only for families whose outcome is `failed` — a family whose later candidate answered no longer carries the earlier candidate's error while sitting in `probesSucceeded`.
- **Fresh names the vantage.** The grade's Fresh criterion keeps its staleness pass/fail (scoring is out of scope) but its detail now says `vantage lost: no probe family answered (failed: …; not exposed: …)` or `vantage partial: failed …`, so a refresh that saw nothing does not read as a fresh look at production. `.journey-outcome.is-lost` uses a new `--warn` text token instead of a hard-coded colour.

**Fixtures re-pinned** (`npm run test:contracts:update`): `tools/fixtures/mcp/adapted/scrape_configs.json` is now per-job target health instead of a job-name `string[]` (the `alertmanager` job is present with its `down` target and `lastError`); `recording_rules.json` / `alert_rules.json` carry the observation fields (`health`, `lastError`, `lastEvaluation`, `evaluationTime`, and `state` / `activeAt`). No crawler or compiler golden changed.

**Suites**: `tools/test-fetch-live.mjs` (placeholder + mapping paths, every scaffold key and its adapter projection, the excluded down job / all-down / legacy / adapter-shape cases, per-index rule stamps, the unsupported/failed split and per-family error mapping), `tools/test-diagnostic-grade.mjs` (every vantage), `server/test-smoke.mjs` (live-status fields on a planted live pack), `tools/test-journey.mjs` (both gate keys, record fields, studio parity, the vantage-lost record via a closed loopback port, the no-record rule), `tools/test-promql-canon.mjs` and `tools/test-diff.mjs` (operators, fences, `ref:` ≡ bare). Docs: `MCP_INTEGRATION.md` (annotation reference, what the fetcher invents, on-wire liveness, burn mapping, journeys), `DIFF.md`, `ADAPTER.md`, `VENDORING.md`, README.

### Studio Diagnose views rejoin the behavioural engine
- **Diagnose → Compare cards classify from the diff again.** The side-by-side view derived its in-both / only-in chips by recomputing keys client-side as `defines || id` — a lossy approximation of the old diff keyspace that stopped matching entirely once the engine moved to behavioural identity keys (`kind::{identity}`): the summary band said N matched while all cards below rendered unclassified and the "In both" / "A − B" slice filters returned nonsense. Cards and slice filters now classify by the artefact ids embedded in the diff entries themselves (unique per pack side) — no client-side key math to drift again.
- **Traceability (Advanced) is diff-driven.** Its repo-vs-live buckets were re-derived locally by raw id match + whole-object JSON equality (only `annotations`/`_sub` stripped), so behaviourally-identical artefacts with different ids double-counted as declared-not-verified *and* verified-not-declared, and endpoint/expression-formatting differences flagged Stale. The four buckets now re-bin the server diff — aligned = inBoth-aligned, stale = inBoth-drifted (with the engine's delta fields named on each row) — and reconcile with the drift drill's **unlensed** totals: out-of-scope artefacts and scaffold placeholders are parked the same way (counts + live-scope control shown), while the drill additionally applies the active product lens. Panels stay compared at dashboard granularity (the engine's rule), now stated in the view. The view fetches the diff itself when it isn't loaded yet. Suppress/resolve prefs now key on behavioural identity keys (collision entries append the artefact symbol so `#NN` ordinal reshuffles don't migrate a suppression), so previously-saved entries no longer match and reset once.
- **Stale-diff hardening.** `state.diff` is stamped with the selection it was computed for (`packs + envs + scope + service`) and every consumer treats a mismatch as *no diff*, refetching instead of rendering — previously a Pack A/env switch could leave the views classifying the new pack against the old diff's positional ids. Late responses for a superseded selection are discarded, concurrent renders share one in-flight fetch, and the three loading branches only re-render on progress (an unregistered Pack A now gets an honest error instead of a render loop).
- `prettyDiffKey` learned the behavioural keyspace (`kind::{identity-json}` with `#NN` occurrence ordinals) — backend, pipeline-stage, storage, mesh/collection, product-keyed, scrape-job, imports, and remediation identities render as readable labels instead of raw JSON, and forecast alerts are no longer mislabelled as burn-rate alerts.

### Account menu: profile, change password, sign out
- The identity chip in the studio header is now an **account menu**: who you're signed in as (and the auth mode), **change password…** (stand-alone mode — OIDC passwords belong to the IdP), and **sign out**. It renders in identity postures only; the open posture (`OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off` or no users file) has no accounts to manage.
- **Signed-in users can finally change their password**: `/auth/change-password` now also accepts a session. The current password is required — that knowledge is the CSRF defence — and wrong guesses share the login damper (5 failures → 30s lockout). The mid-login forced-change flow (flow cookie, skip) is behaviourally preserved, with one deliberate alignment: a user deleted mid-flow now gets "unknown user" before password validation, matching the session path.
- **Login now clears a leftover pwchange flow cookie.** An abandoned forced change (admin/admin typed on a shared browser, then closed) used to leave a 10-minute flow cookie that shadowed the session on `/auth/change-password` — the account menu's link could silently target the *other* account. A normal login expires it.
- Known limitation (stateless sessions, pre-existing model): rotating a password does **not** revoke other outstanding sessions — an already-stolen cookie rides out its TTL (default 8h). Real revocation needs a per-user epoch in `users.json` checked at session verification.
- Suite: 15 new assertions in `server/test-auth-local.mjs` — the session variant of the page (current-password field, no skip control; the flow variant is pinned to *lack* the field), wrong-current 401 in JSON and HTML, short-replacement 400, rotation in JSON and HTML (302 home), old password dead / new signs in, deleted-user GET/POST with a live session, the stale-flow-cookie clear on login, the 429 lockout, and the damper being shared with `/auth/login`.

### "Skip for now" on the forced password change
- The first-boot change-password page now offers **Skip for now — ask again next sign-in**: a session is issued without replacing `admin/admin`, the forced-change flags stay set, and every subsequent sign-in re-prompts. New `POST /auth/change-password/skip`, credentialed by the same signed flow cookie as the change itself.
- **Deliberately narrow**: skipping works only while the record still holds the *seeded default* — an admin-set temporary password (`mustChange` without `seededDefault`) stays a forced change, and the loopback guard is unaffected (a skipped default still refuses to bind beyond loopback).
- Suite: 12 new assertions in `server/test-auth-local.mjs` — skip control rendering, no-cookie rejection, session issuance with flags intact (JSON and browser-form paths), network-bind refusal after a skip, re-prompt at next sign-in, and the temporary-password 403 (JSON and HTML, skip control absent).

### Explicit collision reporting + collision-proof coverage
- **New top-level `collisions` on the `diffPacks()` result** (`{ layer, kind, key, aCount, bCount }`) and `graph.collisions` on `buildDependencyGraph()` (`{ key, kind, count }`): every identity key held by more than one artefact, surfaced explicitly so callers can fail loud instead of inferring collisions from `#NN` occurrence suffixes. Additive — no existing bucket, key, or summary count changes.
- **Alert-route identity deliberately stays severity-keyed.** Widening it with channel kinds was prototyped and declined: the live connector and crawler fabricate a channel kind when routing cannot be introspected (fetch-live's SEV1 msteams placeholder, unmapped-receiver stubs), so channel-kind identity would split declared routes from their live placeholders and report them falsely missing in production. Channel changes stay decision-bearing *drift* on the paired route; a new regression test pins the placeholder pairing.
